# Changelog

All notable changes to Sawhorse are recorded here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/); versions follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/); pre-releases are `0.1.0-alpha.N`, on the [releases page](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/releases).

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed (lane 98, 2026-11-27)

- **After an edit, Undo or Redo the selection holds only what the model still has** ([#152](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/152), from @aramshaw; [#110](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/110)). Selected faces, regions, solids, groups and section planes the model no longer had were already dropped, but edges, points, curves, guides and dimensions were not. Two edges of a rectangle stayed selected after Undo took the rectangle away, drawn in the selection's blue, and Follow Me's W read them as a path: a click on a box's face then swept it along edges the model did not have and added to the box. Now an edge stays only while its own solid or sketch has a segment with both its ends, a point while one of its segments ends there, a curve while it is drawn, and a guide or a dimension while the model has it (`heldByModel` in `src/lib/entities.ts`). Ends are matched within the view's single precision. Under an active section plane a click picks an edge as the view draws it, cut short at the plane or on the cut's outline, so for a solid or sketch under the plane an edge stays while both its ends lie along one of its segments, each at one of that segment's ends or on a plane that cuts that segment (a plane placed on a face, lying along its edges, cuts none of them), or, for a solid, along the cut's outline inside it; a point the same way. An edge that is gone leaves the selection there too, a piece of an edge that a line split, undone, included. A solid's edge or corner moved, turned or scaled with Move, Rotate or Scale now stays selected where it went, and Undo and Redo select it where it was and where it went, as sketch shapes already did; before, it stayed selected where it had been. An edge a later step splits (a line drawn from its middle) is dropped, as no segment has both its ends.
- Tests: `src/lib/entities.test.ts` (each kind kept while the model has it and dropped when it does not; an edge either way round, through single precision and 5 km up one axis; an edge of another owner, or with one end moved, dropped; two owners of one mesh asked in turn; under a section plane an edge cut short, an edge and a point on the cut's outline and a point along a segment kept, and a piece of an edge with an end neither at its segment's end nor on the plane, an edge off every segment, past a segment's end, along another owner's segment, along another solid's cut line, a sketch's edge along an outline, or of a solid the plane does not clip, dropped; 9,600 seeded cases from 1 cm to 5 km against a plain scan with the same allowance), `src/tools/move.test.ts` (a solid's edges and points where a whole-solid move, a move of a group and a move of an edge's ends put them; a neighbouring edge stretching to the moved end), `rotate.test.ts` and `scale.test.ts` (a solid's edge turned and scaled, still selected where it went, the selection before it remembered for Undo), `e2e/follow.spec.ts` (the issue's steps: nothing selected after Ctrl+Z, W reading no path, a click on the box's front face changing nothing; an edge and a corner of the box kept selected across an unrelated step, its Undo and its Redo, and W reading the kept edge as a path) and `e2e/selection-held.spec.ts` (under a section plane, an edge cut short, an edge of the cut's outline, a point where the plane cuts an edge and an uncut edge, each kept across an edit and its Undo; the issue's steps with an active plane in the model, the two edges leaving the selection after Ctrl+Z; half of an edge a line split, and its middle point, leaving it when the line is undone, with an active plane above the box and with one on its top face; a box's edge moved up 2 in, still selected and read as a path, and selected again after Undo and Redo). On the code before this change the issue's e2e test fails, with the two edges still selected.

### Changed (lane 97, 2026-11-26)

- **A line or a section plane drawn in one copy of a component stays in that copy when the component loses the group or the component it was in** ([#149](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/149)). Each copy of a component is rebuilt from its definition, and what a copy holds that the definition no longer has is removed there. A line drawn in one copy is not part of the definition, but when it was drawn in a group of that copy and the group was then deleted, exploded or made into a component in another copy, the line was removed with the group, with nothing said. One level deeper it was the same for a line or a section plane in a group of a nested component and for a line drawn directly in a nested component, when the nested component left. Undo brought each back. Now each stays in its copy, at the same place and with the same id, in the nearest group or component around it that the component still has, or in the copy itself, at every depth. Only what holds it changes (lines and planes are kept in world coordinates), so in a copy that is turned, mirrored or scaled it stays exactly where it was, and a kept line is not joined to lines already there. Three cases in plain words: a group inside a group the component keeps hands what was drawn in it to that kept group, not to the copy (a section plane there went to the copy before); Make Component of a group in another copy puts a new component in this copy in place of the group, and what was drawn in the group goes to the group's parent, not into the new component; and the tidying that follows is as it was, so a copy left with nothing but a kept line stays, and one left with nothing but a kept section plane is removed with the plane, as a copy holding only a plane of its own is. A kept line can so keep a copy or a group that the tidying used to remove: the Outliner then shows a component or group row holding only the line. Which plane is active follows the rule from before: where the kept plane arrives with no active plane there, the first active one met stays active and any other moved in is switched off; where one is active already, it stays so and the planes moved in are switched off. A model whose steps went through this, run again from its steps, now comes out with the line or the plane kept. Saved files open as saved: a `.shz` holds the model, not its steps. Unsaved work set aside by an earlier version, whose steps went through this, is not restored: Restore says "Could not restore the unsaved work: an older version set it aside, and one of its steps gives a different result in this version", and the work stays set aside and is offered until it is discarded. Its later steps name sketches, planes, groups and components by ids that differ here once the line is kept, so replayed as they are they could build something else without a word (a push landing on another rectangle). Such work is refused even where it would have restored with only the line kept, and where the step was undone later. Unsaved work from an earlier version that never went through this restores as before, as does unsaved work this version sets aside (its record in the browser now carries a mark, `rules: 1`; the `.shz` format and the op log are unchanged). What a refused restore should do in general is [#151](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/151).
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/stale_members.rs`. The tests that held the old result now hold the new one: the two lists from #148 (the line in the copy after the Delete, and after the Make Component in the copy and not in the new component), the single group of #149 (now with the issue's own list), the other order (both lines in the copy), and the planes of both groups (the same with and without the line, the line kept); and in `crates/shz` the copy emptied by tidying (the copy holding the line drawn in its group now stays, with the line). New: a copy turned a quarter round, turned by another angle, and mirrored, each line kept to the bit; a group inside a kept group handing its line and plane to the kept group, whose own active plane stays active; everything drawn in a vanished nested component and its group going to the copy (the nested component's own plane active, its group's switched off); a group inside a kept nested component, and a nested component inside a kept group, each handing what was drawn in it to the kept one; three groups inside each other lost, and a nested component inside a lost group, each handing what was drawn in it to the copy whatever order the loop takes them in; a kept line arriving beside the copy's own lines on its plane, nothing joined or split; an emptied copy staying with a kept line and going with a kept plane, as with a plane of its own; Explode in one copy handing the other copy's line to the group's parent; every list undone and redone to the same hashes, saved and opened as the same model, every line and plane where it was to the bit. The 300 seeded models now check that every stroke and plane whose node vanished, unless the edit deleted that node itself, is in the innermost node around it still there (or in a node the edit made inside that one), at the same place with the same id. A one-off run whose driver is not in the repository compared this kernel with the one before it over 4,000 seeded models (239,614 ops): 2,707 models came out different, every one by a line or plane kept as above (29 of them through the tidying that follows), and none otherwise; each model's steps replayed through this kernel gave the same model 2,248 times, a model different as above 1,644 times and a refusal 108 times, each after such a step; every saved file opened as the model it was saved as. New for unsaved work from the earlier version: in `stale_members.rs` a copy scaled evenly and unevenly keeping the line to the bit, and the guarded replay of logs that version wrote (the log whose push lands on another rectangle when replayed plainly refused at its Delete, also with Undo and Redo after it; a log with no such case and one with only the plane move both versions make accepted with that version's own model and log hashes; this version's own log replaying plainly as it was); in `src/lib/autosave.test.ts` the mark written by saveAutosave and setAside, kept by adoptOffered, and records without it or with fields this version does not know still read; in `src/kernel/client.test.ts` the guarded request asked for only when told; and `e2e/older-unsaved.spec.ts` (the refused restore, the model open before untouched, the offer kept after a reload and Discard removing it alone; the same log with the mark restored with the line kept; an unmarked log with no such case restored as before; this version's own work set aside with the mark and restored). A second one-off run whose driver is not in the repository had the earlier version write 4,000 logs and replayed each through this version guarded: 1,316 were accepted, each giving exactly that version's model, ids and log hash (none differed), and 2,684 were refused, 2,679 of them at the very step where the plain replay first differs and 5 at a step whose difference the tidying in that same step removed again (an over-refusal; in 2 of them a later step differs).

### Fixed (lane 96, 2026-11-26)

- **Delete or Make Component in a component no longer stops the kernel when another copy has a line in a group inside that group** ([#148](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/148)). Each copy of a component is rebuilt from the definition, and what a copy holds that the definition no longer has is removed there, a group with everything in it. The groups to remove are taken in the order of the slots they are stored in, which is not always the order they were made in (a slot freed by a delete is used again). When the outer group came first, it took the inner group and its lines with it, and the inner group's turn then read a line that was gone: the kernel stopped, was started again from the last step, and the edit was not made. Now a line that is already gone is skipped. The result is the one the kernel gives when the inner group comes first, which never stopped it: the section planes of both groups go to the copy (when the copy has no active plane of its own, the first active one met stays active there and the other is switched off; when it has one, that one stays active and both are switched off), and a line drawn in a group that vanishes this way was removed with the group, as before. Such a line now stays with the copy (lane 97 above, [#149](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/149)). Nothing else changes: no model hash, op log or saved file.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/stale_members.rs` (the two lists from #148, a Delete and a Make Component, each undone and redone to the same hashes; the planes of both groups going to the copy, the same with and without the line, and switched off both when the copy has an active plane of its own; two planes of one vanished group going to the copy as they were; a solid and an open mesh deleted in one copy going from the other; the other order and a single vanished group holding the result #149 then had (the line removed with the group; lane 97 changed it); and 300 seeded models of components holding groups inside groups, with strokes and section planes drawn in the copies' groups, then Delete, Make Component, Make Group, Explode, Make Unique, Undo and Redo in contexts opened from the top (Move Into is tried and refused in these models): no panic, a stroke whose group vanished gone, a plane whose node vanished either in a node that held that node, at the same place, or gone, and gone only when its node was deleted itself, sat inside an instance that vanished, lost every node around it (a copy emptied and tidied away), or the edit was an Explode, everything else where it was, Undo and Redo giving the same hashes; five of its tests fail on the code before this change, the seeded one with the panic) and the four recorded sequences that found it, in `crates/kernel/tests/repro/2026-11-26-*.json`, each stopping the kernel on its last op before this change.

### Fixed (lane 95, 2026-11-26)

- **A value typed into Scale, Move, Rotate or Offset never undoes a step the tool did not make** ([#147](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/147), from @aramshaw; [#84](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/84)). Each of these tools changes its last step when a value is typed right after it, by an Undo and the step again. After any other step the Undo took that step instead: Scale, then a Move, then S and `2` undid the Move and scaled the board x2 along red from where it had been; a Move after a Rotate undid the turn, a Rotate after a Move undid the move, and an Offset after a Push/Pull undid the pull and was refused. Since #82 the tool in use forgot its step on Undo, Redo, New, Close, Open and Restore Unsaved Work, and since #85 and #111 on Delete and on edits that hide or remove something, but nothing told it of other steps, and a tool not in use was told nothing. Now each keeps a key of the model read right after its own step (`logKey` in `src/lib/steps.ts`: the undo depth and model hash, `stepKey`, moved there from `App.tsx`, and the hash of the op log), and a typed value changes that step only while the model is still at that key (`stillLast`). The log hash is there because the undo depth stops growing once the history is full (200 steps): a later step that leaves the model as it was (activating a scene, which only moves the camera, a rename to the same name) would then have kept the depth and the model hash, and the value would have undone it. Opening or closing a group adds no undo step but moves the log on, so a value typed after it is taken as after any other step. After any other step, from any tool, pane, import or plugin, the value is taken as a first value would be: in Scale a factor scales the selection about its centre, and Move, Rotate, Offset and a size in Scale say what to do first. After a retype the kernel refuses (its Undo has already taken the tool's step back), the next value no longer undoes the step before it. Undo then Redo still forgets the step, as before.
- Tests: `src/lib/steps.test.ts` (the two keys, each part telling states apart; the last step kept only at its own key; with the history full, a step that leaves the model as it was still ending it; every run of up to four later ops, another edit, Undo and Redo, 121 in all, of which only the empty run keeps it), `src/tools/scale.test.ts`, `move.test.ts`, `rotate.test.ts` and `offset.test.ts` (a value typed after another step sends no Undo, and says what to pick first, or for Scale scales the model as it is; Scale the same with the history full; Move's `x3` and `/4` and Rotate's `x3` after a Ctrl copy and another step, and `/4` typed alone after them; with each op moving the model on, which `fakeHost` now can with `stepping`, a value typed right after the step still changing it in all four tools, `x3`, `/4` and a distance or an angle after a copy in Move and Rotate; Offset's next distance after a refused retype) and `e2e/scale.spec.ts` (the issue's steps: a board scaled x1.5, moved 10 in, then S and `2` scaling it about its centre where it went, the Move kept, one undo step more). On the code before this change, the #84 checks in the four tools' tests fail, and so does the e2e test; the tests of `steps.ts` itself are new with it, and the Scale, Move and Offset tests of a value typed right after the step pass there too (Rotate's fails there on `/4` typed alone after another step).
- What the review changed from the pull request: tests and words. A key read before the tool's own edit had finished (Scale), no key kept after a copy (Move), and the check skipped for `/4` (Move, Rotate: `x3` typed first had already let the step go) passed every test before; the new Scale, Move and Rotate tests catch each. The test of a refused retype is new. The user guide says what each tool does with such a value, and that opening or closing a group counts; this entry says which of Scale's values scale and that #111 also told the tool.

### Fixed (lane 94, 2026-11-26)

- **"Restore unsaved work?": one click or one press of a key answers one offer** ([#146](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/146), from @aramshaw; [#145](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/145)). With more than one set of unsaved work kept, an answer puts the next set's offer in the same place at once. On the status line, a double-click on Discard could answer the next offer with its second click: both sets deleted in a narrow window, or, with a file waiting, the next set restored unread, depending on where the next offer's buttons landed. And the next offer's Discard was the same button, so it kept the focus: Enter pressed twice deleted two sets, and Enter held down deleted every set kept. Discard cannot be undone, so the work an unread offer named was gone. Now the offer's buttons, on the status line as on the notice over the model, answer nothing on the second click of a double-click, and the one that answered lets go of the focus, as does one a second click lands on, so Enter pressed again or held has no button to press. Enter on a button tabbed to still answers (Space did not answer before this change and does not now: the app takes it for the Select tool). The same holds for every message with buttons on the status line (`answerOnce` in `src/components/answerOnce.ts`, used by both). ADR-0003 gains a dated amendment.
- Tests: `src/components/ValueBar.test.tsx` and `src/components/StartupNotice.test.tsx` (the second and third clicks running nothing, a single click and a key press running the action, the button that answered giving up the focus to the page itself, so the next offer's button of the same name does not have it; every button of each kind of message on the status line, and every button the notice shows, answering once) and `e2e/startup.spec.ts` (two sets kept: a double-click on the status line's Discard whose second click lands on the next offer's Discard, and with a file waiting on its Restore; a real double-click in a window where the next offer's button lands under the pointer; Enter pressed twice and Enter held on a focused Discard; Enter twice on the notice's; each answering only the first, the second set still kept and the model empty, and the next offer then restoring its set when it is clicked). Where the next offer's buttons land depends on the window, the two texts and the fonts, so the real double-click finds its window from the live layout: it tries widths in turn, measures where the next offer's buttons land, aims at a point inside both buttons and checks the button under the pointer before the second click. The other two double-click tests send the second click to the next offer's button itself. On the code before this change all six e2e tests fail, and so do the new unit tests.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the real double-click test assumed a window 896 px wide, where the pointer was 1.5 px inside the next offer's Restore in a headless build on Windows; with other fonts its second click can miss, and the test then passes on the code before this change too. It now finds the window as above. The tests that a second click or key press answered nothing could pass before that press had been taken; they now end by answering the next offer with a click of its own. A second click that answers nothing now gives up the focus too: in a headless build its press gave the focus to the next offer's button under the pointer, so an Enter after the double-click would press that button. The focus after an answer is checked to be on the page itself, not only off a button. Unit tests were added for the focus after a second click, every button of each kind of message, and every button on the notice.

### Fixed (lane 93, 2026-11-25)

- **Moving or copying a group no longer slows down with the number of groups inside it** ([#142](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/142)). A Move, Rotate or Scale, a Copy and an Array walk each group they take, and each group they met built the model's child lists again (`Model::kids`, one pass over every group, component, solid, mesh and sketch of the model) to find that one group's children; each component instance moved built them twice, and each one copied once. With 11,111 groups that was 11,111 passes over the whole model for one Move, and a selection of several groups repeated it for each. Now one op builds the lists once and hands them down (`transform_node` and `copy_node` take them from the op, and a component instance's re-derivation reads them too). They stay true for what is read from them: a Move changes no item's parent, and an instance's re-derivation says whether it changed any, the lists being built again only if it did; the groups a Copy reads are in the open context and the copies go into it or into each other, so no group being copied gains or loses a child while the op runs. Measured on one desktop PC in Node with the WASM kernel, the issue's part (a 100 x 60 x 30 mm box with three edges rounded, 42 faces) in a group, each level an array of nine copies and a group of all ten, the scene and the state read after every op as the app reads them, the middle of three runs: with 10,000 parts and 11,111 groups, a Move of the one top group 4.5 s where it took 23.1 s, an Array of nine copies of the ten top groups 6.9 s where it took 17.3 s, a Copy of the top group 4.6 s where it took 33.5 s, and a Move of one of the ten top groups 0.48 s where it took 2.2 s; with 1,000 parts, 0.45 s, 0.42 s and 0.44 s where they took 0.65 s, 0.55 s and 0.72 s. The same 10,000 parts with no groups at all took 4.4 s to move, 6.8 s for the array and 4.5 s to copy, so a group now costs about what its solids cost, and ten times the parts about ten times as long. What is left is each moved or copied solid made again and checked as a valid solid. With ops run back to back and nothing read between them, the op itself grows the kernel's memory for a model this size, and the same Move took 18.8 s (38.9 s before). No result changes: the same model, ids, hashes, op log, snapshot and `.shz` bytes; no file format or stored-data change, no new op.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/group_work.rs` (a Move, a Copy and an Array of 2, of four groups together and of one group holding everything, on nested groups at 21 and 341 groups and instances, the part a solid in a group or a component instance, each building the child lists as many times at both sizes and at most four times, counted per thread through `testing::kids_builds`: three now, and 342 to 685 times at 341 on the code before this change, where both of those tests fail; and over two families of 200 seeded models, nested groups and nested groups with component instances among them, each reaching 9 deep with a node at the top counting 1 (the tests measure it and fail if it changes), holding solids, strokes, section planes and open meshes, with hidden and locked members: a Move of a group moving everything in it and nothing else and leaving every id where it was, and a Copy making it again item for item in the order it is walked, each group's groups first and then its solids and meshes, each copy in the saved snapshot field for field its original moved the same way but for its id, parent, display number, lock and the sketch a solid came from, strokes not copied; a Move refused past 10 km giving the refusal of what the group meets first; and a Move inside an open component reaching its other copies). Docs: `docs/LIMITS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 92, 2026-11-25)

- **A file the desktop app was started with no longer looks like it opened empty while it waits for "Restore unsaved work?"** ([#141](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/141), from @aramshaw; [#115](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/115)). A double-clicked `.shz` waits for that answer when the last run left unsaved work, and the question and the line "shelf.shz opens after you answer." were only in the status line, under an empty model with "Nothing yet" in the Outliner. While a file waits, the same question, with the same buttons (Restore and Discard, or Discard alone for work that cannot be restored) and the file's name, is now shown in the middle of the model area too, clear of the chips along its top; when Sawhorse starts in Sheets mode, which hides the 3D view, it is over the sheet area. It stays there until it is answered, also when another message takes the status line (the status line then shows its reminder, as before). After Restore, the "Open shelf.shz" button is shown there as well, for as long as the status line shows it. The buttons run the status line's own actions, so they do the same: Discard deletes the work and opens the file (or offers the next set, when there is one), Restore brings it back as an untitled model, Open asks about the restored work first. The second click of a double-click on the notice answers nothing: the next offer's Discard is drawn under the pointer, and it would delete work the person has not read. On the status line the next offer's buttons land where its text ends, which may or may not be under the pointer; that was not guarded by this change, nor was Enter held down or pressed twice on a focused Discard; both are fixed by the [#145](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/145) entry above. With no file waiting, the offer is on the status line only, as before. Not a system dialog, which has two answers and no room for the Open button after Restore. How old an offer may be and what Save clears are not changed. ADR-0003 gains a dated note.
- Tests: `src/components/StartupNotice.test.tsx` (what the notice shows: the status line's own offer, the offer made again when another message took the line, nothing with no offer left or no file waiting, the Open button's message after Restore, each of the 24 combinations of a waiting file, six kinds of message and an offer left checked against what may differ from nothing shown; its buttons running the message's actions, and the second click of a double-click running nothing; the error look) and `e2e/startup.spec.ts` (the question, its buttons and the file's name centred in the model area's box and clear of its chips while the file waits, kept after Ctrl+Z takes the status line, Discard from it opening the file and the notice gone; Restore from it bringing the work back with the Open button there, Open asking about the work and opening the file; a start in Sheets mode with the notice over the sheet area; two pieces of work, each discarded from the notice, and the file opening after the second; a double-click on its Discard with two pieces of work discarding only the first; a file name with no place to break wrapped inside the model area; the view under the pointer all round the notice and the notice under it on the notice; no notice with no file waiting, and none after a Restore or a Discard with none). On the code before this change the seven waiting-file e2e tests fail.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the notice's buttons ignore the second click of a double-click (a double-click on Discard with two sets of work deleted both, the second unread), a file name with no place to break wraps inside the notice (it ran out past the model area and gave the window a sideways scroll), the changelog's words on the system dialog (it has two answers), and tests: three new e2e tests (the double-click, the long name, the pointer round the notice), Restore and then Discard with no file waiting added to the e2e test with no file, and the second click and the plain look in the unit test.

### Changed (lane 91, 2026-11-25)

- **The view's right-click menu on a dimension, a guide or sketch geometry lists only what acts on it** ([#140](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/140), from @aramshaw; [#139](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/139)). A right-click on a dimension, a guide, a sketch edge or a region opened the list for a solid (Edit Group / Component, Delete, Hide, Lock, Make Group, Make Component…, Make Unique, Explode, Explode Curve, Clear Paint) with all but Delete greyed out, and Explode Curve live on a sketch circle, arc or polygon, since the rest act on solids, groups and components only. It now offers Delete alone, and with a sketch curve in the selection (a circle, an arc, a polygon or an imported curve) Delete and Explode Curve. The items are the menu bar's own, with the same names, keys and rules, and Close Group / Component is still added inside an open group or component. A selection that holds anything of a solid, group or component as well (the solid, a face, an edge, a point or a rim) keeps the solid's list; so do a sketch point (Delete refuses a point alone: "A point goes with its edges") and a section plane with something else. Empty space and one section plane are as before. ADR-0013, which said there is no viewport context menu, gains a dated amendment. Front end only (`menuTarget` in `src/components/ViewMenu.tsx`); no kernel op, stored data or file format change.
- Tests: `src/components/ViewMenu.test.tsx` (the two lists; which list each selection opens, the solid's rim, a sketch point, a curve's segment taken as an edge, an empty selection and a mixed selection included; Close Group / Component on both; and the rule before this change kept in the test and compared on every selection of up to four items from 16 kinds of entity, on something and on empty space: of 139,810, 137,010 agree, 1,554 become Delete alone and 1,246 Delete and Explode Curve, and each that differs was the solid's list with nothing its other items act on) and `e2e/view-menu.spec.ts` (a sketch region and a plain segment offered Delete alone, a circle Delete and Explode Curve, Explode Curve from it one undo step; a guide offered Delete alone, Delete from it one undo step, and with the solid under it selected too the solid's list; inside an open group a sketch region offered Delete and Close Group / Component, Delete erasing it there; a dimension offered Delete alone, where the test checked before that each of the solid's other items was greyed out, now that the menu holds Delete and nothing else).
- What the review changed from the pull request: the comparison with the rule before covered 1,231 different selections, not 20,000: its random numbers came from a product too large for exact arithmetic, so they repeated after 13,399 draws. It now goes through every selection of up to four items and checks the three counts exactly. ADR-0013's note became an amendment at the end of the record, with the paragraph it amends left as it was. The user guide's line also names groups, components and a section plane with something else.

### Fixed (lane 90, 2026-11-24)

- **A rectangle started on a box's corner stays on the face under the cursor** ([#130](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/130)). Started on a corner or an edge, a rectangle's plane follows the cursor until the second click. Over a face that holds the first corner, the second click could still be caught by a guide line that leaves the face: the line of a point the cursor had rested on earlier, also one hidden under the board, or the red or green axis through the first corner out in the air. On a top face the rectangle then went onto a new sketch beside the face ("on a plane 3/4 up"), and Push/Pull made a second board instead of a rabbet; from the near top corner of a 12 in cube in the Iso view, 5 of 25 second clicks on each side face, in a band along the face's diagonal on screen, were refused as "too small (under 0.01 mm)". Now such a guide line gives way to the point on the face under the cursor: the marker shows On Face before the click, and the rectangle is drawn on the face ("on face 2 of solid 1"). Snaps that lie on the face's plane are kept (the face's corners, edges and midpoints, guide lines in the plane), and so is a real corner, edge or midpoint of other geometry off the plane, which the marker shows and the person may mean; it goes where it went before. A lock already set with an arrow key or Shift is kept. The guide line the face rule hides can still be locked to: the Down arrow and Shift lock to it as before ("Locked to the green axis"), and the marker and the click then follow it; the red, green and blue arrows lock as before. Over nothing, or over a face that does not hold the corner, nothing changes: up into the air on an upright plane, on the ground from a bottom corner, level at the corner's height. The Rotated Rectangle's second click follows the same rule (not its width click). The rule waits for an open plane: a rectangle started inside a face keeps that face's plane, as before.
- **Two corners on one line say why nothing is drawn.** A second corner on a line through the first along a side of the rectangle (a click on the edge through the first corner, within 7 px of it, or on its far corner or midpoint) was refused by the kernel as "that shape is too small (under 0.01 mm)", and the first corner was gone. Now the tool refuses it first: "On the drawing plane both corners are on one line, so the rectangle has no width. Click off the line, or type width, depth." A second corner that lands on the first on the drawing plane (a click on the first corner, or straight above or below it on a plane already set) gets "On the drawing plane the second corner is on the first. Click the opposite corner, or type width, depth." The first corner stays, an open plane stays open, and only the click sets the words, never a hover. The Rotated Rectangle is not changed by this: its zero width still gets the kernel's words. Where a click lands is unchanged, so a strip narrower than about 10 px on screen still cannot be clicked (`docs/LIMITS.md`): type its sizes.
- **Typed sizes with the cursor still on the first corner keep the corner and say the way was a guess.** The cursor then gives no face and no direction, so the sizes ran + / + on the plane chosen at the first click, often off the face, and were refused with "the path must run strictly inside the face"; the first corner was gone. They are still tried the same way, so what drew before still draws. When the kernel refuses them, its own reason stays on the status line and the first corner stays with the plane still open. When both typed sizes are over the kernel's smallest (0.01 mm), one sentence follows the reason: "The cursor did not show which way the rectangle runs, so that was a guess: move the cursor onto the face to choose it." A size of zero or under 0.01 mm gets the kernel's words alone. Esc, another tool, a new first corner or new sizes while the sizes are on their way to the kernel: the old first corner does not come back, the kernel's refusal still shows, and the Rectangle adds nothing to it.
- What is left as it was: Line (in `settlePlane`) and Arc (in `settle`) started on a corner settle their plane from the first segment, and a second point on a guide line off the face draws in the air (a line up an edge is often meant); Circle and Polygon take their plane at the centre's click. Neither is this code path. The inference engine, the snap radii and ranking, and the remembered points are unchanged; no kernel op, stored data or file format change.
- Tests: `src/tools/rect.test.ts` (33: the plane and the second corner with the cursor over a face holding the corner and an on-face point, a remembered point's line off the face, the start's axis line off a side face, a guide line in the face's plane, a corner and a midpoint of the face, another solid's corner off the plane, a guide carrying another solid's edge; over a face not holding the corner and over nothing, up and level; the ground from a bottom corner; a lock already set, and the Down arrow and Shift locking to the hidden line, and to nothing hidden once the cursor is off it or after Esc; a plane set by a click inside the face; the Rotated Rectangle's second click and its width click; the marker and the guide lines the preview shows; the words for one line and for one point, a click straight above the first corner, the first corner and the open plane kept, hovering setting nothing, a Rotated Rectangle with no width; typed sizes refused with the cursor on the corner and with no cursor, too small, drawn with the cursor on the corner, refused with a direction, refused with no reason from the kernel, the next plane after a refusal, and Esc, a tool switch, a new first corner and new sizes while the refusal is on its way), and `e2e/rect-face.spec.ts` (7: the route that made a second board: rest on the bottom edge's midpoint, the corner, a click on the top face where that point's line runs, then Push/Pull -3/8 leaving one solid of the rabbet's volume; 25 second clicks over each of the three faces at a 12 in cube's near top corner; the click on the edge and the next click; the typed sizes on the corner, a zero size, then on the face, and the rabbet's 104.625 cubic inches; up into the air, the ground, a midpoint and a corner of the top face, as before).

### Fixed (lane 89, 2026-11-24)

- **An unnamed solid in a group that holds a component reads by the group's name in the cut list** ([#138](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/138), from @aramshaw; [#137](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/137)). A group holding a component or another group is an assembly: the cut list walks into it, and each solid directly in it is a part of its own. Such a solid that was never named read "Solid 1", the Outliner's number, which names no place in the model, and the group's name was in no row. It now reads "Carcass · solid", as the same solid in an assembly component reads by the component's name. The nearest group names it, so a solid in "Carcass" inside "Unit" reads "Carcass · solid", and one in an unnamed group inside "Unit" keeps its number. A solid with a name of its own keeps it, a group with no name leaves the number ("Solid 1"), and a group of solids alone is still one part under its own name. A solid inside a component still reads by the component, also when a named group holds it there. Renaming the group renames the row. Two unnamed solids in one assembly group now share a name, as they do in an assembly component (each keeps its own row), so the MCP server's `summary` lists that name under `shared_names`. Only the name a row is given changes: not the parts, their sizes, the nesting's layout, the file format or the model hash. The cut list's own hash covers names, so it moves for a model with such a solid; no golden holds one. ADR-0006 gains a dated note.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/cutlist.rs` (the group's name, a named solid and the component beside it, renaming the group and then the solid; the nearest of two nested groups, and an unnamed inner group inside a named one; a top-level solid, an unnamed group and a group named only spaces in a hand-edited file keeping the number; a group of solids alone and a solid in an assembly component named as before; an unnamed solid in a named group made inside an open copy of a component reading by the component; a 64-character group name read whole, and the same name with a solid hidden, a solid on a tag, the group hidden, the group locked, the group open and another group open, while an unnamed group of solids in the assembly group keeps "Group"; in a hand-edited file, a group name with spaces at its edges shown as stored and a solid named only spaces keeping that name), `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (two unnamed boards in a group with a component board share the group's name in `summary` and under `shared_names`, and renaming the group lists both rows as changed) and `e2e/cutlist.spec.ts` (the Cut list pane's rows for a group holding a component, a named solid and an unnamed one). On the code before this change, 4 of the 6 new kernel tests fail (the other two hold what does not change), and the server test and the e2e test fail.
- What the review changed from the pull request: tests only, three new kernel tests and one extended. Versions of the code that named a solid by the nearest named group past an unnamed one, named a component's solid by a named group inside it, trimmed or cut short the group's name, read an unnamed group's own row by its parent, treated a solid named only spaces as unnamed, or left the number for a hidden solid, a solid on a tag, a hidden or locked group or while a context was open all passed the pull request's tests; each now fails one.

### Added (lane 88, 2026-11-23)

- **The question over unsaved changes has a Save answer** ([#136](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/136), from @akvalentine977; [#120](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/120)). With unsaved changes, File > New, Open and Close asked "Discard the unsaved changes to this model?", and closing the window, File > Exit and a logout on KDE Plasma the same "and close Sawhorse?", with OK and Cancel: to keep the work a person answered Cancel, saved, and did the thing again. Now the question is "Save the changes to cabinet.shz?" ("this model" when it has no file; "before Sawhorse closes" at a close) with three answers, in this order. **Save** saves as File > Save does, asking for a name when the model is untitled, and then carries on with what was asked. **Discard Changes** does what OK did. **Cancel** does what Cancel did. A save that is cancelled (the Save dialog dismissed, or No at "already exists. Replace it?") or that fails leaves everything as it was, as Cancel does: the model, its name, the unsaved work kept for Restore unsaved work? and the window stay, what was asked for does not happen, and a save that failed says why on the status line, as File > Save does. Every place that asked asks this: File > New, File > Open, a file dropped on the window, a file the app was started with, File > Close, an empty SketchUp file over unsaved work, Restore when the open model cannot be set aside, the window's X and File > Exit. "... already exists. Replace it?" is as it was.
- The question is the app's own dialog, over the model, the same in the desktop app and the browser build (`src/components/UnsavedDialog.tsx`, in the naming dialog's style); the desktop's message dialog and the browser's `window.confirm` are no longer used for it. Save has the focus, so Enter saves. Escape is Cancel. Discard Changes is never the default: it takes a click, or Tab and then Enter. On Windows and macOS, Enter at the old question chose OK and discarded the work (a listed limit); that is gone with it. From the click on Save until the save has ended the dialog shows "Saving cabinet.shz…" and offers Cancel alone, which gives up on the save: nothing more is written after it (a write the shell has already started finishes whole, as every save does: beside the file, then renamed over it), and nothing else happens. In the browser build a save without the browser's file picker is a download, which counts as saved once the browser has been handed the file, as File > Save counts it.
- It is a real modal (`src/components/Modal.tsx`: a `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()`): `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, named by its question; nothing behind it takes a click, the focus or a key (no tool key, Ctrl+Z or Delete reaches the app); Tab stays inside it; and the focus goes back where it was when it closes. The naming dialog (Make Component) did the first of those four only. It is the same modal now, which also means the page behind it no longer takes clicks while it is open: a click on the model or the menu bar while the naming dialog is up does nothing, and its name field keeps the focus. A key in a dialog shown over another (the close question over the naming dialog) is that dialog's alone: Escape there cancels the question and leaves the naming dialog open. A file dropped on the desktop window and a plugin's batch still reach the model while a dialog is up.
- One question at a time (`src/io/unsaved.ts`). A question asked while another shows its answers takes its place, and the first ends as Cancel does: a close request, or a file dropped on the window, while File > New's question is up; to the shell a close question that another took the place of is "keep", however quickly it happened. A question asked while a save chosen at the one before is running waits for that save, and is not asked if the save left nothing unsaved; one asked from inside the edit queue (an empty SketchUp file, Restore) does not wait, since that save waits for the queue, and ends as Cancel. A change that lands while a save chosen at the question is running (a file dropped on the window while the Save dialog is up, a plugin's batch) is not in the file: the model keeps its unsaved mark, the status line says the model changed while it was being saved, and what was asked for does not go ahead over it. File > Save no longer marks such a change saved either.
- **A close request while a save is running.** The close guard closes the window on a second close request two seconds after a question the page has not answered, for a page that has stopped answering. A person choosing a file name is not that, so from the click on Save until the save has ended, its Save dialog included, the page says a new word to the shell, "saving", every 250 ms, and the guard's rule becomes: a close request closes the window only when two seconds have passed since the question went out and since the last "saving" (`sawhorse_close_guard::Guard`; the shell's command only hands it the word and the time). The word counts only while a question is out, and each one buys at most two seconds: a page that stops saying it is the page of the old rule, so two close requests two seconds apart with no "saving" between them still always close the window, and the unsaved work is offered back at the next start. To the shell a save that was cancelled or failed is "keep", and a save that worked is "close". Before "close" the page waits for this session's autosave to be deleted (at most a second, `DISCARD_WAIT_MS`), after Save as after Discard Changes: a save starts that delete and does not wait for it, and a window closed straight after one could keep the autosave, which the next start offered back as "Restore unsaved work?" for work that was in its file.
- On a close request over unsaved changes the shell un-minimises the window and asks for the focus before it asks the page: the question is in Sawhorse's own window now, and a logout on KDE Plasma would otherwise wait behind a question nobody can see. Whether the window then comes forward is the desktop's choice, and on the seven Linux desktops tried by hand none of them let it: see Tried by hand below.
- What does not change. A close with nothing unsaved closes at once. The browser build still does not ask before its tab closes or reloads. On GNOME and Cinnamon a logout does not close the window first, so the desktop's own dialog warns, as before. A Cancel that comes back sooner than a person could give it still counts as the question failing (`ANSWER_FLOOR_MS`). No permission is added: the webview still cannot close, destroy, un-minimise or focus a window. No file-format change.
- What the review changed from the pull request: a question asked from inside the edit queue waited for a Save chosen at another question, which itself waited for the queue, so both waited for good and no edit or save ran again; a change that landed while the Save dialog was up was marked saved without being in the file, and New, Open, Close or the close went ahead over it; one Escape at the close question cancelled the naming dialog under it too; a press outside the naming dialog's field moved the focus off it, so typing went nowhere; and a close question that a dropped file took the place of within 150 ms was told to the shell as "failed", so the next close request closed without asking. Each is fixed as described above, with a test. The user guide's Closing paragraph says which parts were tried only on Linux, and its paragraph on work too large to keep names the new question.
- Tests: `src-tauri/close-guard` (29, twelve of them new: the two seconds held as a number, 1.9 s waiting and 2.1 s closing, the word holding the window for a minute of words and for a timer slowed to once a second, the window closing two seconds after the last word, a word with no question out not kept for a later one, the word neither answering the question nor bringing back one that failed, a save that worked closing whichever of "saved" and "close" comes first, a clock that steps back; `never_trapped_two_requests_two_seconds_apart_always_close` kept as it was, and its twin, over every sequence of up to six of the page's words and close requests with "saving" at three spacings among them: two requests two seconds apart with no "saving" between them always close; and the other side of it, a word between them holds). `sawhorse-logout-hold`: "saving" has its rule (nothing for the hold). `src/io/unsaved.test.ts` (30: the words, each answer, the beat starting at Save and stopping when the save ends however it ends, Cancel while saving, one question at a time, a question from inside the edit queue not held behind a save that waits for the queue, a save over a model that changed meanwhile not going ahead, a dialog that cannot be shown). `src/io/closing.test.ts` (24: each way the question can end, a question another took the place of kept and not failed, the autosave's delete waited for before "close" after Save and after a save that was running, a delete that fails or never ends not holding the window, and the handler over the real question with the shell hearing "saving"). `e2e/close.spec.ts` (24: each answer at the window's X, File > Exit and File > Close, a model with a file and one without, the Save dialog dismissed, a save that fails, "saving" for as long as the Save dialog is up and none after) and `e2e/unsaved.spec.ts` (30: each answer at File > New, Close and Open in the browser build with the saved file opening as the model that was there, a dropped file, a failed save, the keys, the modal and the naming dialog, and in the page as the desktop shell runs it a Save dialog at File > New, giving up on a save, a file dropped on the window, a file dropped while the Save dialog is up, a close request over File > Close's question, a file dropped the moment the close question is up, Escape at the close question over the naming dialog, the focus put back, File > Save with an Undo and a dropped file while its dialog is up (the file the model at the click, the model unsaved with its autosave kept), no key at the question reaching a listener on the window or the document, a press outside the naming dialog's field leaving the focus in it). The specs that answered the old question (`startup`, `window-title`, `files`, `session`) press the new dialog's buttons; those that accepted it in passing (`scale`, `curves`, `corpus`, `private-fixtures`) press Discard Changes as it comes up. Not covered by a browser test: the question at an empty SketchUp file and at Restore, which ask from inside the edit queue (`queued` in `src/io/unsaved.test.ts`). Docs: the user guide's Closing paragraph, `docs/LIMITS.md` (Enter at "Replace it?", and the question behind a minimised window), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tried by hand by akvalentine977 on 2026-11-21, on the .deb, .rpm and AppImage built one commit before the autosave fix named below: GNOME 46 (Ubuntu 24.04), 48.7 (Debian 13), 50.1 (Ubuntu 26.04) and 51.1 (Fedora 45), Cinnamon 6.6.4 (Linux Mint 22.3), and KDE Plasma 6.8.2 (openSUSE Tumbleweed, and CachyOS with the AppImage). On all seven: the question, its three answers and Save in focus as described; Escape cancelled; nothing behind the question took a click; Save with a name saved the file and closed Sawhorse; the question named the file, and Discard Changes closed without saving; Enter at File > New saved with no Save dialog; a Save dialog cancelled left the model, its title and the window. **With Save chosen and the Save dialog left open for ten seconds or more, Quit or Close from the dock or taskbar did not close Sawhorse, on all seven.** Tab and then Enter chose Discard Changes; once, on Debian, coming back to the question from another workspace, it took Tab twice, and Tab once on a repeat without the workspace change. **A minimised Sawhorse did not come forward on any of them**, nor one on another workspace: GNOME showed a notice that the window "is ready", Plasma marked the taskbar entry on Tumbleweed and showed nothing on CachyOS, Cinnamon needed a click on the panel; the question was there each time on going back, and the user guide says this. Logging out of Plasma with an untitled unsaved model: the question showed, Save opened the Save dialog and the model was saved; on CachyOS the logout then carried on, on Tumbleweed Sawhorse closed and the logout waited for Plasma's countdown to end. **Found by this round and fixed:** after Save at a close, the next start offered "Restore unsaved work?" for the work just saved; the autosave was still in the store on four of the seven. A script that draws a rectangle, closes the window, answers Save and reads the webview's IndexedDB found the autosave left in 10 of 15 runs on five of the desktops with that build (0 of 6 after Discard Changes), and in 0 of 19 on six of them with the fix (not run on CachyOS, where the script cannot start an AppImage). **Looked at again by hand on 2026-11-22 with the fix, on the same seven desktops:** after Save at the window's X with a new name, after Enter at the X on a model that has a file, and on both Plasma systems after a logout with Save, the next start offered nothing back, and the file held what was drawn. Read from each system's disk afterwards, the autosave store held no record on any of the seven. On Ubuntu 24.04 the second of those looks did not go as written: two Sawhorse instances had been started in the same second (the session's journal shows it), and the second stayed open on its empty model when the first saved and closed. On Tumbleweed the logout went at once after the save this time, so its wait for the countdown was seen once in two tries. His rounds were on builds from before the rebases onto #125, #127, #128 and #130's fix and before the changes the review made; nothing was tried by hand on the build of this commit. Not tried: Windows and macOS, by hand or by building there; the desktop shell was type-checked for Windows (clippy for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`), not built or run.

### Added (lane 87, 2026-11-23)

- **`add_board` in the MCP server makes a component on request** ([#135](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/135), from @aramshaw; [#116](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/116)). `add_board` takes `component` (default `false`). With `true` the board is wrapped in a component named after it (`make_component` in the same batch, so the board, the component and any library material are one undo step), and `board` in the answer carries its `instance` and `definition` ids beside the `object`. The board is where and what it would be without `component`. `apply_ops` copies, arrays and moves the instance, in `nodes`: a batch cannot open the component, so an op that moves, copies, reshapes or deletes the solid inside it is refused with the kernel's words ("double-click it to edit inside it"), and the component board's grain and stock are set on its definition (`"part": {"definition": id}`). Copies and arrays of that instance (`copy` or `array` with `nodes`) are instances of its definition, so twelve slats read as one row of 12 and stay alike. A second component board of the same name is a definition of its own, listed under `shares_name_with`. Without `component`, or with it `false` or `null`, `add_board` makes the loose solid it made before and answers as before. A component's row in `summary` and in `changes` names its `definition`, the id its `target` gives; other rows are as they were. `shares_name_with` leaves out the new board's own row when it is a component, as it did for a loose one. The `apply_ops` description names `nodes` beside `objects` for `copy` and `array`, and the server's instructions tell an agent to add a part made more than once with `component: true` and copy its instance. The default stays `false`: making it `true` is a decision of its own. The MCP server only: no kernel, file format, model hash or golden change. Docs: `docs/MCP.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the `add_board` description and `docs/MCP.md` said the solid inside a component board could not be edited through `apply_ops`, and the changelog that it could not be named in a batch; renaming it, painting it and setting its grain are taken, so they now say which ops are refused and that grain and stock go on the definition (set on the solid inside, they leave the cut list as it was). `component: null` was refused, where every other optional argument of `add_board` reads `null` as left out; it now does too. The `add_board` description says a second component board of the same name is a part of its own. Tests were added where the pull request's left a gap: in a new model the board's object, instance and definition ids are all 1, so an answer giving the object id as the instance passed every test; spaces round a name of 64 characters, which the component must be named without; a component board over a sketch facing down, whose extrude must turn as a loose board's does; and the descriptions and instructions, checked for what they say rather than for the word `component`.
- Tests: `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (`component: true` wrapping the board where and what it would be without it, in one undo step, over a sketch facing down too, with the instance and definition answered and on the summary row, a name at the 64-character limit, one past it, and spaces round one of 64 trimmed; left out, `false` and `null` giving the same model and the same answer, no definition on a loose row, a `component` that is not true or false refused; an array of a component board one row of 12 instances of one definition, a second component board of the same name its own definition under `shares_name_with`, its instance and definition ids those of its own row; the board's object, instance and definition three different ids, the instance moved through `nodes`, the solid inside refused by `transform`, `copy` and `delete_object`, and grain set on the definition; no `definition` on a group's row or on the rows of an assembly component's parts, and a component's own row naming it; the instructions telling an agent to pass `component: true`, the `add_board` description giving the default as false, the `apply_ops` description naming `nodes` and saying copies of an instance are instances of its definition, and the `summary` description naming the definition on a component's row). All seven fail on the code before this change.

### Added (lane 86, 2026-11-23)

- **A right-click menu in the 3D view, and Edit > Edit Group / Component** ([#133](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/133), from @aramshaw; [#107](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/107)). A right press let go within 4 px of where it went down (`DRAG_PX`, the distance that already tells a left click from a selection box, now in `src/lib/gesture.ts` so the view, the tools, the tool driver and the Outliner read one number) is a right-click; on macOS a Control+click is too. Past that it is the pan it always was, the point grabbed at the press under the pointer; until then the view does not move, so a click that wobbles opens the menu over what was under it. Another button pressed during the right press, or the press cancelled by the browser or the window, opens no menu. No hold time. The menu holds only the menu bar's own commands, the very items of the menu bar, so names, keys, greyed-out rules and actions are the same: on a solid, group or component Edit Group / Component, Delete, Hide, Lock, Make Group, Make Component…, Make Unique, Explode, Explode Curve and Clear Paint (on a dimension, a guide, a sketch edge or a region the same list, Delete the only one not greyed out); on a section plane Activate or Deactivate Cut, Reverse Section Plane, Create Group from Slice and Delete; on empty space Select All, Select None, Zoom Extents, Undo and Redo; inside an open group or component, Close Group / Component after them. What is under the pointer is read as Select's click reads it (`contextPick` in `src/tools/select.ts`, Select's own code moved out of the class, with Select's pick context, so a model dimension takes it on its line, its number and its extension lines): a thing outside the selection is selected first, on its own (Shift or Ctrl held with the right button does not add or toggle), one inside it keeps the selection, and a thing outside the open group closes back to where it is, as a click does; empty space keeps the selection. Selecting this way is not an undo step. A choice that changes the model is one undo step, as from the menu bar. Esc, a left or middle press or the wheel in the view, or the window losing focus closes the menu with nothing changed (that press and that turn of the wheel go no further, and that Esc does not also clear the selection or close a group); a right press elsewhere in the view opens the menu there. It also closes when the model, the selection or the tool changes under it (keys other than Esc still reach the app, as with the Outliner's and the Cut list's menus) and when the window is resized. While a tool is part way through a step (its `busy()`, read once the clicks already on their way have reached the tool) a right-click does nothing, so it cannot cancel the step, and a right-click overtaken by a key or another press while its pick is on its way (Esc, a right-drag straight after it) is dropped; a right-drag still pans; between steps the menu opens in any tool. Entity Info is not in it, as it is not in the menu bar. Edit Group / Component, beside Close Group / Component in the Edit menu, opens the one selected group or component as a double-click does, and is greyed out on a locked one, as the Outliner's entry for it is (a double-click on a locked one is refused, as before). The Outliner's entry, Edit inside, is now called Edit Group / Component too, and the Outliner takes a press that moves exactly 4 px as a click, as the view and the tools do (it took it as a drag). Front end only: no kernel, file format, model hash or golden change. Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `README.md` (out of "Not yet"), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tests: `src/viewport/controls.test.ts` (a right press let go within 4 px a click that moves nothing, past it a pan and no click, a press the window drops, cancels or chords with the left button no click, a Mac Control+click a click and a Control+drag a pan), `src/components/ViewMenu.test.tsx` (the items each right-click offers, the menu bar's own objects, separators, a choice running the action once, Esc, a press outside, the wheel, focus lost and a resize closing it, Esc going no further, a left or middle press or the wheel on the view held back and a right press there and a press elsewhere let through, and nothing held back once the menu is gone), `src/components/structure.test.tsx` (the Outliner: exactly 4 px a click, 5 px a drag) and `e2e/view-menu.spec.ts` (a solid, with Hide one undo step by the undo depth; empty space and Esc changing nothing; a right-drag; a tool part way through, Line with a point down and Push/Pull part way through a pull, a right-click straight after the click that starts a line included; a section plane, its Delete one undo step; inside an open group; two selected solids kept; a solid outside the open group; Edit Group / Component and the lock; a dimension selected by its number, its Delete one undo step and the Delete key closing the menu; a press, a middle drag and the wheel in the view closing the menu and changing nothing else, and a right press there opening it there; the menu closing when only the selection, or only the tool, changes; a right-click overtaken by Esc or by a right-drag before its pick comes back opening nothing; the menu at the page point of the right-click, the canvas not starting at the page's top left, and kept inside a small window near its right and bottom edges). On the code before this change, 11 of the 13 end-to-end tests and 2 of the 4 new tests in `src/viewport/controls.test.ts` fail; the ones that pass hold what stays the same or what that code never did: a right-drag past 4 px still pans (unit and e2e), a cancelled or chorded right press opens nothing (unit), and a right-click overtaken by Esc or a right-drag opens nothing (e2e). `src/components/ViewMenu.test.tsx` has no menu to load there, and the Outliner's 4 px case fails with its old `< DRAG_PX`.
- The pull request's own last commit makes the right-click read the pick with Select's pick context, so a model dimension's number and extension lines (taken by Select's click since lane 85) take it; this change has that line and the end-to-end test above for it. What the review changed from the pull request: a left press, a middle drag or the wheel in the view that closed the menu also reached the tool and the camera (it cleared the selection, started a line, orbited or zoomed); it now only closes the menu. The menu stayed open over a selection, a model or a tool that had changed under it (the Delete key, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+Z, a tool key, a section plane removed by Undo), its items then acting on what was there instead; it now closes, and closes when the window is resized. A right-click straight after a click was checked against the tool before that click had reached it, so a line just started could be given the menu; it now waits for the clicks already on their way. A right-click whose pick was still on its way when Esc or a right-drag came opened its menu after them; it is now dropped. A section plane's menu has Delete, as Edit > Delete with a plane selected does. The Outliner's 4 px and the name of its entry, above. The tool driver reads `DRAG_PX` from `src/lib/gesture.ts`. The end-to-end test that waited a fixed 300 ms for nothing to happen now waits for a later hover to come back through the same kernel queue.

### Fixed (lane 85, 2026-11-23)

- **A click with nothing under it, and a drag, no longer draw nothing without a word** ([#130](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/130)). In the Front or Right view of an empty model a click meets no ground and no face (nor does one wherever else the eye ray misses both). Line, Rectangle, Circle and Arc (and Rotated Rectangle, Polygon and 3-Point Arc), Tape, Dimension, Rotate and Move then did nothing and the status line did not change. A press and release 5 px or more apart is a drag, which those tools dropped without a word, as did Section Plane, Follow Me, Paint and a Push/Pull drag started on nothing. Nothing is drawn still, and the status line now says why: "Nothing to draw on there: click the ground or a face, or use Top or Iso." (Tape "Nothing to measure there", Dimension "Nothing to dimension there", Rotate "Nothing to turn about there" and Move "Nothing to move from there" with something selected, each with the same advice; Move and Rotate with nothing selected say what their click on the ground says then, "Click a solid or a sketch shape, or select what to move first.", since the ground would not do either), and for a drag "Click, move, then click again. A drag does not draw." (Tape "does not measure", Dimension "does not place a dimension"; Rotate "Click the centre, then where the angle starts and ends. A drag does not turn.", or with nothing selected "Click a solid or a sketch shape, or select what to move first. A drag does not turn."; Section Plane "Click to place a section plane. A drag does not place one."; Follow Me "Click the profile: a face, or a region of a sketch. A drag does not sweep.", or the path still to select; Paint "Click a face to paint it. A drag does not paint.", or with no material chosen "Choose a material in the Materials pane first (or Alt+click a face to pick one up). A drag does not paint."; Push/Pull what its click on nothing says, "Click a face or a region of a sketch to push or pull it."). The advice in each of these, before a gesture starts, was followed in a headless build and led to the step it names. A press and release 4 px apart is still a click. Halfway through a shape, the point already placed stays where it was and the words end with the next step, so that hint stays on the status line: "A drag does not draw. Click the opposite corner, or type width, depth." and "Nothing to click on there. Click the opposite corner, or type width, depth." Only the click or the drag says it, never a hover, and the next click that lands says what it did, as before. Left as they were, since a click or a drag there already means something or already says something: Select (a click on nothing clears the selection, a drag is a box), the Eraser (a click on nothing places no point; a drag sweeps), Orbit and Pan, the drags of Move, Scale, Offset and Push/Pull, and the clicks of Push/Pull, Offset, Scale, Paint, Follow Me and Section Plane. Where a click lands does not change: there is still no drawing plane under the cursor in the Front view of an empty model (typing a size works there, and Top and Iso draw on the ground).
- **A dimension in the model takes a click on its number and on its extension lines, the Eraser's hint names it, and an Eraser drag across one erases it** ([#129](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/129)). Only the dimension line took a click, within 5 px, so a click on the number, or on the short lines from the measured points out to it, erased and selected nothing (or the face behind). Now, for Erase and for Select, the number's box and both extension lines (with the bit past the dimension line, as drawn) take the click too, the lines within 5 px as the dimension line is (`src/lib/dimPick.ts`). On its lines a dimension still gives way to a point, an edge or a guide within reach, as its line always did, and takes the click over a face or a sketch region; inside the number's box the number wins over what is under it, edges and points included, since it is drawn over them. Neither tool shows a hover highlight for a dimension, before or now. The other tools pick as before: a number over a face still gives Push/Pull, Move and Paint the face. The Eraser's hint said only "Click or drag over edges to erase"; it reads "Click or drag over edges, guides, dimensions. Ctrl softens and smooths, Shift hides, Ctrl+Shift unsoftens." An Eraser drag across a dimension (its line, number or extension lines) or across a guide erases it with the rest of the drag, in the drag's one undo step, as `docs/USER-GUIDE.md` said already; a drag took neither before. With Ctrl or Shift held a drag still flags edges only. An Eraser sweep whose release never reached the view (the button let go outside it) was erased by the next drag, however small and wherever it was (an edge so before, and now a dimension or a guide too); a new press now drops such a sweep, and it erases nothing. Not changed: a dimension in the model still does not follow its solid and cannot be edited or moved; erase it and place it again with D.
- Tests: `src/tools/dropped.test.ts` (every tool above: a hover over nothing sets nothing, a click on nothing and a drag before the first point and halfway through, the words, the point kept, the next click's own words; a rectangle and a line finished from the point clicked before a drag), `src/lib/dimPick.test.ts` (the line, each extension line and the number picked, just outside each not, a dimension seen end on, one behind the eye, the nearer of two, and what wins over a point, an edge, a guide and a face), `src/tools/driver.test.ts` (the number and an extension line over a face for the tools that ask, the face for the others; through the pointer path, a press and release 4 px apart a click and 5 px apart a drag), `src/tools/erase.test.ts` (the hint; a click; a drag over an edge, a dimension and a guide in one op; Ctrl leaving them alone; a sweep let go outside the view erasing nothing on the next drag), `src/tools/select.test.ts`, and `e2e/quiet-clicks.spec.ts` (Front then Top with Rectangle, drags with Rectangle and Line, a first corner kept through a drag, the messages fitting the status line in a 1000 px window; a dimension erased by its number and by an extension line, selected by its number and deleted, and erased by a drag with an edge, each undone in one step; 4 px and 5 px between press and release; an Eraser sweep let go outside the view). Run on the code before this change, with `src/tools/dropped.ts` and `src/lib/dimPick.ts` from this change copied in so the files load, `npx vitest run src/tools/dropped.test.ts src/lib/dimPick.test.ts src/tools/driver.test.ts src/tools/erase.test.ts src/tools/select.test.ts` fails 42 of its 75 cases (the rest test what does not change, or the copied `dimPick.ts` itself), and `npx playwright test e2e/quiet-clicks.spec.ts` fails all 4 of its tests.

### Added (lane 84, 2026-11-23)

- **Drag rows in the Outliner onto a group to put them in it, or onto a strip below the rows to take them out to the model, through a new kernel op, `move_into`** ([#128](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/128), from @aramshaw; [#117](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/117)). Before, the only ways in or out of a group were Make Group and Explode, which loses the group's name, tag, flags, grain, stock, material and transform. `move_into { items, into }` takes solids, groups and component instances directly in the open context, as Make Group does, and puts them in the group `into`, or in the model when `into` is `null`, as one undo step, answering `moved_into { into, objects, nodes }`. Only where each item sits in the tree changes: nothing moves in the world, what an item holds goes with it, and its name, tag, hidden and locked flags, grain, stock, material and an instance's definition stay. It is refused, changing nothing, for no items, one or a target that is not there, an item not directly in the open context, a locked item or target or a target inside a locked group, a group into itself or something inside it ("a group cannot go into itself or into something inside it"), a component instance or a group inside one as the target ("that is a component: a part put into it would be in every copy"), an item already in the target (the whole op), and with the open context inside a component instance, where Make Group and Explode do work: an item moved out of a definition would keep a slot with no instance round it, and a move within one definition is left out. A repeated item is dropped without a word. What follows from the tree: a group of only solids is one part in the cut list, so a solid moved into one joins that part (its own grain and stock are kept but not read while it is there), and a group moved into one makes it a group of parts; the section planes around a solid's new place cut it; a group left empty goes, as after any step, unless it is open, and a section plane in it goes with it, since tidying does not count planes. The Outliner drags a row, or the selected rows, onto a group's row (into it), onto any other row (into what holds that row), or onto the strip ("Drop here to move out to the model"); it opens what holds the rows first, as Make Group does, refuses rows from two places before opening anything, puts back the context that was open, and names on the status line any group the move left empty. No order is set by a drop: the Outliner lists groups and components first, then solids in the order they were made, as before, and the row order is not stored. A drop where the rows already are moves nothing. A press becomes a drag after 4 px, so a click still selects; only the left button's release drops; Esc cancels; locked rows, imported meshes and section planes do not drag, a locked row takes no drop, and selected rows that include a locked one are refused whole on the drop ("that is locked: unlock it first"); a drag ends, dropping nothing, when the left button is found up, the window loses focus or another button is pressed (that press opens no menu). Plugins and the MCP server reach the op through their batches, which cannot open or close a group, so a batch works in the context that is open: with the model open, it moves top-level items into a group and cannot take one out; a plugin run while the person has a group open, or the MCP server on a file saved with a group open, can move what is directly in that group out of it. Unsaved work that holds a `move_into` cannot be restored by 0.1.0-alpha.7 or older, as for any new op: Restore there answers "Could not restore the unsaved work: unknown variant `move_into`, expected one of" followed by the name of every op, and the work stays offered with Discard beside it, so nothing is lost without a word. No file-format change: a `.shz` already stores each item's parent. No golden or older log changes. Docs: [ADR-0005](docs/adr/0005-groups-components.md) (a dated amendment, the old text kept), `docs/PLUGINS.md`, `docs/MCP.md`, `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`; `src/kernel/types.ts` has the op and its answer.
