- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/move_into.rs` (solids, a group and an instance in and out with nothing moving; what an item keeps; every refusal and its words; a repeated item; the three cut list cases; the landing place's section planes; a group left with only a plane; a scene whose group goes; replay; a property test that in and out again gives back the hash once the open context is put back), the fuzzer's `MoveInto` intent in `crates/kernel/tests/sequences.rs` (plain sequences and plugin batches), `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (in through `apply_ops`, out refused), `src/components/structure.test.tsx` (what drags, where a drop lands, a click against a drag, Esc, the strip, rows from two places handed over) and `e2e/outliner-drag.spec.ts` (in, out, the group that went, several rows, a drop where they already are, Esc, locked rows and groups). All fail on the code before this change.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the docs said plugins and the MCP server could never take an item out of a group, which holds only while the model is open (a `.shz` keeps the group that was open when it was saved); they now say when it holds, tested in the kernel (a batch with a group open) and in `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (a file saved with a group open). A drag outlived a release the page never got (a move with no button down kept it going, so the next click dropped), a right press during a drag opened the menu, and a right or middle press left the drag going; now a move with the left button up, the window losing focus, or any other button going down ends the drag and drops nothing, and a right press so used opens no menu. A drag of selected rows that include a locked one did nothing and said nothing; it now goes on to the drop, which is refused whole with the kernel's words. A right button released during a drag dropped the rows on the row under the pointer (and the right-click menu opened too); now only the left button's release ends a drag or drops. The status line says a group left empty was "removed" in place of "went". The user guide says a drop sets no order and where a dropped row shows, and that the move needs the mouse. Tests were added where the pull request's left a gap: a group refused going into a group two levels inside it (a check of one level passed every test before), groups moved and turned and nested two deep left and entered with nothing moving, Undo, Redo and replay; what an item keeps of its material, stock, a locked solid inside a group and an instance's definition and other copy; a target inside a group locked two levels up; a mesh, a sketch or a section plane as an item, no `items`, `items` not a list and `into` a string, which do not read and which a batch refuses naming the field ("op 0 (move_into): field `items`: unknown variant `mesh`, expected `object` or `node`", "op 0 (move_into): missing field `items`"); a hidden target and an item in a hidden group, which are let through; the op's and the answer's stored bytes, `into` written `null` and read as the model when absent; the open context a group nested inside a component instance, refused as inside a component; in `src/components/structure.test.tsx`, a press that moves 3 px still a click and one of 5 px a drag, a right button released in a drag dropping nothing, a drag ending with the left button found up, on losing focus and on another button, Esc going no further than the Outliner, no drag while a name is typed, and a locked row among the selected ones going with the drag; and in `e2e/outliner-drag.spec.ts`, a drag telling the active tool (Scale's box and grips go with the selection, a line half drawn starts again), Esc in a drag leaving a line half drawn to be finished, a drag with a group open opening it again after, and a selection holding a locked group refused on the drop ("that is locked: unlock it first") and the checks that nothing moved wait for every queued edit and compare the undo depth (waiting showed that the locked group's check read the model hash before the lock itself had landed), and a drop of a row that is not selected beside another in its own place, which the pull request's test did not reach (its drop landed on a selected row, which is not a place to drop). The fuzzer's `MoveInto` now picks mostly a group other than the open one as the target, and the model only while a group is open: over 10,000 cases (seed 20261011) it was accepted 2 times in 774 before and 7 in 774 after, since most generated models hold no second group to move into; the unit and property tests carry the op.

### Fixed (lane 83, 2026-11-23)

- **`copy`, `array` and `transform` take `nodes` alone, and a known op with a field missing or of the wrong kind says which field** ([#127](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/127), from @aramshaw; [#116](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/116)). An agent copying a component instance through the MCP server's `apply_ops` with `{"op": "array", "nodes": [1], "xform": ..., "count": 5}` was refused with "not an op the kernel knows (missing field `objects`)": `Copy`, `Array` and `Transform` in `crates/kernel/src/model.rs` defaulted `nodes` to empty on reading but required `objects`, and `crates/kernel/src/refbatch.rs` called every op that did not read an op the kernel does not know. Now `objects` is empty when left out, as `nodes` is, and is still written as before (`[]`), so no stored log, golden or model hash changes. A batch op whose name the kernel knows but which does not read says so in the form of a refusal, naming the op: "op 0 (array): missing field `count`" with serde's own words for a missing field, and for one field of the wrong kind, the field too, "op 0 (array): field `count`: invalid type: string \"three\", expected u32", a wrong value inside a field included ("op 0 (rename): field `item`: unknown variant `objekt` ..."). Serde names a wrong field only by what it expected, so the field is found by reading the op again without each field in turn. Only a field that does not read alone with the op's name is tried that way: reading ignores fields an op does not have, and an op holding 16,000 of them after a wrong one took 20 s to refuse when every field was tried (release build in WSL), where an op of 4 MB with 240,000 of them is now refused in under 0.3 s. Two wrong fields at once name none, and a field missing one level down (`translation` in `xform`) is named without the level, as serde gives it. An op the kernel does not know, one with no `op`, or an `op` that is not a name, keeps "not an op the kernel knows". The op's name alone tells which, so a wrong value inside a field spelt like the op (`{"rename": 1}` as `rename`'s `item`) names the field. Copy, array and transform with neither list are refused, as before, with no undo step: in a batch, "op 0 (copy): nothing is selected to transform (`objects` and `nodes` are both empty or left out)", where it said "op 0 (copy): nothing is selected to transform". A misspelt `objects` (`"object": [4]`) now reads as left out and gets that refusal, where it said "op 0: not an op the kernel knows (missing field `objects`)". An array's count is still checked first. `src/kernel/types.ts` marks `objects` optional for the three ops. Docs: `docs/PLUGINS.md` (both lists empty when left out, and the refusals), `docs/MCP.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the cheap test above before each field is tried (the search was quadratic in the fields of one op, and a message may hold 4 MB); the op's name alone deciding whether it is an op the kernel knows (an `item` of `{"rename": 1}` in a `rename` was called an unknown op); and the refusal for neither list naming both. No change to what is read or written.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/src/refbatch.rs` (`copy`, `array` and `transform` of an instance by `nodes` alone, four instances in one cut list row; `objects` written as `[]` after reading without it, and read back the same; missing and wrong fields named for required and optional fields of three ops, and a wrong value inside `rename`'s `item`, also one spelt `rename`; two wrong fields naming none; an unknown op, one with no `op` and `"op": 5` still unknown; each of the three ops with `nodes` alone and with `"objects": []` giving the same model hash, log hash and log; neither list refused naming both, with nothing changed, an array of 0 copies still refused for its count and `move_sections` with no planes as before; a later op of a batch named by its own index and name; an op with 20,000 fields it does not have and a wrong one after them refused naming it in under 5 s) and `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (`copy_and_array_take_nodes_alone`, through the server). All fail on the code before this change.

### Fixed (lane 82, 2026-11-20)

- **On Linux, the Open and Save dialogs start in a folder you would look in** ([#125](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/125), from @akvalentine977; [#71](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/71), [#80](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/80)). The page gave the desktop's dialogs no folder: Save a bare name (`Untitled.shz`) and Open nothing. GTK's chooser then starts Save in the process's working directory, which for an AppImage is the mount it runs from (`/tmp/.mount_Sawhor...`, read-only and gone when the app closes), and Open in Recent. Now the page keeps the folder of the last file it opened or saved (`sawhorse.lastFolder` in the webview's storage, beside the units setting, so it lasts between starts) and names it to the shell before each dialog; Save As names the folder of the model's own file ahead of it. The shell's new `dialog_folder` command answers, on Linux, with the first of those, then Documents, then home, that is a folder the user can list now, and the dialog is given it: Open, Import and Plugins > Load Unpacked Folder as their folder, Save, Save As and the exports in front of the file name. One folder serves every dialog, a plugin's own file dialogs and Plugins > Install from .zip included. A file dropped on the window or one the app was started with counts as opened; a plugin folder picked with Load Unpacked Folder does not move the folder kept, since a plugin's folder is not where the models are.
- The rule is one function, `choose` in `sawhorse-dialog-folder`, a new plain crate under `src-tauri` with tests of its own; the command in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` only hands it the page's folders, the two system folders and the check on the disk, and has no branch per system. A folder is taken only when its path is absolute with no `..`, is Unicode, and can be listed (`read_dir`): one that is gone, is a file, or is closed to the user is passed over.
- What does not change. Windows and macOS: the command answers nothing there and looks at nothing, and the dialogs get exactly the options they got before (the page still asks, and with no answer gives Save the bare `Untitled.shz` and Open no folder: `src/io/folder.test.ts`, and `e2e/dialog-folder.spec.ts` with the bridge stood in for as on Windows and macOS). The browser build: it has no shell and keeps no folder. The capabilities: no permission is added, and nothing is added to the fs scope; the dialog plugin adds only the path picked, as before. The folder is kept under its own key and goes nowhere else: not into a model file, an export, the unsaved work kept for Restore unsaved work?, a plugin, or a log line. No file-format change.
- What the page can learn from the shell that it could not before: of up to four absolute paths it names, the first that is a folder this user can list, else Documents or home. It cannot learn what is in a folder, or whether a file is there.
- A dialog is never held up by this. The check runs on a blocking thread of its own (`spawn_blocking`), not on the main thread or the async runtime's workers, and only one runs at a time: while one has not come back (a network mount that does not answer), the next dialog's question is answered nothing at once, so such a folder holds one thread and no more. The page waits at most a second and a half for the answer and then opens the dialog with no folder, as before; a shell without the command or storage that cannot be read means the same. The command writes and keeps nothing, so an answer that comes late is dropped and leaves nothing behind.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the check of the disk ran inside the async runtime's task (`#[tauri::command(async)]` on a plain function), so each check stuck on a mount that does not answer held one of the runtime's few worker threads for as long as the mount did, and enough of them would stall every other command, the dialogs' own included; it now runs on a blocking thread, one at a time (`OneAtATime` in `sawhorse-dialog-folder`). The Linux switch moved into the crate as `ON_LINUX`, held by a test and at compile time, so a build for Windows or macOS with the switch on does not compile. An end-to-end test of a shell that answers 2 s late was added, and tests were tightened where a planted fault passed them: the limit of four, a cancelled Open keeping no folder when the shell answers another, and nothing logged when the shell lacks the command. The changelog and the user guide say what the slow case does.
- Tests: `src-tauri/dialog-folder` (16: the order, on Linux and off it; the Linux switch (`ON_LINUX`, the only thing the shell's command decides by) held to the platform the test runs on; a folder gone, a file, a path that is not whole or not Unicode; a folder closed to the user, on the real disk; the limits on what the page may name, four at most; one check at a time, a check that does not come back holding its turn and a second question answered at once without looking), run in CI's `tauri-check`; `src/io/folder.test.ts` (33: what is kept and named, what each dialog is given, the wait given up on, the plugin folder picker, the one storage key, the bytes saved being the bytes given, the browser build); `e2e/dialog-folder.spec.ts` (10, the page as it runs in the shell with the bridge stood in for: Open, Save As, the first Save, a folder gone, a shell 2 s late given up on with the late answer changing nothing, Load Unpacked Folder, Windows and macOS unchanged, a shell without the command, and with autosave on that neither the saved file's entries, nor anything in IndexedDB, any other storage key or the console holds the folder). Docs: the user guide's Saving section, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tried by hand by akvalentine977 on 2026-11-20, on the .deb, .rpm and AppImage built from the pull request, each from a first start: GNOME 46 (Ubuntu 24.04), 48.7 (Debian 13), 50.1 (Ubuntu 26.04) and 51.1 (Fedora 45), Cinnamon 6.6.4 (Linux Mint 22.3), and KDE Plasma 6.8.2 (openSUSE Tumbleweed, and CachyOS with the AppImage started from a desktop launcher). On all seven: the first Open starts in Documents, not Recent; the first Save starts in Documents with `Untitled.shz`, on the AppImage too; after a save into Downloads, Open starts in Downloads, and still does after the app is quit and started again; Export > STL starts in the folder of the model just opened; Save As starts in the model's folder with the model's name; Plugins > Load Unpacked Folder starts in the folder kept. Then on all seven, with the model in Downloads and an export saved into Documents: Save As still starts in Downloads, and Load Unpacked Folder and Open start in Documents. Not tried by hand: Windows and macOS (the tests show the dialogs' options are unchanged there; the shell was not built or run on either), Import, a file dropped on the window or given at the start, and a folder that is gone or closed to the user. The command as it landed (the check on a blocking thread, one at a time) was not tried by hand: it differs from the pull request he built.

### Fixed (lane 81, 2026-11-20)

- **A boolean of two solids that touch face to face where they meet is built instead of refused** ([#124](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/124)). `Object::combine` refused, as not a valid solid, the intersection of a side and a shelf 1 mm too thick for its dado with the shelf's end on the dado's floor (side first, `EdgeThroughFace`; both ways, and the difference too, once both were turned 0.83 rad about a skew axis), a shelf cut to its dado's size pushed 0.5 or 1 mm into the dado's floor (both ways, square to the axes), and two boards lying on each other, turned and moved 1 km (`FacesOverlap` with `NonPositiveVolume(0.0)`). The engine's answer had the right volume: it resolves coplanar faces by symbolic perturbation, and where two faces touch it can keep both over the contact area, facing opposite ways, a sheet of no thickness hinged to the true result, which the validator refuses as faces overlapping or an edge through a face. `boolean::combine` now cancels such sheets: on each plane where faces facing opposite ways overlap in area (the validator's own face-on-face test), each facing keeps what it covers and the other does not, on the faces' own corners; a ring left running out and back along a line loses that spike, and a corner a neighbouring edge needs goes back in. It only changes a result the validator refuses, so a result that was accepted is the same. A result that is nothing but such sheets is empty: boards that only touch, and boards less than the merge distance into each other whose faces were welded together. Corners the overlay cannot match to the faces' own leave the result as it was. When the result with the sheets cancelled does not build or validate, the result without the step is built instead and its answer given (the engine runs once; `boolean::Combined::build`, used by `Object::combine`, push/pull and Follow Me): a boolean the step cannot save is refused with exactly the words it had before, and one it saves is unchanged. Now these answer, the same both ways, square to the axes, turned about x, turned about the skew axis, and moved about 50 m and 1 km: the shelf too thick for its dado (60 mm3) and the one 1 mm off the floor (50 mm3), with their differences and unions; a shelf 0.011 to 1 mm too thick for a 19 mm dado; the shelf 0.5 or 1 mm into the dado's floor (2,850 and 5,700 mm3) and the differences; boards that only touch (empty, also 5 km out) and boards 0.005 mm into each other (empty). No file-format, model hash, golden or engine change; `npm run boolean:diff` agrees on its 26 cases.
- Still refused, each pinned by an ignored test with what it should answer: boards exactly the merge distance (0.01 mm) into each other, turned away from the axes (square to them, the overlap is empty): the engine's result is welded at the corners that round to the merge distance and not at those that round past it, which needs a decision on what an overlap of exactly that depth is; and the shelf 0.5 mm into the dado's floor, turned 0.83 rad about the skew axis and moved by (40, -25, 12) m: the engine's result is right, but the weld that closes slivers joins a corner of the dado's floor with a point the engine made about 2.6 um from it and keeps that point, so a face is 1.4 um off its plane; keeping the corner instead changes which point every weld keeps, which is for a lane that can show what that does to every result that welds today. Seen while testing and not changed, as before this lane: some turned results come out a few micrometres off (turned 0.83 rad about the skew axis and moved about 1 km, a shelf 0.02 mm too thick reads 34.35 of 36 mm3 side first and is refused shelf first), and some unions and differences of turned parts are still refused: over 3,000 random turns and moves up to 1 km of two 600 x 300 x 19 mm boards, the union of boards lying on each other was refused in 488 (their difference in none), and of boards overlapping 0.05 to 5 mm and slid, the difference in 27 and the union in 42.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/touching_booleans.rs` (the issue's steps with the volumes worked out by hand, both orders, in five placements: the shelf too thick for its dado and 1 mm off the floor, every operation; the shelf 0.011, 0.02, 0.1 and 1 mm too thick for the wide dado; the shelf 0.5 and 1 mm into the floor, also moved 40 m along x and along z, intersections and differences; boards that only touch, also 5 km out; boards 0.005 mm into each other; a property test of boards lying on each other, slid, turned about any axis and moved up to 1 km, empty both ways; a refusal the step cannot save, a U profile less a box square to the axes and an L profile less a box moved 40 m, refused as without the step; eleven fixed pairs from a seeded sweep of boxes, L and U profiles and polygons, their volume, faces and vertices and the boolean's own result pinned, chosen so that taking out or changing the corner check, where the step runs (after the plane snapping), running it once, the tolerance it groups faces by, putting corners back, healing, the area under which a piece is dropped, or the plane a piece keeps fails at least one row; and the two ignored tests above). Four of those rows are refused today though taking out a guard makes them answer with the right volume: those guards cost answers. Each runs on the code before this change and fails there, refused. `crates/mcp/tests/measure.rs`: the shelf too thick for its dado now answers both ways and gives no reason when turned; a shelf 1 mm into the wide dado's floor, turned 0.83 rad and moved by (40, -25, 12) m, still refused side first, keeps the other order's answer tested, checked against the kernel. `check_clearance` still tries the other order. Docs: `docs/MCP.md` (the cases seen), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Added (lane 80, 2026-11-20)

- **The MCP server measures how two parts sit: `check_clearance` and `measure`** ([#122](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/122), from @aramshaw; [#14](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/14)). Each takes two parts as items from `summary` (`{"object": id}` or `{"node": id}`; a group or component instance stands for every solid in it, at any depth, where it sits). `check_clearance` answers `gap_mm`, the shortest distance between their surfaces, worked out exactly over their triangles (an edge through a face, edge to edge, corner to face), so a turned part reads right, and 0 when they touch or overlap, a part wholly inside another included; the closest points; `overlap_mm3` and the overlap's box along the world axes, from the kernel's boolean intersection on copies; and `seated`, the gap and that box's thinnest side both within `POINT_MERGE` (0.01 mm, from `tolerance.rs`, with `LENGTH_EPS` for rounding). For parts square to the axes, an overlap about the merge distance deep or less is one the kernel's intersection does not keep, so it reads 0 and seated. Where the kernel cannot work out the intersection either way round (cases seen include boards turned away from the axes, exactly 0.01 mm into each other, and a shelf cut to its dado's size pushed 0.5 mm into the dado's floor, turned 0.83 rad about a skew axis and moved by (40, -25, 12) m; since lane 81 boards touching face to face read empty, and the other shelves in dados tried answer), the overlap and `seated` are `null` with `overlap_unknown` giving the first such pair's reason, from the first order, while the gap still answers. A solid inside a group, named by its own item, answers with its own name. `measure` lists each pair of parallel flat faces (within the kernel's `PARALLEL_EPS`), one from each part, facing each other with some area in common (up to the merge distance past each other listed at 0), nearest first, at most 50 and how many more: the distance along the normal and the faces (object and persistent face id), so two sides of a shelf give the inside width, and a shelf short of its dado's floor gives how far. Hidden parts are measured and the answer says which. The same item twice, one item inside the other, an item that is not there and one that holds no solid are refused plainly. Past 20,000,000 triangle pairs a call is refused before any work: 4,000,000 pairs with no box check skipping any took 0.45 s (release build in WSL, 2026-11-20). `check_clearance` is also refused before any work when its intersections would take in more than 1,000,000 triangles: two groups of 372 overlapping boards, under the pair limit, took 11.6 s. The slowest accepted calls found (release build in WSL, nothing else running) took 3.5 s and 6.9 s when the intersections built (998,784 and 985,608 triangles), and 9.4 s when they could not be built either way round (998,784 triangles). Both only read: no undo step, the same model hash, the same saved file. Answers give lengths to the nanometre. No kernel, file format, model hash or golden change.
- What the review changed from the pull request: the intersection is tried in the other order before giving up (a shelf whose end sits on its dado's floor read "not known" one way round and right the other); the limit on the intersections' triangles above; `measure`'s test for parallel faces is `PARALLEL_EPS` from `tolerance.rs` in place of a number of its own (which let faces 0.002 degrees apart through, where the distance between them moves by more than the nanometres it is given to); the docs say what `seated` does not tell (a shelf touching both walls of its dado but short of the floor is seated), that a curved part is measured to its facets, that a group's overlap is added up pair by pair, and where the kernel's intersection was seen to fail. That failure is the kernel's to fix, filed on its own.
- Tests: `crates/mcp/tests/measure.rs` (18: the 11 of the pull request, the read-only one also with refused calls, a call where the intersection fails and the saved file's bytes, and five from the review: groups two deep, a group's overlap added up and a component copy turned a quarter; a shelf in a dado, fitted, thinner, short of the floor and too thick, and one the other order answers; both limits on either side, refused before any work; `measure` with a face turned 0.001 degrees and with 60 pairs facing; a group holding only an imported mesh or only a sketch refused; the pair limit at exactly 20,000,000 and one disc side over, and what the intersection limit counts: solids within 0.01 mm of each other's boxes counted, solids further apart not; which pair and order `overlap_unknown` names, checked against the kernel on the saved file; and `measure` with faces 0.01 and 0.0101 mm past each other), `crates/mcp/src/measure.rs` (2, and the ignored timing `pair_cost`); all fail on the code before this change, which has neither tool. And the worked example in `docs/MCP.md` now measures the shelf's sides and checks a shelf is seated (`the_worked_example_in_the_docs_runs`). Docs: `docs/MCP.md` (the tools, what a group does, the costs, the worked example; out of Not yet), `README.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 79, 2026-11-20)

- **The MCP server's first save through a link the file server follows is refused, and leaves nothing of the model outside the folder** ([#121](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/121), from @aramshaw; [#118](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/118)). `place` in `crates/mcp/src/paths.rs` resolves the folder a new file goes in and refuses one outside the server's folder. On a Samba share seen from Windows, a link made on the server to another folder of the share resolves as the folder asked for, and only a file in it shows where it really is, so `save_model` wrote `out/Escaped.shz` outside the folder while `open_model` and a second save refused the same path. Now `save_model` resolves the temporary file it wrote (`written_inside`), and when that is outside the folder removes it and refuses with the words a second save gives ("it leads out of the server's folder"), before anything is read back or moved. A refusal whose temporary file could not be removed says so. The check is made once, after the write: another program changing a link between it and the move is not covered (as `docs/MCP.md` says of every check). The save never removes a file it did not make, so where a share's client holds a stale view of a folder and says a temporary file's name is taken while the server makes that file, an empty file of that name can stay outside. Server only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tried by the contributor on a Samba share from Windows 11 with a GNU cross-build of `main` and of the pull request, the link made on the file server: on `main` the first save through it landed outside; on the pull request the first and second saves were refused with nothing left outside, on the mapped drive and on the UNC path, and saves inside the folder gave `main`'s model hash. The empty file above is what he saw when the client's view was stale. Not tried: the share with CI's MSVC build, and a real MCP client against the change.
- Tests: `crates/mcp/src/paths.rs` (a written file inside passes; one outside, and one in a folder whose name only starts with the folder's name, are refused with those words; one that is not there is refused) and `crates/mcp/src/tools.rs` (a save into a folder whose files resolve outside, with the place made by hand since a symbolic link on Unix and a junction on Windows resolve as a folder and `place` refuses them first: refused with nothing left outside, and with a file already there outside refused leaving that file as it was; the same save inside goes through). It runs on Unix and, through a junction, on Windows. Both fail on the code before this change. Docs: `docs/MCP.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

## [0.1.0-alpha.7] - 2026-11-19

A pre-release, [v0.1.0-alpha.7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.7); every change since alpha.6: [v0.1.0-alpha.6...v0.1.0-alpha.7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/compare/v0.1.0-alpha.6...v0.1.0-alpha.7). The release notes: [docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.7.md](docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.7.md).

### Release (the alpha.7 preparation, 2026-11-19)

- Version 0.1.0-alpha.7 in npm, Tauri (MSI version 0.1.0.7), both Cargo workspaces and their locks (only the members' own version lines), the six plain crates under `src-tauri` (`logout-hold` among them), the `.shz` generator examples, the site's download links and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s list of the release's files; the release notes in `docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.7.md` (the glue-up note on the cut list row and the Minimum strip setting, #56, #67, #57 and #70, first; the Nesting fields, #89, #90, #92, #95, #93, #96, #98, #101, #102, #100, #106 and #88; Scale, Move and Follow Me after Undo, Delete and Hide, #76, #82, #85, #105, #111, #114, #86, #104, #81 and #83; big sketches and many curves, #11, #61, #64, #87 and #65; unsaved work kept in up to five sets, #94; the cabinet generator's back, #60; the MCP server, #14 and #63; the logout dialog on GNOME and Cinnamon, #73 and #119; the smaller fixes, #9, #97 and #66; not yet, with a minimum strip for one part, #57, and the MCP server in the installers, #14; the known limits, with the GNOME countdown, #73, a saved model offered back on a Mac, #103, a double-clicked file behind the offer, #115, edges Undo removed staying selected, #110, and the MCP server's loose solids and network share, #116 and #118; thanks by GitHub login). The README, the site and `docs/SIGNING.md` point at them. In this section, the entries for #88, #63, #64, #61 and #87 and lane 68's now say what is true at the release: the status line's 3.18 mm, the MCP server built and run on Windows and macOS by CI, moving many curves no longer slow, the #87 timing test ignored for the count, and P1LOTRX credited without the links that no longer answer.
- Docs and site for alpha.7: the site's cut list and nesting line says the narrowest strip of a glue-up can be set, and its offline line that work not saved is kept as you go and offered back at the next start; `docs/LIMITS.md` says it is current for alpha.7 (with lanes 46, 49, 60 and 64); `docs/MCP.md` records what @aramshaw reported on [#14](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/14), a CI-built Windows binary run from a real MCP client and from a folder on a network share, and the one gap he found there, [#118](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/118); `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` points the test build's Mac walkthrough at the alpha.7 notes and says @akvalentine977 opened the Linux files of test build run 43123831440 on seven desktops ([#73](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/73)).

### Fixed (lane 78, 2026-11-19)

- **On GNOME and Cinnamon, logging out, restarting or shutting down with unsaved changes names Sawhorse in the desktop's own dialog** ([#119](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/119), from @akvalentine977; [#73](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/73)). KDE Plasma closes each window when the session ends, so the question the window asks before it closes showed there already. GNOME and Cinnamon end the session without closing the windows first, so nothing was asked (the work came back at the next start through Restore unsaved work?). Both expect an app with unsaved work to hold a logout inhibitor with the session manager. Now, on Linux, the shell takes one when the page says the model is unsaved and gives it back when the page says it is saved or the window is going, the words `close_guard` already hears: `Inhibit` on `org.gnome.SessionManager`, the name gnome-session and cinnamon-session both own, with the reason "This model has unsaved changes" and the flag for logging out only (not suspend or idle). While it is held, the Log Out, Restart and Power Off dialogs list Sawhorse with that reason; after a save they do not. The call goes to the session manager itself and not through GTK's `gtk_application_inhibit`: the Linux window is made with no GTK application id, and with none the contributor measured GTK's call registering an inhibitor with an empty app name (and a GLib warning), which GNOME's dialog cannot list. The name sent is `Sawhorse`, the `productName` in `tauri.conf.json`, since GNOME lists an inhibitor only when it can find the app. What this does not do: GNOME still logs out when its dialog's 60-second countdown ends (seen by the contributor on GNOME 48 with a script holding the same inhibitor), so the dialog is a warning and a chance to cancel, not a block. A desktop with no such session manager (KDE Plasma: the name is not on the session bus) is asked once and holds nothing. Windows and macOS are not touched. The calls are made on a thread of their own, so the window never waits on the session manager, and the session manager drops an inhibitor when the connection that took it goes, so none is left when Sawhorse ends. `Inhibit` and `Uninhibit` are waited on for as long as the session manager takes, and only the bus's own answer to whether a session manager is there is given up on after two seconds: an `Inhibit` given up on could still be granted afterwards, with a cookie the shell never heard, and that inhibitor would then stay until Sawhorse ends, saved or not. No file-format change.
- Tried by hand by akvalentine977 on 2026-11-19, on the .deb and .rpm of the pull request: GNOME 46 (Ubuntu 24.04), 48.7 (Debian 13), 50.1 (Ubuntu 26.04) and 51.1 (Fedora 45), and Cinnamon 6.6.4 (Linux Mint 22.3). Unsaved, Log Out and Power Off or Restart name Sawhorse; saved, they do not (on Cinnamon, Log Out with nothing unsaved went through with no dialog). KDE Plasma 6.8.2 (openSUSE Tumbleweed, and CachyOS with the AppImage): as before, the desktop's dialog first and then Sawhorse's own question. Not tried: the AppImage on GNOME, which installs no desktop file, so GNOME may not list it. Those builds gave each call up after two seconds; the change to waiting on `Inhibit` and `Uninhibit` was not tried on a desktop.
- Tests: `src-tauri/logout-hold` (`sawhorse-logout-hold`, 11 tests, the shell's memory of the inhibitor as a state machine with no bus in it: the first unsaved takes and the save releases the same cookie; unsaved again while held takes no second one; saved with nothing held releases nothing; each unsaved spell takes its own; a desktop with no session manager is asked once; a failed call holds nothing and is tried again; every sequence six steps deep with every outcome of each take; the app name is `productName` in `tauri.conf.json`; "unsaved" takes the hold and "saved" and "close" give it back; every word `close-guard` parses has a rule here; the flags hold logging out only). The calls on the session bus have no automated test: they need a running session manager. CI: `ci.yml` runs the new crate's tests in `tauri-check`. Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` (the release checklist bumps `src-tauri/logout-hold` with the other shell crates).

### Changed (lane 75, tests only, 2026-11-19)

- **The property test that extrude then push back restores the sketch draws its sizes on a 1 um grid, so it no longer fails now and then on a shape that came back right** ([#113](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/113), from @aramshaw; [#112](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/112)). No change to what the kernel does: the same models, hashes and file format. `extrude_then_push_back_restores_the_sketch` in `crates/kernel/tests/invariants.rs` extrudes a rectangle, pushes the cap back flat and checks the model hashes exactly as it did with the sketch alone. The extrude takes the rectangle out of the sketch, so the push back draws it again from the solid's world points, and that round trip carries float noise of up to about 2e-14 m. The model hash rounds the sketch's plane-local positions to 1 nm, so a size within that noise of a half-nanometre hashed differently after the round trip with the shape right: CI found one on #108, a 1 x 667.931 mm rectangle 49.86 m from the origin whose top corners came back 1.8e-15 m higher. The test now takes its sizes as whole micrometres from 1 mm to 10 m, either sign (`grid_size`), which sit at least 0.499998 nm from the nearest rounding boundary, and keeps the exact hash comparison; the origin and the normal stay arbitrary, and `size()` is unchanged for the other tests. The input CI found is kept as a plain test, `extrude_then_push_back_gives_the_corners_back`, that holds what the kernel does guarantee for it, with no hash pinned: the solid collapses, one region comes back, every corner comes back within 1 pm, and the region extrudes again. The same test runs a second rectangle on a skew plane about 84 m from the origin, with sizes about 0.027 nm from the nearest multiple of 0.1 nm, so returned points snapped to a 0.5 nm or a 0.1 nm grid, which CI's input and the grid sizes let through, fail it. Measured with a harness outside the repository (release builds in WSL): the old sizes hashed differently 8 times in 3,000,000 random cases on one seed and 18 times in 10,000,000 on another, once in about 375,000 and 556,000 cases, or once in about 1,500 and 2,200 runs of the test's 256; the grid sizes never did, in the same counts, and in a scratch run outside the repository the test itself passed 2,000,000 cases.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/invariants.rs`. Docs: `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 76, 2026-11-19)

- **The Outliner's Delete, Explode Curve, Make Group, Make Component, Explode and the steps that hide something tell the active tool the model changed, so Scale's box and grips go with what they remove or hide** ([#114](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/114), from @aramshaw; [#111](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/111)). Since #105, Delete, Backspace and Edit > Delete tell the active tool what Undo tells it. The other steps that remove or hide the selected thing did not: with Scale the tool and a board selected, the Outliner's Delete erased the board and left the dashed box and its 26 grips over the empty model, and Edit > Hide, the Outliner's eye, Hide tag in the Tags pane, putting the board on a hidden tag and a scene that hides the board emptied the selection and left the grips round the hidden board, where a factor typed next scaled the hidden board. Explode Curve left them round a circle's edges once the circle left the selection. After Make Group or Make Component the box stayed on the solid inside the new group and a factor typed was refused as inside a group that is not open; after Explode it was refused as a group that no longer exists; Close Group / Component emptied the selection and left the grips. Now the Outliner's Delete, Explode Curve, Make Group, Make Component and Explode, once the kernel takes them, call what Undo and Delete call: the box and grips go, or move to what is selected now, a factor typed next asks for something to scale or scales the new selection, and the measurements box shows the tool's default. The Outliner's eye, Edit > Hide, Hide tag and Show tag, putting the selection on a tag, a scene tab and Close Group / Component from the Edit menu do the same only when they hid a solid, a group or component or a section plane that was drawn, or took something out of the selection. Showing things, or a scene that only moves the camera, leaves a step half done alone, as before: a line started, a rectangle's first corner, a tape's first point, a move picked up, a push or a scale under way all go on after the click. As with Delete, a step half done when something is removed or hidden is dropped, and Follow Me lets its path go. Not changed: a plugin's batch, since a plugin's own tool applies its steps through it and telling the tool there would clear what that tool draws mid-gesture (a plugin command that deletes the selection while Scale is the tool still leaves the box); Delete Imported Meshes and "Make parts from loose faces", which change only imported meshes, which cannot be selected; Lock, after which the grips stay on the locked solid and a factor typed is refused. Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tests: `e2e/scale.spec.ts` (the issue's three ways, the Outliner's Delete, Edit > Hide and the Outliner's eye, with Show leaving the board unselected and its box on hover; Hide tag; the board put on a hidden tag; a scene that hides the board; Explode Curve on a selected circle; the eye hiding a board whose box showed on hover with nothing selected; Make Group then Explode; Make Component; Close Group / Component; a camera-only scene clicked with a line started, the line finished as one step; a move under way through the Outliner's Show and a tag shown). The first eleven fail on the code before this change; the last two pass there and fail when every step through these callers tells the tool. `src/lib/scope.test.ts` (`hidSomething`: what a step hid, a group holding only sketch geometry hidden among it, and that showing things or a camera-only scene hid nothing). Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Changed (lane 77, CI only, 2026-11-19)

- **Every job in `ci.yml`, `pages.yml` and `desktop-test-build.yml`, and the Playwright install step, stops after a set time**. No change to what the app does. On a pull request on 2026-11-19 the web job's "install Playwright Chromium" step stalled in `apt-get update` on the runner; the run's page shows the job cancelled after six hours, GitHub's limit for a job, with that step cancelled and the end-to-end step skipped. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` set no time limit. Now the `rust` job is stopped after 40 minutes, `mcp` after 45 on each system, `web` after 60 and `tauri-check` after 60 (its own apt step has passed after 37 minutes). In the web job, "install Playwright Chromium" fails after 20 minutes, `install wasm-pack` after 5 and `npm ci` after 10. The Playwright install usually takes under a minute, but with the mirror slow the slowest green one in either workflow took about 11 minutes, so 20 is under twice that. A step that runs out of time is not tried again. Every job in `pages.yml` and `desktop-test-build.yml` has a limit too, and the site build's Playwright install fails after 20 minutes. `release.yml` and `nightly-perf.yml`'s job for a missing runner are unchanged and still have no limit.

### Changed (lane 74, docs only, 2026-11-18)

- **The setup docs name the Node version and the WSL distro the build needs** ([#108](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/108), from @aramshaw). The README said Node 24+, and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` said Node 24, but jsdom 30.1.2, the page Vitest's front-end tests run in, and three of its dependencies (`@asamuzakjp/css-color`, `@asamuzakjp/dom-selector`, `w3c-xmlserializer`) declare `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26.0.0` in their `engines` (`package-lock.json`), so an earlier 24 is outside what they support. No other package in the lockfile asks for more on Node 24. Both docs now say 24.15 or later. On an earlier 24, `npm ci` warns (`EBADENGINE`) and goes on, since the repository does not set `engine-strict`. `scripts/cargo.sh` and `npm run wasm` (`scripts/build-wasm.mjs`) forward to WSL with `wsl.exe` and no distro named, so they run in whatever the default distro is, and fail where that distro has no Rust toolchain; the one-time WSL setup in `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` now says to make Ubuntu the default first, by the name `wsl --list` shows. Docs only: no code, kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Docs: `README.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 73, 2026-11-18)

- **A Nesting setting changed right after Undo or Redo keeps what Undo or Redo did** ([#106](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/106), from @aramshaw; [#100](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/100)). The Nesting section works the nesting out again 200 ms after a change, and its fields and settings came from that last nesting. A setting changed inside those 200 ms after Undo or Redo was stored with the others as the last nesting had them: set Kerf and Trim to 3.1 mm, Undo (Trim back to 0), then type 3.15 in Kerf and press Enter, and Trim came back as 3.1 mm. A length typed back after Undo was compared with the old reading too, so Enter on 3.1 after Undo took a 3.1 mm kerf back stored nothing. Now the fields show, and a change is laid over, the settings stored now, from the state the app reads after every change (`DocState.nest_settings`); Undo and Redo put the fields back at once, before the diagrams catch up. The line under the diagrams names the settings of the nesting drawn, so while the nesting is worked out again it is dimmed with the diagrams. The Saw, Kerf, Trim, Joint loss and Minimum strip (clearing it too) all go through it. Read only: no op, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tests: `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (a change laid over the stored settings, not the last nesting, in a length field, the Saw, Joint loss and clearing Minimum strip; a typed length compared with the stored value; the stored values shown at once; the controls still waiting for a first nesting; the line under the diagrams dimmed while the nesting is worked out again; the last nesting's used when nothing stored is given), `crates/wasm-api` (`the_state_holds_the_nest_settings_stored_now`: the stored settings in the state, written as the nesting writes them, through Undo, Redo and Built-in stock) and `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (the issue's two ways and a Redo, with the pane's refresh held by the page's clock). Each fails on the code before this change, except the case with nothing stored and the controls waiting for a first nesting, which pin behaviour that stays. Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/adr/0007-nesting.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 72, 2026-11-18)

- **Delete tells the active tool the model changed, so Scale's box and grips go with the solid** ([#105](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/105), from @socrabs151; [#85](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/85)). With Scale the tool and a board selected, Delete, Backspace or Edit > Delete erased the board and left the dashed box and its 26 grips over nothing. The grips still lit up under the cursor, a press on one was refused with "that solid no longer exists", and a factor typed next began with an Undo, which took the Delete back and scaled the board. Now Delete tells the active tool what Undo tells it: the box and grips go at once, a factor typed next asks for something to scale, and the measurements box shows the tool's default. Undo of the Delete brings the board back unselected, so its box shows when the cursor is over it. The same goes for every tool: a step half done when Delete erases the selection is dropped, as Undo drops it (a line started, a move picked up, a push under way, a tape's first point), and a click starts again. Follow Me lets go of a path Delete erased, so a click on a profile asks for the path instead of sweeping along the erased edge. Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tests: `e2e/scale.spec.ts` (the issue's steps, with a press, move and click where a grip was changing nothing; Edit > Delete and Undo; a factor typed after Delete; Delete in the middle of a scale); `e2e/follow.spec.ts` (the steps of #86 with Delete in place of Ctrl+Z). All five fail on the code before this change. Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 71, 2026-11-18)

- **Follow Me lets its path go when the model changes under it** ([#104](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/104), from @socrabs151; [#86](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/86)). Follow Me reads its path from the selection when W is pressed and keeps it as points. After Undo, Redo, File > New, Close, Open, Edit > Restore Unsaved Work, or an empty SketchUp file brought in by File > Import, File > Open or a drop (it opens in place of the model) with Follow Me still the tool, a click on a profile swept it along the path as it was: with a region's edge as the path and the rectangle that made it undone, the box's front face was swept round the rectangle that was gone and added to the box. Now the tool lets the path go when the model changes under it. The blue path leaves the view, the status line keeps what Undo or the other command wrote, and a click on a profile changes nothing and asks for the path; pressing W again reads it from the selection as it is then. A selection that still names sketch edges Undo removed is read as a path again by W, and a click on a profile then sweeps along those edges ([#110](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/110)). Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tests: `src/tools/follow.test.ts` (the path dropped after the model changed, a face's edge and a chain of edges, the overlay empty, no status written by the drop, the click after it refused with the same words as with nothing selected and no op sent, W again reading the path); `e2e/follow.spec.ts` (the issue's steps: the click after Ctrl+Z sweeps nothing, the model hash and the redo step stay). The unit test of the drop and the end-to-end test fail on the code before this change. Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Changed (lane 70, tests only, 2026-11-18)

- **The test that a big sketch Move grows with the selection counts work instead of timing it** ([#11](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/11)). No change to what the kernel does: the same models, hashes and file format. Since lane 60 `crates/kernel/tests/move_redraw.rs` timed a Move and a Copy of everything at two sizes and failed when four times the rings took more than twelve times as long. That is a wall-clock comparison, and on a loaded machine it failed now and then for reasons that had nothing to do with the code. Now the default run counts the vertices, edges and curve points the sketch's searches look at during each op, a number that is the same on every run and machine, and fails when four times the rings cost more than six times the work. It covers a Move, a Copy and an Array of 3, of circles and of polylines, drawn one ring at a time and imported. The counts grow about 4.1 times; with the kernel before lane 60 they grow about 16 times and every case fails. The count is kept per thread in the kernel and read through `testing::sketch_visits`, a hidden test hook. The wall-clock test is ignored now, beside the timing table, and still runs by hand in a release build.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/move_redraw.rs`.

### Fixed (lane 69, 2026-11-18)

- **A refused Nesting length loses its error mark when a new reading replaces it** ([#102](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/102), from @socrabs151; [#101](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/101)). In the Kerf, Trim, Joint loss and Minimum strip fields, a refused length stays as typed, marked in red with its reason in the tooltip. When the field had been left and a new reading of the stored length then replaced the text (Redo, or a change of units), the red mark and the reason stayed beside a length that is fine, until a length was next committed in that field. Now the mark and the reason go with the text they belong to. As before, a refused length keeps both while it is still in the focused field, through a new reading too, and while the stored length reads the same. Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tests: `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (the issue's steps; a refused length that was left giving way to a new reading with no mark or reason in all four fields, typed as a length that is not one or one out of range, in millimetres, fractions and decimal inches; a refused length in the focused field keeping its mark and reason through a new reading, refused by Enter or by the pause; a change of units with the field focused and after it was left; a refused Minimum strip giving way to auto, and keeping its mark through the same reading). The tests of a refused length that was left fail on the code before this change. Docs: `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 68, 2026-11-18)

- **A Nesting length field leaves the text being typed alone when its reading changes** (from P1LOTRX; [#98](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/98)). In the Kerf, Trim, Joint loss and Minimum strip fields, a new reading of the stored length that arrived while a person was typing replaced their text. The pause stored `3.1` typed in a millimetre document and turned the field into `3.1 mm`, so the next key gave `3.1 mm5` and an error. The nesting the pane works out again about 200 ms after Undo or Redo put the reading over text typed in that time, so Enter stored nothing new. Now text typed in a focused field stays until Enter, leaving the field or Escape. Enter and leaving the field store it and then show it with its unit; Escape gives it up for the reading stored now, even one that arrived while the person typed. A length that is refused stays as typed with its reason through a new reading too. A focused field with nothing typed in it, or typed back to the reading it showed, follows a new reading as before, and so does a field the focus has left. The pause stores what it stored before, at the same moment. Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change.
- Tests: `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (the pause and the next keys, a late refresh then Enter in all four fields, leaving the field and Escape after a reading arrived, a focused field with nothing typed or typed back to its reading, a refused length kept through a new reading, a reading from elsewhere after the pause, typing again before a commit's own refresh, and a field the focus left without a blur); `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (a late refresh held by the page's clock until a length is typed, then Enter and Escape, and the pause and more keys in millimetres, fractions and decimal inches; the #93 test now expects a 4 typed over 5 mm to stay as typed after the pause until the field is left). Docs: `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 60, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-17)

- **A Move, Copy or Array of a big sketch selection grows with the selection, not its square** ([#87](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/87), from @aramshaw; [#11](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/11), from boardfoot's split and scratch change there). A move takes out every edge touching a moving vertex and draws it again with the insertion rules; with everything selected that is every edge, and each was drawn with a scan of every vertex and edge, so a typed Move of 400 circles of 48 segments took about 3.6 s from Enter to the "Moved" line in a headless Edge on the development machine. Now the edges are drawn through a lookup, as a long stroke has been since #65, when there are enough of them for one to pay, and the edges still there after the repair are kept through one set of vertex pairs; a selection of polylines named edge by edge finds its points through one grid of the vertices and checks its edges against one set, not a scan each. The same for a Copy or an Array's segments, and a copy's curves are made together, as an import's are since #61, instead of one at a time with every curve in the sketch settled for each. Measured the same way: a Move of 400 circles of 48 segments about 0.22 s, the same rings as polylines about 0.27 s where they took about 4.9 s, 400 circles of 24 segments about 0.12 s where they took about 1.0 s; natively, a Copy of 200 circles of 48 segments about 0.12 s where it took about 3.7 s, and an Array of three of 100 about 0.12 s where it took about 4.5 s. Four times as many rings now cost about seven times as long, where they cost about fifteen to sixteen times: the square term of the redraw is gone, though what is left still grows a little faster than the count (doubling the rings costs about 2.2 to 3 times). What a move, copy or array leaves is the same, ids and bits included: the model hash after each of 24 such ops (Move, Copy and Array of circles and polylines of 24 and 48 segments) is the one `main` gives. A shape lifted off its plane still makes its curves one at a time. No golden, model hash or file format change. Docs: ADR-0012 and ADR-0013 (dated notes), `docs/LIMITS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/src/sketch.rs` `lookup_tests` (a move's edges and a copy's segments drawn through the lookup leave exactly the sketch the scans leave, keys and bits included, with the same regions and curve pieces, over hostile sketches with circles, every vertex or one in two to six moved, shifted by the merge distance, a hair past it or a rectangle's width, turned or scaled; the move test fails with the lookup built before the vertices move; the selection's points found through one grid as `vertex_at` finds them, at, either side of the merge distance and on a tie; the new edges kept after the repair, given either way round beside pairs with no edge, the same through the set for over 64 as through the scans, which fails with the set's pair taken as given); `crates/kernel/tests/move_redraw.rs` (a Move of everything, of circles and of polylines named edge by edge, and a Copy of circles: four times the rings costing at most twelve times as long, the fastest of three runs and of up to nine while not within the bound; about fifteen to sixteen times on `main`, which fails it every time, about 7 times now; since lane 70 that timing test is ignored, and the default run counts the work instead, four times the rings at most six times the work; ignored, release only, the Move, Copy and Array timings and model hashes above); `crates/kernel/tests/curves.rs` (an array of two of a circle, an arc and a polygon numbers its six new curves copy by copy in the selection's order, each on its own copy; fails with a copy's curves handed over in another order); `crates/kernel/tests/sketch_move.rs` (a rectangle moved beside another leaves its edges in the slots drawing them again in the sketch's order gives; fails with the taken edges drawn in another order).

### Fixed (lane 64, 2026-11-17)

- **Restore unsaved work? never hides newer work, and Restore never loses other work** ([#94](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/94)). An offer left unanswered stayed the offer at every later start, so work drawn after it and left unsaved was never offered, and Restore of the old offer wrote the old work over it about 300 ms later, for good. An edit while the offer showed replaced its line and nothing said it was still set aside, Save, Open and New never cleared it, so an old offer could outlive several saved sessions, and Edit > Restore Unsaved Work after drawing replaced the drawing without a word. Now every piece of unsaved work is kept set aside until it is restored or discarded. A start offers the newest first and says how many older sets are set aside too ("1 older set of unsaved work is set aside too."); Discard offers the next at once, and after Restore the rest stay set aside, on Edit > Restore Unsaved Work one at a time, newest first, and offered again at the next start. Restore sets the model it replaces aside first when it holds unsaved changes, behind the others, and says how many presses of the menu come before it, leaving out sets the menu cannot restore ("The model you had open is set aside as unsaved work: Edit > Restore Unsaved Work brings it back after 1 other set."; at the next start all are offered by time again), asks the discard question when that model is too large to keep, and gives an untitled model, so Save never writes restored work over an open file. The restored work is kept as this session's work at once, and a reload at any point during Restore finds each piece of work once. While work is set aside and its offer is not on the status line, the line ends with "Unsaved work set aside. Click to restore or discard.", and the click shows the offer again. At most 5 sets are kept, each at most 64 MB as before: a start that would keep a sixth deletes the oldest and says so ("At most 5 sets of unsaved work are kept: the oldest (...) was deleted."). Two sets with exactly the same log are kept as one. With several windows open one model can still be set aside more than once, at different stages. A file the desktop app was started with still waits for the answer: each Discard offers the next set and the file opens after the last, and Restore offers the file beside the work, as before. What older builds kept is read as it is, nothing lost. Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change. Docs: `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, `docs/LIMITS.md`.
- Tests: `src/lib/autosave.test.ts` (on an in-memory stand-in for IndexedDB: the issue's three starts, Restore, Restore over this session's work, setting the open model aside as a move, an exact copy kept once and counted once toward the cap, Save, Open and New, the four layouts older builds left, the cap, logs with no work and records that cannot be read, storage that fails, ids and order); `src/components/ValueBar.test.tsx` (the reminder); `e2e/offers.spec.ts` (twelve tests: the issue's three starts, Discard, edits while an offer waits, Save, New and Open, Restore from the Edit menu after drawing, the older builds' layouts, the cap, `?autosave=0`, Restore with another set waiting, the count leaving out sets that cannot be restored, a Restore the kernel refuses, and the restored work in the store before any autosave runs); `e2e/startup.spec.ts` (two sets and a start-up file); `e2e/containment.spec.ts` (a box drawn while an unreadable record is offered is offered first at the next start). Eleven of the twelve new end-to-end tests fail on the code before this change; the `?autosave=0` test passes there too. The last one also fails when Restore leaves the restored work to the next autosave instead of keeping it at once.

### Changed (lane 62, CI only, 2026-11-17)

- **The MCP server binary CI builds on Windows and macOS is kept for a week** ([#14](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/14)). No change to what the app or the server does. On a push to main, the `mcp` job uploads the release binary it has just run, as the artifact `sawhorse-mcp-Windows` or `sawhorse-mcp-macOS` on the run's page, for seven days. It is unsigned and it is not a release: the server is still not in the installers. It is there so the server can be tried with a real MCP client on a machine that cannot build it.

### Changed (lane 59, tests only, 2026-11-16)

- **Where the app tells the active tool the model changed is pinned by tests** ([#82](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/82); [#76](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/76)). No change to what the app does. A check of #82 planted faults in `src/App.tsx` and found six no test caught: the call after Redo, after Open, after opening an empty model and after File > Close taken out, the live selection cleared after the call on Open instead of before, and the tool's `reset` no longer called after Undo. Tests: `e2e/scale.spec.ts` (a 24 x 4 x 2 in board stretched to 36 in, undone and redone: 26 grips and the +red face grip hot at 36 in; Scale active over the board, then File > Close: no grips over the empty model; a 12 x 12 x 6 in box saved, then a board whose solid has the box's number selected with Scale active and the box's file opened: no grips, and a typed `2` says to select something and leaves the box as it was); `e2e/draw.spec.ts` (a 12 in line drawn and undone while the chain waits at its end: a length typed then draws nothing and says where to click); `e2e/private-fixtures.spec.ts` (Scale active over the board, then the private empty SketchUp file opened: no grips over the empty model). The private-fixtures test runs only where the private files are, so CI does not run it. Each fails with its fault planted: the Redo test finds no grip at 36 in, the Close, Open and empty-file tests find 26 grips, and the line test reads the 6 in line it drew.

### Changed (lane 58, a pull request from @akvalentine977, 2026-11-16)

- **Scale's status line says which key is held and which is tapped** ([#83](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/83), from @akvalentine977; [#81](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/81)). It read "Shift: uniform. Ctrl: about the centre.", the same form for both, though Shift applies only while it is held and Ctrl switches on a tap and stays on until the next. It now reads "Hold Shift: uniform. Tap Ctrl: switch between the opposite grip and the centre.", when the tool is picked and while the pointer is over a grip. The guide's tool table and its Scale paragraph say the same. Nothing about what the keys do has changed.
- Tests: `src/tools/scale.test.ts` (the line on activating the tool, and the line over a face grip).

### Fixed (lane 58, a pull request from @akvalentine977, 2026-11-16)

- **After Undo or Redo, Scale's box and grips go back round the shape, and a value typed then in Scale, Move, Rotate or Offset no longer undoes a further step** ([#82](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/82), from @akvalentine977; [#76](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/76)). With Scale the active tool, Ctrl+Z after a scale put the shape back and left the dashed box and its grips where the scaled shape had been, so the grips that showed were not where a press was taken. A value typed at that point was worse: the tool still took the undone scale for the last step and began, as it does when a scale is typed again, with an Undo of its own. That took the step before the scale (the Push/Pull that made the board, in the test) and the scale was then refused with "that solid no longer exists". Move, Rotate and Offset did the same with a distance or an angle typed after Undo. File > New, Close and Open with Scale active left the old model's box drawn over the new one, and it stayed there. Now the app tells the active tool when the model changes other than by the tool's own step (`Tool.modelChanged`, called after `reset` on Undo, Redo, New, Close, Open and Edit > Restore Unsaved Work). Scale works its box out again from the selection as it is and forgets the scale it would have redone, so a value typed after Undo or Redo scales the selection about its centre, as it does when no grip has been picked. Move, Rotate and Offset forget the step they would have redone, so a value typed then says what to do first and changes nothing. New, Close, Open and Restore Unsaved Work now also drop a gesture in progress in whatever tool is active, as Undo already did; keeping the model when asked about unsaved changes leaves the gesture as it was.
- Tests: `src/tools/scale.test.ts` (after a scale and the app's `reset` and `modelChanged`, the 26 grips and the dashed box are at the solid's bounds as the host now gives them; a typed `2` sends one `transform` about the centre and no `undo`; with nothing selected and no solids there are no grips and no overlay); `src/tools/move.test.ts`, `rotate.test.ts` and `offset.test.ts` (a value typed after `reset` and `modelChanged` sends nothing); `e2e/scale.spec.ts` (a 24 x 4 x 2 in board stretched to 36 in and undone: 26 grips, the +red face grip hot at 24 in, and none left after File > New; a typed `2` after the Undo leaves one watertight solid from (-12, -2, -1) to (36, 6, 3) in; a board stretched while unsaved work is offered, then Edit > Restore Unsaved Work: no grips, and a typed `2` leaves the restored 24 x 16 x 12 in box as it was); `e2e/move.spec.ts`, `e2e/rotate.spec.ts` and `e2e/offset.spec.ts` (a distance or angle typed after Undo leaves the box or board as it was). Each end-to-end test fails without the change: the first finds no grip at 24 in, the restore test finds 26 grips, and the others read "that solid no longer exists".

### Changed (lane 56, tests only, 2026-11-16)

- **The Minimum strip setting's choice of stock, its top bound and a set 0 are pinned by tests** ([#57](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/57)). No change to what the app does. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (a 12 1/16 in part on 6 in and 4 1/2 in boards glued from the 6 in board with the setting absent and from the 4 1/2 in board with it set to 0, where the 6 in board would leave a 1/16 in last strip, narrower than the kerf); `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (0.3048 m and the double under it taken and the double above it refused, each typed in metres; a set 0 shown as "0 in" and "0 mm", not empty, and cleared back to auto).

### Added (lane 55, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-16)

- **A Minimum strip setting for glue-ups** ([#57](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/57)). Since #56 a glue-up's last strip narrower than 1 1/2 in, or a quarter of the board when that is less, is evened out with the full strip before it. What is acceptable depends on whether the joint shows, so the minimum is now a document setting beside Kerf, Trim and Joint loss in the Nesting section. The field is empty, reading "auto", until set, and the #56 rule holds. Set, a last strip narrower than the setting, or than half the board when that is less, is evened out, so the two equal strips are never under it; on a narrow board the setting holds where the rule's quarter-board cap would have dropped it. 0 never evens out: the last strip has only to clear the kerf and 1 mm, as before #56, so a part auto would glue can be left on no stock. Clearing the field (Enter, or leaving it; a pause on an empty field does nothing) goes back to auto. The setting is an undo step, is saved with the file as `settings.minStrip` (0 to 12 in, 0.3048 m, so 304.8 mm and 1 ft are taken) only when set, and is hashed only when set, so every file, hash and golden from before is unchanged. The nesting's footer names it beside the joint loss when something is glued up. The per-part override in #57 waits, as agreed there. Docs: `docs/format.md`, ADR-0007 (dated notes), `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, `docs/CLEANROOM.md`.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (absent by default and in an older file; saved only when set; 0 and 12 in taken, below 0, past 12 in, NaN and infinity refused, and a refusal through the op changing nothing; the model's hash as before until set and again once cleared, through undo and redo, and 2 in and 3 in hashing apart; saved and opened with the same hash and nesting; on 6 in boards the rule's 1 1/2 in last strip kept, a set 2 in evening it out, a set 4 in held at half the board either side of 3 in; on 2 in boards a set 3/4 in where the rule's quarter cap gives 1/2 in; 0 keeping a 1/4 in last strip and refusing one narrower than the kerf the rule would have evened out; the property tests with the setting absent, 0, 12 in or any in range, every glue-up checked against it; the range's end and the half-board cap to the last bit, and a last strip of exactly half the board kept), the fuzzer's nest settings setting it a third of the time; `crates/shz` (a document with it set and then cleared saving byte for byte as one that never had it, with the built-in library and with a changed kerf, and the same nesting hash before saving and after opening); `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (the field empty with "auto" until set, a typed length sent, past 12 in refused, a set value shown, cleared sent without it, 0 sent as 0; a pause on the emptied field clearing nothing, leaving it clearing it; millimetres in a metric document, and 304.8 mm, 1 ft and 12 in all taken); `src/nesting/exports.test.ts` (the footer naming it beside the joint loss only when something is glued up); `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (a 24 1/4 in top: 1/4 in typed, 12 + 12 + 1/2 in and the footer and status line naming it; Undo, Redo, and the field cleared back to auto and 12 + 6 1/4 + 6 1/4 in).

### Fixed (lane 54, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-16)

- **A second rectangle started level with a corner the cursor rested on stays on the ground** ([#69](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/69), from @aramshaw; [#9](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/9)). With a rectangle on the ground, a second one started on the grid or an edge could stand up on a vertical plane such as x = 16 in. Since #23 the far corner skips the blue line through the first corner while the rectangle's plane is open, but not the blue line through a corner the cursor had rested on (a "from point" anchor). When the second rectangle's first corner shared that corner's x or y, the far corner snapped onto that line above the ground, and the upright plane through the first corner held it. Now the directions a rectangle skips go for the anchors' lines too, while its plane is open. Red and green alignments from an anchor stay, an arrow-key lock keeps every line as before, and a rectangle still stands up when its far corner is on a real point, edge or face off the ground. No kernel, model hash, golden or file format change. Docs: `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tests: `crates/inference/tests/infer.rs` (the plane open, the first corner at the origin and an anchor at (0, 1, 0): the far corner 2 px from the anchor's blue line half a metre up is a from point there without the skip, on the anchor's red line on the ground with blue skipped, and on the start's blue line with a blue lock); `e2e/draw.spec.ts` (in the iso view, a 16 x 10 in rectangle, the cursor resting on its corner at (16, 10) in, a second rectangle from about (16, -6) in, taken on the green line through that corner, with its far corner 2 px beside the blue line through that corner, 8 in up, drawn on the ground). Both fail on the code before this change.

### Fixed (lane 53, 2026-11-15)

- **An extrude of exactly 0.01 mm is refused as too short, never as a kernel bug** ([#66](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/66)). The shortest push/pull is 0.01 mm, the distance under which two points are one. An extrude of exactly that made walls exactly that tall, which the kernel's own check refuses, so it was rolled back as "kernel bug, operation rolled back: ... VertexOnFace" on the ground, as four zero-length edges 500 mm up (the corners, once placed, rounded a hair under 0.01 mm apart), and went through 1000 mm up (they rounded a hair over). The same rounding reached a few doubles over the minimum. Now an extrude of 0.01 mm or less is refused on every plane with "an extrude must be more than 0.01 mm" (after the check that the shape stays within 10 km, as before). A push/pull, extrude or offset up to 1 nm over the minimum that fails the way the minimum makes things fail (the kernel's check finding zero-length edges or a corner on a face, push/pull's degenerate result, an offset's loop meeting or leaving the face's own edges), while the same op at 0.02 mm would go through, is refused with "too short here: at this distance two corners, or a corner and a face, would be 0.01 mm or less apart; use a longer distance" (an offset: "too short here: the offset would come 0.01 mm or less from the edges it starts from; use a longer distance"), alone, as an op of a batch, or in a plugin's or the MCP server's batch. Before, a push/pull with Ctrl said "push/pull through the solid: the result has degenerate geometry" and an inward offset that the path crossed the face's boundary or had to run strictly inside it. Under the minimum a push/pull still says "distance must be at least 0.01 mm" and an offset "offset distance must be at least 0.01 mm": a plain push/pull or an outward offset of exactly 0.01 mm goes through as before. Any other failure at the minimum keeps its own message (and a kernel bug of another kind stays one), and everything from just over 1 nm over the minimum is unchanged. A loop drawn on a face that would leave two corners under 0.01 mm apart is refused like one that comes within 0.01 mm of another face, instead of reaching the kernel's check. Measured over six planes (level both ways, upright, two sloped, a diagonal), 0 to 5 km out along their normal, a 0.4 x 0.1 m rectangle and an 8 km one, both ways: the rounding reaches 1.4e-12 m over the minimum; nothing ends as a kernel bug, the WASM kernel the same. Inside a batch each such op's own result is checked at once, so the kernel's check refusing it is judged there too; a failure the check finds only after later ops of the batch is not traced back to one op and stays a kernel bug (none was seen), and so does a refusal that mixes the minimum's violations with any other.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/min_distance.rs` (the issue's cases on the ground, 500 mm and 1000 mm up, both ways, refused with nothing changed, and 0.0100001 and 0.02 mm extruding by exactly that distance; the document's history kept on the refusal; a plain push/pull of exactly 0.01 mm still sliding the face; a sweep of the six planes at six heights, two rectangles, both ways and 76 distances from two doubles under the minimum to just past the band, through extrude, push/pull, push/pull with Ctrl and offsets of a face and of a region, with never a kernel bug, only the one refusal each op may give at each distance, and everything past the band going through; a property test over any plane within 9 km and any rectangle; each refusal's words true where it is shown; a Ctrl push/pull and an inward offset at the minimum refused the same way as the first and the last op of a raw batch and in a plugin's batch, nothing left behind; other failures at the minimum keeping their own message, alone and in a batch; a shape beyond 10 km saying so at exactly the minimum; an op whose failure at the minimum is the minimum's kind but which fails at 0.02 mm too keeping its own error, alone and in a batch; an extrude within the band judged by the same rule as every other op, alone and in a batch, its own result checked at once), `model::tests::only_the_minimum_s_own_failures_are_relabelled`, the unit test of an extrude's refusals.
