Without the fix the issue's cases come back as the kernel bug, and the sweep and the property test fail; without the batch's own retry the batch test fails with "the result has degenerate geometry".
- **A long stroke goes in in time that grows with its length, and one certain to break a sketch's edge limit is not drawn first** ([#65](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/65)). Each new segment of a stroke was checked against every point and every edge already in the sketch (to snap its ends, to split it at points it passes and to find the edges it crosses), so a stroke cost its length squared, and one over the sketch's 20,000 edges held the kernel for seconds before it was refused. Now a long stroke looks those up in a grid of the sketch's points and edges and checks the few nearby, with the same tests on every point and edge that can pass them, so the sketch it leaves is the same, point for point and edge for edge. The grid is used only where it pays: for strokes of 48 segments or more (a 24-segment circle always scans), with a cell the size of the stroke's segments, of the sketch's edges, or between, whichever is estimated cheapest, and only when that estimate (entering the sketch's points and edges, and each segment's queries with what they find in nine cells of the box the sketch and the stroke cover) is at most half what the scans cost, decided in one pass over the sketch's edges and the stroke before anything is built, and only within a ceiling of four entries per point, edge and segment (at most about 250 bytes each). A grid that turns out crowded, out of room, or answering most queries with most of the sketch is dropped on the way and the scans finish. And a stroke that crosses, touches and folds over nothing, itself included, adds exactly one edge a segment: when it is the last op of its edit and would take its sketch past the limit, it is counted instead of drawn, and the edit is refused with the error the limit check gives with it drawn (the first sketch over the limit, in the check's order, with its count: "a sketch would hold 23999 edges; at most 20000"). Every other stroke is drawn and then checked as before, so the strokes accepted, and the errors of those refused, are exactly those before. A file can already hold a sketch past the limit (opening it does not check this one); every edit to it is refused for that sketch, as before. The issue's zigzag sent as one batch, before and after, on the WASM kernel in Node: 8,000 points 1.21 s and 0.10 s, 20,000 points 8.43 s and 0.18 s (drawn); 24,000 points 7.17 s and 0.03 s, 32,000 points 11.59 s and 0.04 s (refused); native release, best of three: 0.47 s and 0.022 s, 2.92 s and 0.062 s; 4.17 s and 0.014 s, 7.52 s and 0.021 s. Beside 19,000 parallel lines 1 m long (native release, best of three): drawing the lines 4.53 s and 0.87 s, a stroke of 1,000 segments of 0.5 mm 0.91 s and 0.57 s, of 5,000 segments 2.77 s and 1.24 s; the process peaked up to 2.3 MB higher (16 MB and 18 MB), and 4.6 MB higher (16.9 MB against 12.3 MB) beside 2,000 lines 0.2 m long with a 5,000-segment stroke, and the WASM kernel's memory 1.5 to 2 MB higher; a 20,000-point stroke takes about 4 MB more of the WASM kernel's memory while it goes in (15.1 MB against 11.1 MB), and a refused one less. Drawing circles is as fast as before: one more 8 or 24-segment circle into a sketch of 100 to 400 circles took 0.99 to 1.02 times as long, the 64-segment one 0.81 to 0.84, and 400 circles drawn one at a time in the WASM kernel 11.08 s against 11.42 s. One shape is slower: beside 250 lines 0.52 m long, 3 mm apart, a stroke of about 200 to 250 segments of 0.5 mm crossing them, diagonal to them or closed over them took 5 to 12% longer, a few tenths of a millisecond (closed, 256 segments: 4.15 ms against 3.71 ms; across, 256 segments: 3.95 ms against 3.76 ms); there strokes of 128 segments or fewer were level, and of 512 segments 7 to 15% faster (a stroke of 512 segments 5 cm clear of them, 3.86 ms against 6.32 ms). Elsewhere, sweeps of strokes of 16 to 2,048 segments beside, across, diagonal to, closed over and inside sketches of none to 38,400 points and edges found no other shape slower (beside them, nothing of 0.5 ms or more over 1.03 times as long), strokes of 48 to 128 segments as fast or up to 2.2 times faster and of 512 to 2,048 segments up to 14 times faster; and a stroke crossing itself thousands of times (an import of 4,650 points ending with 145,088 edges) as long as before. `sawhorse-mcp` keeps its own limit of 20,000 points a batch.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/stroke_limit.rs` (the issue's zigzag of 24,000, 32,000 and 200,000 points refused with its count and nothing changed, as a draw, as the last op of a batch and as a polyline in the sketch's frame, each within a generous 120 s on another thread, which fails before the fix (the 200,000-point stroke would take hours); a stroke past the limit that is not the last op of its batch drawn and then erased, allowed; a stroke past the limit with a point off the sketch's plane refused for that point; a batch drawing past the limit into two sketches, either way round, refused for the first sketch the check comes to; another limit broken earlier in the batch reported as with the stroke drawn; and, ignored, release only: 20,001 points making exactly 20,000 edges drawn, 20,002 refused with 20,001, and the refusal after drawing equal to the early one), `model::tests::a_file_already_over_the_sketch_limit`, `model::tests::a_stroke_of_exactly_the_limit_is_drawn` (a stroke to exactly 20,000 edges drawn, not counted, in the default run), `model::tests::the_redo_with_the_stroke_drawn` (a test hook leaves out a stroke under the limit to reach the path, otherwise unreachable, that does the edit again with the stroke drawn: the same model as drawing it), `sketch::sure_count_tests` (the strokes the count is certain for and not; a property test that a certain count is the count inserting gives, on random and on a fixed seed, both answers coming up often), `sketch::lookup_tests` (random hostile strokes, revisiting, folding back and running along edges at distances either side of the merge distance, into sketches with rectangles, a 40 m diagonal and an earlier stroke, at the origin and 9 km out, through the grid and through the scans, one stroke at a time and as an import, leaving the same vertices and edges under the same keys, the same regions and curves and the same new edges), `sketch::lookup_border_tests` (the same comparison where a grid is easiest to get wrong: steps of one to a few merge distances, so the cell is its smallest, every point on a cell border, a rounding or a fraction of the merge distance off it, beside short and long edges on the same borders and with strokes running along a long edge under the merge distance away, on a fixed seed in every run; planted faults in the grid, a dropped neighbour column, a cell under the merge distance, edges looked up without their neighbour ring, fail it), `sketch::lookup_cost_tests` (2,000 lines of 1 m beside a stroke of 0.5 mm segments, where a grid of the stroke's cells would need 8 million entries: held under the ceiling throughout, the same sketch; a grid crowded half way through a stroke, and one forced out of room part way, dropped with the same sketch as the scans; a query along a 10 km segment scanning instead of taking a cell per sample), `sketch::lookup_decision_tests` (beside 400 circles a stroke of 47 segments scans and one of 48 or 512 builds a grid; beside 250 lines 0.52 m long a stroke of 128 segments scans and one of 2,048 builds one; a grid whose queries find most of the sketch dropped with the same sketch; a segment's sample count held within a 32-bit `usize`, as on wasm32). Checked as well against the kernel before this change: 6,000 random cases (strokes, polylines and imports of up to 4,650 points, beside rectangles and a 40 m diagonal, at the origin and 9 km out) and 76 ops shaped like two reviews' (thousands of long lines then a short-segment stroke beside, across and along them; 250 lines 0.52 m long then a zigzag of 16 to 2,048 segments beside or across them; batches past the limit into two sketches) gave the same outcomes and model hashes on both.

### Added (lane 52, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-15)

- **A glued part's cut list row says what it is glued from, and a board counts it once** ([#67](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/67), from @aramshaw; [#56](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/56) point 4). Since #53 the Nesting section lists the glue-ups, but the cut list row of a glued part had no note, and a board holding both strips of one panel said "2 parts" while the totals said "1 of 1 part placed". Now, while the Nesting section is open, a glued row's notes say "glued from 2 (4/4 x 12 x 96)", naming the board from the stock library as the Nesting section does, in a metric document too. The note comes only from a nesting worked out for the model as it is shown, with the same Visible only as the list, and only for the row whose parts were glued; after a change it is gone until the nesting is worked out again. The CSV, the printed cut list and the labels leave it out, so they read the same whether Nesting is open or not. Each board's count of parts counts a glued part once, with its strips: "1 part in 2 strips", or "2 parts in 3 pieces" with a whole part beside them; a board with no strips reads as before. A part whose strips are too long to share a board is named on each board that holds some of them ("1 strip of 4a", the tag the glue-up list and the diagrams use), not counted as a part there, so no part is counted on two boards and each counts once in the totals. The count is the same in the pane's caption and diagram label, the booklet's Stock pieces list and each SVG's header. In the booklet a board with strips whose count runs into its yield gives the yield a line of its own, kept on the page with its title (so such a row can move to the next page), and a count naming the strips of several parts wraps after its commas rather than being cut short; a booklet with no glue-up is the same as before, byte for byte. Front end only: no kernel, model hash, golden or file format change. Docs: ADR-0007 (dated note), `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tests: `src/nesting/glue-note.test.ts` (the note for copies glued alike, some glued, and glued from different boards; none without a nesting, with no glue-ups, for another row, or for a glue-up whose part is not one of the row's; the count with no strips, strips only, strips of two parts, strips beside a whole part, and a part whose strips are split across boards, as the booklet and the SVG write it; each of sixty booklet rows keeping its yield on its page, with the list starting at fourteen heights; a row with no strips whose count ends a point before its yield kept on one line, as before; a long count wrapped after its commas, whole, with the yield under it); `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (the row's note with the nesting there and gone, the other rows without it; a board's caption and diagram label); `src/nesting/nesting.test.ts` (on the real kernel, each glue-up's row is the cut list's row at all three roundings, on the `cutlist-mixed` golden, whose 16 in top reads "glued from 2 (4/4 x 12 x 96)", and on a 15 in panel whose hidden rail drops out with Visible only, moving the panel's row up one); `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (a 15 in panel's row noted only while Nesting is open, its board's caption, diagram label and SVG saying "1 part in 2 strips"; three panels, one hidden, whose note, recorded at every change of the page, never reads "3 glued from 2" as Visible only is ticked, nor the board from before as the panel's stock is set and undone, which fails with either guard in `App.tsx` taken out; the 15 in panel's cut list CSV, PDF, labels and printed report the same bytes with Nesting closed and open with the note showing, and no note in the CSV, which fails when the exports are made to carry the note). Random cut lists through the kernel's nesting were also compared with the notes and counts their placements make, and random nestings with no glue-up with the files they gave before.

### Fixed (lane 51, 2026-11-15)

- **A sheet or board the parts fill reads 0.00 waste, not -0.00.** With no kerf and no trim, parts that fill their stock exactly (nine 16 x 32 in parts on a 4 x 8 sheet, three 4 x 32 in parts on a 4/4 x 4 x 96 board) have areas that, added up, came to a hair more than the stock's by rounding alone, so the Nesting panel, the SVG of each piece and the PDF booklet said "100.0% used · -0.00 sq ft waste" and the yield tile "-0.00 sq ft waste", and the nesting's numbers had a waste a little below zero and an efficiency a little over 1. Now the kernel reports such a piece, its group and the totals as exactly full: no waste and an efficiency of 1, and the words say "0.00 sq ft waste". Only a sum over its stock by no more than rounding can make is brought back, so parts truly larger than their stock still show; every layout, placement, cut and nesting hash is as before, and stock the parts do not fill reads the same numbers to the bit. An area that rounds to zero at two decimals is also never written with a minus, in the nesting and in the cut list.
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (the filled sheet and board, a positive zero waste and an efficiency of exactly 1 for each piece, group and the totals, and their plain sums over the stock, so the case is the one rounding put over; ten parts each a whole 5 x 5 sheet, whose sheets added one by one came to more than ten times one sheet, filling the group and the totals exactly; a part half a nanometre larger than its sheet each way still below zero and over 1; the bookshelf, the plywood carcass, a part-filled sheet and the rails reading as before to the bit), `src/nesting/exports.test.ts` (an area that rounds to zero written as 0.00 in square feet and square metres, one of -0.01 or less keeping its minus, and the piece's line and the yield tile for the filled sheet's old and new numbers); the filled-stock tests fail on the code before this change. Two more guard the code as it is: `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (an absolute path, `/probe.shz` and real ones outside the folder, refused by `open_model` and `save_model` with the message that says it is absolute, `new_model` refusing a path, nothing made or changed in or outside the folder; spaces around a path trimmed, the save, the open and the answer all `a.shz`; each fails on Linux with its guard in `paths.rs` taken out, where before only the Windows run did) and `src/tools/gather.test.ts` (points exactly half way up their cell, near -3.4e6 m where a cell's number is quantised to quarters, gathered as the scan does, which fails with the lookup's side taken at `<= 0.5`).

### Changed (lane 50, 2026-11-14)

- **The MCP server's folder rule and its save are now tried on Windows and macOS on every change** ([#14](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/14)). `sawhorse-mcp` reads and writes only `.shz` files under the folder it was started with, and until now that had been shown on Linux only. A new CI job, `mcp`, lints the server and runs all its tests on Windows and macOS, then builds the release binary and runs it once on a fresh folder (a board added, saved and its cut list read over stdin and stdout), failing unless every answer is the expected one and the saved file is the only one in the folder. `docs/MCP.md` says where it has been tried, that `file` on Windows is the `\\?\C:\...` form, and what those runs showed: a save over a read-only file is replaced on Linux and macOS and refused on Windows with the old file kept; on Windows a save over a file another program holds open is done when that program lets others delete it and refused otherwise, never leaving a half-written file or a temporary file; and a name differing only in case names the same file on Windows and macOS.
- Tests: `crates/mcp/tests/windows.rs`, on Windows only (drive, drive-relative, rooted, UNC, `\\?\` and `\\.\` paths and `..` with `\` or mixed separators refused, with nothing changed outside the folder, beside it or where such a path would land; streams, device names in lower, upper and mixed case and names ending in a dot refused, with no stream made; junctions, folder links and file links out of the folder refused, a junction to such a junction too, and those whose target is inside followed; a save over a hard link or a file link never writing through it; a start folder given as a junction, with a separator or a `.` at its end, or by its 8.3 short name; a path over 260 characters saved and opened; a save over a file another handle holds open, in each share mode, leaving the old file or the new one whole and no temporary file; `file` in the long resolved form), and in `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` on every system (requests ending in CRLF; a relative start folder and one ending in a separator; a save over a read-only file, the old file or the new one whole; a name differing only in case from a file that is there, one file after where the volume ignores case; a start folder through a link, on Unix, which on macOS is every test's temporary folder). Where what a system does is not certain, the test asserts what must hold and prints what happened. The shared helpers moved to `crates/mcp/tests/common/mod.rs`.

### Fixed (lane 49, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-14)

- **A Move, Rotate or Scale of a big sketch selection starts without waiting on gathering it** ([#64](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/64), from @aramshaw; [#11](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/11)). As a drag began, the tools gathered the selected sketch points, edges and moving points, each once, by checking every new one against all those gathered so far, so the time grew with the square of the selection. Now each is looked up in a grid of the ones gathered so far, which checks the few cells around it, so the gather grows with the selection. Measured on the development machine in a headless Edge, with everything selected: the time from the press to the first preview of a Move is about 0.15 s for 1,000 rectangles (4,000 edges) where it was about 0.28 s, about 0.3 to 0.4 s for 2,000 rectangles where it was about 0.8 s, and about 0.29 s for 400 circles of 24 segments where it was about 3 s. Rotate is the same, and Scale's grips come up in about 0.07, 0.13 and 0.2 s where they took about 0.2, 0.57 and 2.5 s. In Node, 4,000 edges gather in about 16 ms where they took about 350 ms, and 8,000 in about 32 ms where they took about 1.4 s; the pull request measured the same on another machine. What is gathered is the same, in the same order, with the same point kept where two are near: the same 1 nm test, both ways round for an edge, and a point that is not finite, or too far out for a cell, checked against everything as before. A typed Move of 400 circles, and of the same rings as polylines, leaves the same model hash as before. A fan of many edges from one point still gathers slowly, a big drag still waits a moment as it starts for other reasons, and a Move of many curves was still slow once it was made, after the click or Enter that ends it (#11), until lane 60 ([#87](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/87)). Docs: ADR-0012 (dated note), `docs/LIMITS.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.
- Tests: `src/tools/gather.test.ts` (eight times the edges cost less than 24 times as much, the fastest of nine alternating runs, and of up to 27 while they are not within the bound: 8.5 to 9.3 times here alone, up to 13.1 beside full vitest runs and a cargo build, and 65 times on the scan it replaced, where it fails; over 500 seeded random selections, the same points, edges and moving points in the same order as the scan it replaced, written out in the test, with near misses either side of 1 nm, edges both ways round, two sketches and solids' edges left out; duplicates 1e300 m out and at the largest double gathered once, NaN and Infinity each time, as the scan did, which fails with the fallback for points without a cell taken out; points exactly 1 nm apart and one to three doubles either side, across zero, across multiples of a few nm and out to 1e15 m, as two points and as edges, gathered as the scan does, which fails with cells 2 nm wide where the other tests pass). Compared with the scan in `main` before this change, the gather agreed on 600,000 more random selections, on 2.16 million pairs of points a few ulps apart from 100 km to a billion km out, and on hand cases exactly 1 nm apart and a hair either side, on cell edges and in cell middles, at plus and minus zero and at subnormals.

### Added (lane 48, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-14)

- **`sawhorse-mcp`: an AI agent builds, reads and saves Sawhorse models over the Model Context Protocol** ([#63](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/63), from @aramshaw; [#14](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/14)). Until now an agent could read a saved file's cut list through `sawhorse-cli`, but not make or change a model without the app. `sawhorse-mcp <folder>` is a server on the same kernel that an agent starts and talks to over stdin and stdout (MCP revision 2025-11-25, JSON-RPC with `serde_json`, no new dependency). Its tools: `new_model`, `open_model` and `save_model`; `summary` (every part by name, with its size in mm to 0.1 mm, material, grain and ids); `add_board` (one named solid board from a size, a corner, the axes its length and thickness run along, and a material from the model or the app's library); `apply_ops` (the kernel ops a plugin batch sends, through the same checks); `cut_list` and `nest` (the same JSON as `sawhorse-cli`, with the keys in alphabetical order); `undo` and `redo`. Every change is one undo step, all or nothing, and answers with the cut list rows it added, removed and changed. Where two parts share a name the answer says so and picks neither. It reads and writes only `.shz` files under the folder it was started with: an absolute path, `..`, or a link out of the folder is refused, so is a name that would mean something else on Windows (a `:`, a `\`, a device name such as `CON` or `NUL`, a dot or a space at its end), and a save goes through the kernel's writer to a new temporary file beside the target, is read back and its model hash checked before it replaces the file, and the answer says where the file is on the machine. These rules have been tried on Linux; since lane 50 CI also builds, tests and runs the server on Windows and macOS. A message that is not JSON, too deeply nested, over 4 MiB or of the wrong shape gets an error and the server reads on, and a batch of more points than a sketch may hold edges is refused before any work. A new workspace crate, `crates/mcp`; built from a checkout like `sawhorse-cli`, not in the installers. The tools and a worked example: `docs/MCP.md`.
- Tests: `crates/mcp/tests/server.rs` (a bookshelf built with `add_board` and saved, whose cut list read back through `sawhorse-cli` is the one the server showed; boards on all six pairs of axes, in inches, and over a sketch facing the other way, which fails with the distance left unturned; one undo step per change and its cut list changes; a failing `apply_ops` and nine bad boards change nothing; shared names; paths out of the folder, by `..`, absolute or through a link, refused; malformed, deep and over-long input answered with errors; the binary end to end; the worked example in `docs/MCP.md` as written; the finds of a second check on the first version: a 20,001-point stroke refused in under a second, `..` refused inside the folder, a 65-character name refused as a name, unknown `cut_list` and `nest` arguments refused, 2025-03-26 answered in 2025-11-25; names that mean something else on Windows refused on every system while names close to them save; a file already at the temporary file's first name neither written over nor removed; a save over a hard link or a link to another file replacing it, never writing through it; from the pull request's own run with a real client, sizes in answers to 0.1 mm with no float noise, the saved file's place on the machine, and `apply_ops` pointing to `copy` and `array`). They fail without the crate. Two guards no input reaches have unit tests of their own in `crates/mcp/src`: a file that does not read back as the model, under the wrong hash or as bytes that are not a model, is never moved over the target, which keeps its old bytes; and a panic inside a tool comes back as a failed call, with the next call answered. Each fails when its guard is taken out.

### Added (lane 47, a request from @sandulucian-oss, 2026-11-14)

- **The cabinet generator's back takes the thickness you give it** ([#60](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/60), requested by @sandulucian-oss, with thanks). The back was always thin sheet, 6 mm in a metric document and 1/4 in otherwise, and the panel only had a box to leave it off, so a cabinet with a 16 or 18 mm back had to be drawn by hand. Now the panel has a **Back thickness** field right after **Stock**, in the document's units like the other sizes. It starts at 6 mm in a metric document and 1/4 in otherwise, so a 16 or 18 mm back is one number to type. The back is the same part in the same place, between the sides at the rear from the toe kick up, and the top, the bottom and the shelves are that much less deep: a 600 x 720 x 560 mm cabinet with an 18 mm back lists Back 18 x 564 x 620 and a top 542 deep. A back over 1/4 in is solid stock in the Cut list like the sides, so with the built-in stock library it nests on the same 4/4 boards as they do; give the parts a plywood stock and they go on the 18 mm Baltic birch sheets together. The field is greyed out while **Back panel** is off. A back of no thickness says "The back needs a thickness.", and one that leaves no depth says "The depth must be more than the stock and the back together (578)."; the button waits until it is fixed, as for the other sizes. The line under the button names the back's thickness ("Adds a cabinet: 600 x 720 x 560, 1 shelf, 18 back, 100 toe kick."). A cabinet made with the defaults is exactly the one made before, with the same parts and the same model hash. Until you type a thickness the back follows the document's units, 6 mm or 1/4 in, as it did; once typed it is kept with the other sizes for next time and stays that length when the units change. Sizes kept by an earlier version, without the field, start at the default; the document keeps only which cabinet the plugin made, as before, so a model saved now opens in 0.1.0-alpha.6 as it is, and that version's generator keeps the extra size and ignores it. Changing only the back and pressing **Update selected cabinet** replaces the cabinet, as one undo step. The sample is version 1.1.0. Docs: the plugin's README, `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`, `docs/PLUGINS.md` (the packed file's name), `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `src/plugins/cabinet.test.ts` (new: the plugin's own `main.js` run against a fake host with its batches applied to the WASM kernel; the defaults in mm and inches, a 30 x 72 x 12 in cabinet with three shelves, one without a back, one started in inches with the units then set to mm, and sizes kept by alpha.6 make the same model hash as on the commit before this change; 16 and 18 mm backs at the rear between the sides, with the top, bottom and shelf depths and the cut list's rows; a 1/2 in back in an inch document; the field's place, starting values and greying out; each message; kept and old sizes, and kept values that are not numbers; the units; Update as one undo step), `e2e/plugins-samples.spec.ts` (an 18 mm back typed in the panel, on the model, the Cut list and the boards, kept through a save and reopen; a 1/2 in back in an inch document, kept through a reload). For the unit test, `vite.config.ts` points the `"sawhorse"` module at the API's own shim in tests only.

### Fixed (lane 46, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-14)

- **Importing a file of many circles and arcs no longer slows with their square** ([#61](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/61), from @aramshaw; [#11](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/11)). An import made each of the file's curves on its own: it found each curve's points by checking every point of the sketch, then settled every curve in the sketch again, so a file of n curves took time in n squared. Now each curve's points are found through one grid of the sketch's points, and only the new curve is settled, against the edges the curves already there hold: the graph does not change while an import adds its curves, and settling the others again changed nothing. Measured on the development machine: 400 DXF circles import in about 3.9 s in a headless Edge, where they took about 7.5 s (the same rings as polylines: about 4.4 s); natively in the test profile 400 circles take 6.0 s where they took 9.6 s (6.1 s plain), 1,000 small circles with half of them crossed by a line 1.6 s where they took 5.6 s (1.6 s plain), and 5,000 three-segment circles in one sketch 4.4 s where they took 41 s (4.4 s plain). The pull request measured 8.1 s to 4.7 s and 5.1 s to 1.3 s for the first two on another machine. What an import leaves is what it left before, the same curves with the same ids and the same model hash, also where a curve splits, or drops a piece too short to be a curve and a later curve takes that edge: the 203 corpus `.skp` files and thirteen DXF, SVG and import files of circles, arcs and lines crossing, repeated and on many planes give the same model hash, curves, curve counters and notes as before, with their curves and as plain strokes, and in the app the same import report, counts and hash. A sketch whose curves would not settle to themselves, which no file the app writes holds but a hand-edited `.shz` can, takes an import's curves one at a time as before. Moving many curves was still slow (#11's Move half) until lane 60 ([#87](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/87)). No golden, corpus file, model hash or file format changes; there is no new setting. How: `Sketch::add_curves` in `crates/kernel/src/sketch.rs` (with `VertexGrid` beside it, a hash of cells twice the reach wide, and one curve's part of `settle_curves` as `settle_curve`), called from the import in `crates/kernel/src/model.rs`; a draw still uses `Sketch::add_curve`. Docs: ADR-0013 (a dated note, and a correction: a stroke that landed whole but off its circle becomes a polyline curve, it does not stay plain edges), `docs/LIMITS.md`, `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/import_curves.rs` (two imports, 72 curves apart on two planes and then 11 more crossing them, one repeated exactly, and a line across, leave the model hashes and notes the code before this change leaves; so do a square polygon split by spurs at two corners with an arc along that side, and a P-shaped polyline with a polyline along its tail, where an older curve drops a piece and a newer one takes its edge; 1,000 circles of 8 segments, every other one crossed, import as curves in no more than twice the time of the same strokes plain, the fastest of three alternating runs each, and of up to nine while the fastest so far are not within the bound: 0.98 to 1.03 times here in ten runs alone and at most 1.26 times in twelve beside a parallel release build, and 3.7 times on the code before this change, where the test fails); `sketch::tests` in `crates/kernel/src/sketch.rs` (over 1,000 seeded random imports, crowded and spread out, into sketches that may hold curves already, with circles, polygons, arcs of one segment and more, over another curve's chord too, P-shaped and doubled-back polylines, spurs, lines across, strokes repeated exactly or within the merge distance and centres a little off, `add_curves` leaves the same curves, ids, next id and sketch hash and returns the same ids as `add_curve` one at a time; curves split in 722 of them and stay plain edges in 764; the vertex grid finds what the scan finds, points at the reach, far out and not finite included). Lane 46 made the timing test take up to nine rounds, since beside a parallel build one round of three measured 1.75 times against its bound of 2, and added two tests: a sketch a hand-edited file left with a circle running whole through two junctions takes an import's curves one at a time and splits the circle as that did, and a point exactly as far from two vertices finds the one with the smaller id through the grid, as the scan does. Each of eleven deliberate breakages of the grid, `add_curves` and the import's count fails at least one of these tests. Compared with adding the curves one at a time, in the same build: every import of the curve property tests, 25,886 imports and 124,236 curves over 6,000 random sequences that draw, cross, erase, explode, offset, move, turn, scale, mirror, copy, extrude and push curves and import more into the sketch and into the document reopened from its snapshot, left the same curves, ids, counter and sketch hash; and no model any test reached held a sketch whose curves would not settle to themselves.

## [0.1.0-alpha.6] - 2026-11-14

A pre-release, [v0.1.0-alpha.6](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.6); every change since alpha.5: [v0.1.0-alpha.5...v0.1.0-alpha.6](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/compare/v0.1.0-alpha.5...v0.1.0-alpha.6). The release notes: [docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.6.md](docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.6.md).

### Release (the alpha.6 hygiene pass, 2026-11-14)

- Version 0.1.0-alpha.6 in npm, Tauri (MSI version 0.1.0.6), both Cargo workspaces and their locks (only the members' own version lines), the five plain crates under `src-tauri`, the `.shz` generator examples, the site's download links and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s list of the release's files; the release notes in `docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.6.md` (the cut list's glue-ups and shared boards, #49, #53, #62, #50 and #51, first; the desktop app's close question and File > Close and Exit, #44; the double-click on Windows and the title bar, #45; the selection after a move that splits or merges shapes, #7 and #55; the Move preview, #8 and #59; Control+click on a Mac, #40, #41 and #42, not yet reported from a real Mac; what alpha.5's ad-hoc signature did on three Apple Silicon Macs, #27; the Save As extension, #29 and #31, not yet reported from Linux; the smaller fixes; not yet, with cutting sizes, #54, joints, #25, the glue-up's cut list row, #56, its narrowest strip, #57, metric stock and starting values, #26 and #58, and the cabinet generator's back, #60; the known limits, with Cmd+Q on a Mac, Enter at the desktop app's questions, two windows' unsaved work and what a glue-up cannot do; the macOS walkthrough as #27 found it; thanks by GitHub login). The README, the site and `docs/SIGNING.md` point at them.
- Docs and site for alpha.6: the site's cut list and nesting line names the shared boards and the glue-ups, and its unsigned-builds note says what macOS shows on the first open since alpha.5 (the warning that Apple could not verify the app, then Open Anyway), where it spoke of a damaged app; the README's signed-installers line and `docs/SIGNING.md` say what alpha.5's ad-hoc signature did on three Apple Silicon Macs; `docs/LIMITS.md` says it is current for alpha.6 (with lanes 32, 37, 39, 41, 43 and 44); `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` points the test build's Mac walkthrough at the alpha.6 notes, and its release checklist says the preparation is gated and verified on a branch, then landed on `main` by fast-forward and tagged from `main`, and to read each cited issue's state before the notes call it open or closed.

### Changed (lane 45, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-14)

- **A glue-up comes from the board that needs the fewest joints, never leaves a thin last strip, and uses a longer board when the widest is too short** ([#62](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/62), from @aramshaw; [#56](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/56)). Since #49 a solid part wider than every board was glued from the board the rest of that wood used, whatever it cost: a 16 in top beside narrow oak parts on 4/4 x 4 was five strips, 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 1/2 in, four joints and a sliver. Its last strip was whatever width was left, down to the kerf. And a part longer than the widest board was reported too large, even when a longer, narrower board could glue it. Now the strips come from the board of that thickness that glues the part from the fewest strips; on a tie, the board the rest of that wood uses, then the board listed first. That top is 12 + 4 1/8 in from 4/4 x 12, one joint. A last strip narrower than 1 1/2 in, or than a quarter of the board when that is less, and the full strip before it become two equal strips: a 24 1/4 in top from 12 in boards is 12 + 6 1/4 + 6 1/4 in, not 12 + 12 + 1/2 in, and a 6 1/16 in panel on 6 in boards, refused before for its 1/16 in sliver, is 3 1/32 + 3 1/32 in. A board shorter than the part is never a candidate, so with 4/4 x 12 x 96 and 4/4 x 6 x 120 a 15 x 100 in top is 6 + 6 + 3 1/4 in from the longer board, and so is a 10 x 100 in part narrow enough for 4/4 x 12 but too long for it (6 + 4 1/8 in); a part is too large only when no board of its thickness can glue it and no board makes it whole. So a part narrow enough for the board the rest of its wood shares but longer than every board of its thickness now goes in one piece on a thicker board long enough, when the library has one, where #53 reported it too large. On a tie, "the board the rest of that wood uses" is the one board the parts of that wood and thickness that fit a board's width are nested on; parts on other boards (a stock that is set, another glue-up, a thicker board) do not count, and with no such part library order decides. A part whose stock is set, by the part or as its material's default, keeps that board, as before. The refusals stay, now on the narrowest strip: narrower than the kerf or 1 mm, or more than 100 strips. One visible effect of the tie rule: a 15 in panel with nothing else of its wood, which #53 glued 12 + 3 1/8 in from the widest board, is 8 + 7 1/8 in from 4/4 x 8, also one joint, since 4/4 x 8 is listed first. The `cutlist-mixed` golden's nesting hash moved, for that top, and no other hash: no model, cut list or file format change. How: `choose_stock` and `glue_widths` in `crates/kernel/src/nesting.rs`, with `glue_minimum` beside them; `testing::check_nesting_of` checks the new rule. Docs: the user guide's Glue-ups, ADR-0007 (a dated note), `DECISIONS.md`, `CLEANROOM.md`, `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (a 17 in side beside 5 in shelves is 12 + 5 1/8 in from 4/4 x 12, where #53 glued it 6 + 6 + 5 1/4; a 13 in part ties between 4/4 x 8 and 4/4 x 12 and goes with the rest of the oak, else to library order; 15 x 100 in on 4/4 x 6 x 120, and too large at 130 in; a 10 x 100 in part, alone and beside a 15 x 100 in one, glued from 4/4 x 6 x 120, a 5 x 100 in part made whole on it, and 10 x 130 in still too large on 4/4 x 12; a 6 1/16 in panel, the 6.003 in part #56 named, and 4.9 in on 4 in boards made equal, while 7 1/2 in on 6 in boards and 5.1 in on 4 in boards keep their filler; a 1 nm filler made equal, 8 m and 2 nm on 4 m boards made 4 + 2 + 2 m and a hair, which #53 refused; widths a hundred floating point steps either side of a whole number of boards never leave a strip under the minimum); the invariants now check that every glue-up's strips are as the rule plans them, none under the minimum, that a part reported too large glues on no board of its class, and that no board of its class glues a part from fewer strips, so the property tests check the new rule on random nestings, now with longer, narrower boards in the library too; `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (the 15 in panel is 8 + 7 1/8 in in the app, and a 24 1/4 in top is drawn and listed as 12 + 6 1/4 + 6 1/4 in). The tests that pinned #53's choices were changed to the new rule (the 17 in side, the 15 in panel, the 12 1/16 in panel, the refused 1 nm and 2 nm fillers), each saying what it was. Nine of the 28 nesting tests fail on the code before this change, each for the case it names; lane 45 added three: a 3 x 100 in rail beside a 5 in shelf, whole on 5/4 x 6 x 120 when the 4/4 board is 96 in long, and too large without that board; a 17 in side whose stock is 4/4 x 6, as oak's default or named by the part, still 6 + 6 + 5 1/4 in though 4/4 x 12 would take two strips; and a 0.8 in part on 3/4 in strip stock, two equal 0.4 in strips with a 3/8 in kerf and too large with a 1/2 in kerf. Compared with the code before this change over 100,000 random nestings (mixed lengths, grain, material defaults, set stocks, kerf, trim and joint loss from 0 to their limits, libraries with longer, narrower and odd boards): no part placed whole moved or was glued, no glue-up was refused, no sheet, no part with its grain across and no part whose stock is set or whose material's default applies changed board, a board change at the same strip count always went to a board listed earlier, and every other change was fewer strips, two equal last strips, or a part reported too large that is now glued or whole.

### Fixed (lane 44, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-13)

- **Dragging a big sketch with Move, Rotate or Scale keeps up** ([#59](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/59), from @aramshaw; [#8](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/8)). The drag preview checked both ends of every edge against every moving point on every frame, and did the same kind of scan several times as the drag started, so its cost grew with the edges times the moving points. With 1,000 rectangles selected (4,000 sketch edges), each step of a drag took a quarter to a third of a second, so the shapes trailed well behind the cursor. Now, as the drag starts, the moving points go into a grid lookup and each edge's two ends are looked up once; each frame only moves the ends already known to move. The preview draws the same edges with the same ends moved, each moving point found with its own tolerance as before, and it lies where the shapes land. Measured on the development machine with 4,000 moving edges: the preview's own work went from about 210 ms to 0.5 ms a frame, and from about 330 ms to 25 ms at the start of the drag (in Node); in a headless Edge, every frame of a Move, Rotate or Scale drag after the first now comes within 7 ms, where each took 0.24 to 0.31 s. The pull request measured 356 ms to about 1 ms a frame on another machine. Starting a Move of that selection still pauses once, for about 0.2 s where it was 0.6 s: gathering the selected shapes' points checks each one against those already gathered ([#11](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/11)), and `docs/LIMITS.md` lists it. How: `nearIndex` and `SketchPreview.moves` in `src/tools/xform.ts`, used by `sketchPreviews` and `sketchSegments`. Nothing in the kernel, the model or the file changes. Docs: `docs/LIMITS.md`, ADR-0012 (a dated note), `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `src/tools/move.test.ts` (eight times the edges cost less than 24 times as much, each frame and at the start of the drag, timed as the fastest of nine alternating runs after a warm-up: 5.5 to 11 times with the change, alone and beside other work, and about 64 times without it; on a sketch where every third point moves, the preview is the same edges with the same ends moved, plain and as a copy, as checking each end against every moving point; a point 10 km out, whose 4 mm tolerance makes the lookup's cells 8 mm wide, leaves a point 3 micrometres from a quarter-metre point unmoved and moves one 2.9 mm from itself, and a moving point that is not finite gives the preview the scan gave; over 400 seeded random sketches from 1 mm to 10 km across, some with a point 10 km out, with rings, carried edges either way round and points selected on their own, the same edges, moved ends and lift as checking every point, and the same segments under five moves, turns and scales, plain, as a copy and without the recorded ends).

### Fixed (lane 43, 2026-11-12)

- **Redo of a move that split a shape selects the pieces again** ([#7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/7)). Move a circle across a rectangle's edge, press Undo and then Redo: Redo replays the edit without the Move tool, so what Undo had left selected stayed selected, the circle's old region id, which the kernel had given to one of its pieces again. The hint bar said one region was selected, one half was lit, and the next Move, Delete or P with a typed distance acted on that half alone. Now Move, Rotate and Scale tell the app what was selected when the step started, and the app keeps it with what the tool left selected and Push/Pull's target before and after, for that one step (known by the undo depth and model hash it went from and to). Undo of the step selects what was selected before it, Redo selects what the tool left, each only where it still exists, and any other undo or redo keeps the selection as before. Nothing in the kernel, the model or the file changes. For tests, `window.__sawhorse.selected()` now says what is selected (each entity, with its kind and ids) and what the view lights as selected (regions and faces by id, imported meshes, and how many edge segments and points); it hands back a copy and changes nothing. How: `rememberSelection` on the tool host (`src/tools/tool.ts`), called by `src/tools/move.ts`, `rotate.ts` and `scale.ts`; `src/App.tsx` keeps the steps and hands the kept selection to the undo or redo it belongs to. Docs: the user guide, `docs/LIMITS.md` (the Redo case is gone from #7's list), ADR-0012, `DEVELOPMENT.md` (the hook). Tests: `e2e/sketch-move.spec.ts` (a 4 in circle moved 16 in across a 24 x 12 in rectangle's edge, then Undo, Redo, Undo and Redo, each read through the hook: the circle selected and lit after each Undo, its two halves after each Redo; changing what the hook returned changes nothing), which reads only the circle's old id after Redo without the change; `src/tools/move.test.ts` (the Move tool hands the host the selection from before the step, also when a distance typed after the move does it again).
- **A shape moved exactly onto an identical one is selected as the shape there** ([#7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/7)). Move a circle so it lands point for point on an identical circle, and the two become one: the moved circle's edges weld onto the other's. The selection kept the moved circle's region id, which named no region any more, so the hint bar counted one selected but nothing was lit, and P with a typed distance was refused. Now, when the kernel names no region for a region moved in its plane (it reports none for a shape that welds whole onto another), the selection is the regions the view mesh draws in that sketch inside where the shape went, here the circle it merged with, or nothing when there is none, never an id that names nothing. Push/Pull's target follows, so P and a typed distance push that circle. Nothing in the kernel, the model or the file changes. How: `regionsInside` beside `movedSelection` in `src/tools/xform.ts`. Docs: the user guide, `docs/LIMITS.md`, ADR-0012, `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `e2e/sketch-move.spec.ts` (two circles of 4 in radius, one moved 20 in onto the other: one region left, and it is what the hook says is selected and lit; P and 2 in make a solid of the circle's area times 2 in), which reads the moved circle's old id selected and nothing lit without the change; `src/tools/move.test.ts` (through the WASM kernel: a unit square moved onto an identical one is selected as the square that stays, and every id selected is a region the new mesh draws), which gets the old id back without the change.
- **A shape lifted onto another plane across a shape there is selected as its own pieces** ([#7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/7)). Lift a rectangle with Move onto the plane of another sketch, so that it lands across a square drawn there: it cuts the square, and the two split each other. The selection was every region the lifted edges bound there, so it held the rest of the square too, and Push/Pull's target was the first of those regions, which could be that remainder: P and a typed distance pushed the square's remainder. Now a lifted shape is selected as the regions there that lie inside where it went, by the same test as a move within the plane (lane 41), and Push/Pull's target follows: the one region it became there, or nothing when it is several, so P waits for a click on the piece to push. A shape lifted onto an empty plane, or onto a part of one with nothing in the way, is selected and pushed as before. Nothing in the kernel, the model or the file changes. How: `movedSelection` and `targetAfterMove` in `src/tools/xform.ts`, which is now given the target from before the edit and the sketch each moved sketch is in after it (`sketchesAfter`) by Move, Rotate and Scale. Docs: the user guide, `docs/LIMITS.md` (the lift case is gone from the #7 list), ADR-0012, `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `src/tools/move.test.ts` (through the WASM kernel: a 2 x 1 bar lifted 1 up across a 2 x 2 square on another plane is selected as its two halves, not the square's remainder of 3, and its target is none where the old rule gave that remainder; with the tool host, a region lifted across a shape there leaves no target and one lifted onto an empty plane targets the region it is there; `targetAfterMove` for a lift), each of which fails without the change.
- **A circle's rim a move breaks up is selected as every arc it became** ([#7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/7)). Select a circle's rim on its own and move it across a rectangle's edge: it splits into two arcs where it crosses. The selection kept the circle's curve id, which the first arc carries, so it shrank to that one arc, lit as such, and the next Move or Delete acted on half the circle. The same happened to a rim lifted onto another sketch's plane across an edge there. Now the kernel says which curve each further piece came from, and the selection is the first arc and every other. A rim moved exactly onto an identical circle shares its edges with it, and the circle drawn first keeps them; when that is the other circle, the moved rim's id named nothing and stayed selected, and now nothing is selected for it. Nothing in the model or the file changes, and no golden or model hash moves: the move's result gains `pieces`, (curve in the op, sketch, piece) for each further piece, left out when nothing broke up. How: `settle_curves` in `crates/kernel/src/sketch.rs` returns which curve each new piece came from (and `add_curve_traced` beside `add_curve` for a lifted curve); `transform_sketch` in `crates/kernel/src/sketch_move.rs` reports the op's own curves' pieces in `SketchMoved.pieces` (`src/kernel/types.ts` in step); `movedSelection` in `src/tools/xform.ts` selects them, and within the plane finds a curve by its id. Docs: the user guide, `docs/LIMITS.md` (the #7 entry is gone: none of its four cases is left), ADR-0013, `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/curves.rs` (a circle moved across a square's edge, and one lifted 1 up onto another plane across one, reports its two arcs, `curves` and `pieces` together naming the sketch's only curves; a line moved across a circle reports no pieces of the circle it cut); `src/tools/move.test.ts` (through the WASM kernel: the rim moved and lifted across a rectangle's edge is selected as both its arcs; a newer rim moved onto an older circle selects nothing, an older one onto a newer circle stays selected), which select one arc, and the merged rim's dead id, without the change.

### Fixed (lane 42 fix pass, found by the lane's verifier, 2026-11-12)

- **A part a hair wider than whole boards no longer stops the nesting** (the verifier's random glue-ups, 17 of 20,000 cases). A part 12 in and 1 nm wide on 6 in boards with no joint loss was planned as two strips, the second a hair wider than its board, and the board layout refused that strip and panicked, so the Nesting section could not be shown. The strip count came from a quotient and the filler from a sum, and at that boundary the two round apart by far less than a billionth of a millimetre. Now the count is settled on the filler itself: the least count whose filler the board holds, and a filler wider than the board is refused. That part is reported too large, as before #49, since its third strip would be a 1 nm sliver. Away from such a boundary every glue-up is planned as before, and no golden changed. A part of no length is never glued up either. How: `glue_widths` in `crates/kernel/src/nesting.rs`, and `testing::check_nesting_of` now works out each count's filler the way the kernel does, so the two agree to the last bit. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (that part, and every width a hundred floating point steps either side of such a boundary for 2 to 40 strips, four joint losses and two kerfs, with every invariant checked and no strip wider than its board; a 30 in part of no length never glued). Both fail without the change.
- **Parts of no size end their cut sequence** (found while fixing the above). With no kerf, a part of no length laid out after another part, or of no width beside one, sat on the far end of the offcut the cut before it left, and the first cut found was just before it: the same offcut with the same part, cut again and again until memory ran out (1.9 GB asked for, then an abort). Older than #49, and a model never reaches it, since its cut list does not count a part of no thickness, but the kernel takes any list. A cut before the first part must now also fall short of the offcut's far end; a part with a size always starts before the far end, so no layout of real parts changes (ADR-0007). How: `find_cut` in `crates/kernel/src/nesting.rs`. Test: `parts_of_no_size_end_their_cut_sequence` in `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs`, which aborts without the change.

### Added (lane 42, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-12)

- **A solid part wider than every board is planned as a glue-up, not left on no stock** ([#53](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/53), from @aramshaw; [#49](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/49)). A cabinet side in solid timber is often wider than any board: a 17 in side from 6 in stock is three strips, ripped, jointed and glued. The nesting listed such a part under "Parts on no stock" as larger than the stock, so the boards it needed were never counted or drawn. Now it is planned as full-width strips of a board and one narrower filler, the least number that covers its width once each joint loses the new **Joint loss** (1/8 in to start, 0 to 1/2 in, beside Kerf and Trim): `n >= (W - J) / (w - J)` strips for a part `W` wide from a board `w` wide, the last one `W + (n - 1) J - (n - 1) w`. That side is 6 + 6 + 5 1/4 in. The strips come off the board the rest of that wood and thickness uses, so they nest with the other parts and a narrow filler can share a board's width (#50); with nothing else of that wood, the widest board of its thickness. Each strip is tagged after its part ("4a.1", "4a.2"), drawn and listed at its own width ("strip 2 of 3"), and the Nesting section and the PDF booklet list every glue-up with its strips; the footer names the joint loss when something is glued up. A glued part counts once in the totals, and hovering it in the model lights every strip. A part some listed board thick enough makes in one piece is never glued, even a thicker, wider board than the rest of its wood uses; a part whose stock is set, by the part or as its material's default, is glued from that board when it is wider than it. Gluing adds only width, and a plan has to make sense at the saw: a part longer than the board, with its grain across, on a board no wider than the joint loss, whose last strip would be narrower than the kerf or 1 mm, or that would take more than 100 strips is still on no stock, now said as "larger than the board and cannot be glued up for width" with those five reasons. The joint loss is saved (`settings.jointLoss`, `docs/format.md`) and hashed only when it is not the 1/8 in default, so every file and model hash from before stays the same. Three goldens' nesting hashes moved, and only those: `components.json`, `cutlist-mixed.json` and `transforms.json`, each because a part that was too large is now glued up (two shelves and a solid from 5/4 x 6, a top from 4/4 x 4, a solid from 8/4 x 6); their model and cut list hashes did not move. The cut list's rows are unchanged; only the nesting knows about glue-ups. Docs: the user guide's Nesting section (**Glue-ups**, the joint loss, parts on no stock), `docs/format.md`, ADR-0007, `DECISIONS.md`, `CLEANROOM.md` and `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (a 15 in panel from a 12 in strip and a 3 1/8 in filler on one board; a 17 in side from three strips of the 6 in board two shelves use, and a 10 in part on a 12 in board never glued; a 14 in part beside two 3 in parts goes whole on a 5/4 x 16 board rather than glued from their 4 in board; no joint loss, two whole 12 in boards; a part too long to glue still too large; a 12 1/16 in panel with no joint loss and a 1/8 in kerf, which would leave a 1/16 in filler, still too large, and glued with a 1/16 in joint loss; a 12 in panel from 1/2 in boards with a 0.49 in joint loss, which would take 1,151 strips, too large, while a hundred 1 in strips make a 100 in panel; the joint loss saved, read back, refused outside 0 to 1/2 in, and hashed only when set; the stock test's oak on 8 in boards now glues its sides and top, and on a 24 in board reports them too large with that stock); the property tests now vary the joint loss, and `testing::check_nesting_of` checks every glue-up (strips 1 to n each once, off one board, all but the filler full width, their widths the part's width plus a joint loss per joint, listed as glued, and a part reported too large only when gluing cannot make it); the fuzzer sets the joint loss with the kerf and trim; `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (the Joint loss field and its range; a glue-up listed, its strips at their widths, every strip lit from the model, the footer); `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (a 15 x 40 in panel opened in the app is glued up on one board, drawn and listed, and a joint loss typed as 1/16 in re-plans its filler). The Rust tests do not compile on main (the new fields); there the same cut lists report each part too large. The vitest and e2e tests fail on main (no Joint loss field, no glue-up).

### Fixed (lane 41, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-12)

- **A region moved across another shape is selected as the pieces it became** ([#55](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/55), from @aramshaw; [#7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/7)). Move a circle so it lands across a rectangle's edge, and the circle splits in two where it crosses (and cuts the rectangle). The selection kept the circle's old region id, which the kernel had given to one of the pieces, so the hint bar said one region was selected, the highlight showed a single piece, and the next Move or Delete acted on that piece alone until you clicked again. Now the Move, Rotate and Scale tools select the pieces the region became: of the regions the kernel says the moved edges bound (`SketchMoved.regions`), those inside the moved shape, found by testing a point of each (the centre of its largest triangle in the view mesh) against the selection's rings where the move took them. It runs once per committed move, in one pass over the view mesh's triangles: about 11 ms for a move of 60 circles in a sketch of 400 (19,000 triangles), and 20 ms for 800 selected circles, measured in Node on the development machine. The rectangle's remainder stays unselected, and a move that splits nothing selects the region as before. Push/Pull's next target follows: a region that became one piece targets it, one that became several targets nothing, so P and a typed distance wait for a click on the piece to push instead of pushing the one piece the old id named. Nothing in the kernel, the model or the file changes. Four cases are not fixed here and `docs/LIMITS.md` lists them: a circle's rim selected on its own still shrinks to its first arc (the kernel names only a split curve's first piece); a shape moved exactly onto an identical one, so the two merge, keeps its old id, which names no region now (the kernel reports no regions for it); a shape lifted onto another sketch's plane where it cuts a shape there selects that shape's remainder too; and Redo of a split, which replays the edit without the tool, leaves the old id on one piece. How: `movedSelection` in `src/tools/xform.ts`, now given the new scene's view mesh by Move, Rotate and Scale, and `targetAfterMove` beside it. Docs: the user guide, `docs/LIMITS.md`, ADR-0012 and ADR-0013, `DEVELOPMENT.md`. Tests: `src/tools/move.test.ts` (a unit square moved across another shape, with the kernel reporting two pieces inside it and one outside, is selected as the two inside; a move that splits nothing keeps the region; Push/Pull's target after a split, a single piece and no split); `e2e/sketch-move.spec.ts` (a 4 in circle moved 16 in across a 24 x 12 in rectangle's edge in the app: three regions, the selection is the circle's two halves, P and a typed distance push nothing, and a click on a half then pushes it). Both fail without the change: the unit test gets the old id back, the end-to-end test reads "1" selected, as #7 describes. Three more in `src/tools/move.test.ts` run the tools' own steps through the WASM kernel and read the selection back by area: a bar turned 180 degrees across a square's edge is its two halves and the square's remainder is not selected (Rotate); a triangle mirrored across a square's edge is both its pieces (Scale); a square with a hole moved across another square is its two pieces, with its island and the other square's remainder left out (Move). Each gets the one old id back without the change.

### Added (lane 40, 2026-11-11)

- **The desktop test build also makes Mac and Linux files.** Someone testing on a Mac or a Linux desktop could not try `main` before a release: the "Desktop test build" workflow, run by hand from the Actions tab, made only the unsigned Windows installers. Now it has three jobs that do not depend on each other, so one that fails still leaves the others' files, and each uploads one artifact of that commit, kept 7 days: `unsigned-test-build-<commit>-windows` (the `.exe` and `.msi`, unchanged), `unsigned-test-build-<commit>-macos` (the Apple Silicon `.dmg`) and `unsigned-test-build-<commit>-linux` (the `.AppImage`, `.deb` and `.rpm`). Downloading an artifact needs a signed-in GitHub account. The Mac app is signed ad hoc and nothing more, as in alpha.5, so macOS asks on the first open and the steps under Unsigned installers in the [alpha.5 release notes](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/blob/main/docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.5.md#unsigned-installers) are the way through; the Linux files are unsigned. How: each job runs `release.yml`'s steps for its platform (the Mac one on `macos-15-intel` with `--target aarch64-apple-darwin`, since this repository's Apple Silicon runners would not start, its ad-hoc signing step and `scripts/macos-adhoc-check.sh` with no release tag; the Linux one on `ubuntu-22.04` with the same apt packages) with `npx tauri build` in place of tauri-action. The workflow still reads no secret, hands no GitHub token to any build step, has read-only permissions and makes no release and no tag; `release.yml`'s optional signing secrets step is left out. `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` says what each job makes. The Mac and Linux jobs had not been run when this landed; their first run, [43123831440](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/actions/runs/43123831440) on d99dfa4 on 2026-11-11, finished all three jobs with their files. Nobody has yet reported opening the Mac or the Linux files of a test build on a real machine.

### Fixed (lane 39, 2026-11-11)

- **A start-up path with `..` in it opens the file** ([#52](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/52)). Started from a terminal as `sawhorse-desktop.exe ..\box.shz`, from a folder beside the model, the desktop app opened an empty model and the status line showed the file reading side's raw error ("Can't do that. invalid args path for command stat: ... cannot traverse directory, rewrite the path without the use of ../"). The relative path was joined to the starting folder with its `..` still in it, and the fs plugin the page reads files through refuses any path that holds a `..`. Now the path is cleaned on its text alone once it is absolute: a `.` is dropped and a `..` takes away the folder name before it, so `..\box.shz` is `box.shz` in the folder above, and an absolute path holding `..` is cleaned the same way. A path whose `..` would climb above the root, a drive or a share is no file at all, nor is a `.` or `..` after a Windows `\\?\` prefix (Windows does not resolve them there); a `..` after the file's own name (`box.shz\..`) names a folder and opens nothing. The check that the file exists, the fs scope and what the page is handed all use exactly the cleaned path: still one file per start, still only a type File > Open takes, nothing made from it and nothing run. The cleaning reads nothing from the disk and never canonicalizes, so when a folder named just before a `..` is a link, the file opened is the one beside the link ([LIMITS](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md#the-desktop-app)). A double-click, a plain relative name and a full path open exactly as before. How: `clean` in `src-tauri/start-file`, over `std::path::Component`, called by `candidates`. Tests: `src-tauri/start-file` (`..` in a relative path, in an absolute one, several in a row, at the very start of an absolute path, climbing above the starting folder's root, `.` components, `..` after the file name, the existence check asked only about cleaned paths, and every answer of every test checked against what the fs plugin refuses: no `.` or `..` and nothing it would read as a URL); eight of them fail without the change. The Windows forms (a drive, a share, `\\?\`, backslashes) have tests that only a Windows build runs, and no job runs one yet; CI compiles the desktop shell on Linux only, and no desktop build of this change has been run by hand yet.
- **The desktop window's title bar names the model** (found in the same hand test as [#52](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/52)). The page has always put the model's name in its title, with a dot while there are unsaved changes ("• shelf - Sawhorse"), and a browser shows that on the tab, but the desktop app's title bar said only "Sawhorse" whatever model was open. Since lane 37 a second double-click on a `.shz` opens a second window, so two windows could not be told apart. Now the title bar shows the same text as the page's title and follows it: the name when a file opens, the dot after an edit, "Untitled - Sawhorse" after File > Close. In the browser build nothing changes. How: the page hands the text it gives `document.title` to the shell's new `window_title` command (`src/io/title.ts`, desktop app only); the shell passes it through `title` in the new plain crate `src-tauri/window-title` (no dependencies), which removes control characters and line breaks, keeps at most 200 characters, cut on a character boundary and ending in "…", and falls back to "Sawhorse" when nothing is left, then sets it with Tauri's `Window::set_title` on the calling window only. No permission is added: the webview still has no core:window permission. A call that fails leaves the title bar as it was and says nothing. Tests: `src-tauri/window-title` (control characters, line and paragraph separators, a very long name in one-byte and many-byte characters, names in several scripts, blank titles); `src/io/title.test.ts` (the same text as `document.title`, never called in the browser build, a refused or throwing call swallowed, `App.tsx` setting its title only through it); `e2e/window-title.spec.ts` with the bridge stand-in (`e2e/tauri.ts`, which now plays `window_title`): the title following a start-up file, an edit and File > Close, a failing call never shown, the browser build untouched. The page's tests and the spec fail without the change. The desktop shell was not compiled on this machine (CI compiles it on Linux only), and nobody has seen the real title bar change yet.

### Fixed (lane 38, 2026-11-11)

- **A tiny solid's volume in inches reads "< 0.1 in³", never "0.0 in³"** ([#26](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/26)). With the units in fractional inches, decimal inches or feet and inches, the view's badge printed a solid under half a tenth of a cubic inch, a 7 mm cube or a 1/4 in cube say, as "0.0 in³", which is also what an empty model reads, so the solid looked as if it had no volume. Lane 33 made the metric badge read "< 0.000001 m³" for a solid under its step; the inch badge now keeps the same rule and reads "< 0.1 in³". Every other volume reads exactly as before, an empty model still reads "0.0 in³", and nothing in the model, the file or its hash changes. How: `formatVolume` in `src/lib/units.ts`, the one place the badge's volume is written. Tests: `src/lib/units.test.ts` (a 7 mm cube, a 1/4 in cube, a hair under half a tenth and the smallest volume a double holds read "< 0.1 in³" in every inch system; half a tenth and more rounds as before; zero and a tiny negative read as before; the spread of volumes from before #46 prints as it did, the one real solid under the step aside); `e2e/units.spec.ts` (a 7 mm cube drawn in the app reads "< 0.1 in³" and a 20 mm cube "0.5 in³" in each inch system). The unit tests fail without the change, and so does the 7 mm cube's end to end test, which read "0.0 in³" before.

### Added (lane 37, a report from @aramshaw, 2026-11-11)

- **Double-clicking a `.shz` on Windows opens it in Sawhorse** ([#45](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/45), reported by @aramshaw). A model saved as `shelf.shz` could not be opened from File Explorer: Windows asked which app to use and Sawhorse was not in the list, because the installers declared no file association and the desktop app never read a path it was started with. Now both Windows installers (the setup `.exe` and the `.msi`) associate `.shz` files with Sawhorse: a double-click, or Sawhorse in the Open with list, starts Sawhorse with that model, the files show the Sawhorse icon (from the setup `.exe`; the `.msi` names no icon for them), and uninstalling removes the association. On every system, a path after the program's name on a command line opens that file the same way, for any file File > Open takes; one file per start, and further paths are not opened. The file is opened exactly as a file dropped on the window is, with the same checks and messages. A double-click while Sawhorse is open starts a second window with that model, as starting Sawhorse twice does, so nothing in the first window is replaced and nothing needs asking there; the two windows keep their unsaved work in the same place ([LIMITS](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/blob/main/docs/LIMITS.md#the-desktop-app)). If the last run left unsaved work, Restore unsaved work? is asked first ("shelf.shz opens after you answer."): Discard opens the file, Restore brings the work back and puts an Open shelf.shz button beside it, which asks before replacing the work. Not yet: on a Mac a double-click does not open the file in Sawhorse and the Mac app claims no `.shz` files, and Linux file managers do not offer Sawhorse for them; File > Open, dragging onto the window and the command line work there.

### Changed (lane 36, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-11)

- **Narrow parts share a board's width when that saves a board** ([#51](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/51), from @aramshaw; [#50](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/50)). Board nesting was one-dimensional: every part took the board's full width along its length, so a 1 1/2 in rail on a 4 in board was charged all 4 in, and a job with many rails and stretchers over-ordered. Now a board can be crosscut into sections, each as long as its longest part, with parts ripped side by side from a section, a kerf apart, inside the board's width: four 1 1/2 x 40 in rails come off one 4/4 x 4 x 96 board instead of two, and the Nesting panel, the buy totals, the SVG, DXF and PDF booklet all show the one board. How: `board_layout` in `crates/kernel/src/nesting.rs` runs the existing first-fit-decreasing and lookahead twice, once with each part its own full-width section (exactly as before) and once on shelves (parts longest first, each into the first section with room left across the board, else a new section as long as itself), and takes the shelves only when they need fewer boards. So a layout that saves nothing is unchanged, deterministic as before (no solver, no randomness, input-order tie breaks), grain stays along the board for every piece, and the cut sequence is the one `decompose` already finds on a board: crosscut a section off, rip it to width (with a crosscut to length where a shorter part rides beside a longer one), then the next. On a board the parts are now listed in that order too, section by section, so the list beside the diagram follows the cuts; a board whose parts all lie on its near edge, which is every board before this change, lists them exactly as before. Sheets, stock choice and the file format are untouched, and so is every hash but that of a nesting whose board count drops; no golden moved. Nesting a large job takes a little longer, since the lookahead runs twice, each run within its own node budget: 1,000 narrow parts take about 2 ms, and 2,000 short parts of different lengths, the lookahead's worst case, about 115 ms where they took 70. Docs: the user guide's Nesting section (**Boards**), ADR-0007 and `docs/format.md`'s line on board stock. Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/nesting.rs` (four 1 1/2 x 40 in rails take one 4 in board in two sections of two, a kerf apart each way, the section's crosscut before its rips; two rails that already fit end to end stay end to end on the near edge; a part as wide as the board keeps its own section while two narrow rails share one beside it, one board instead of two; two each of 1 1/2 x 40, 1 1/4 x 30 and 1 x 20 in rails take one board in sections of 40, 30 and 20 in, with every placement, the parts' order and all ten cuts in order checked); `e2e/nesting.spec.ts` (four rails opened in the app give one board in the Nesting panel, the rails drawn side by side where the layout puts them with their tags, the six cuts listed and drawn in order, and the parts listed section by section). The property tests (no overlaps, kerf gaps, inside the usable area, parts in = placed + unplaced, cuts that cross no part, deterministic hashes) hold on the new layouts. Every new test that expects a board saved fails without the change; the end to end test reads "2 boards" there.

### Fixed (lane 35, a report from @aramshaw, 2026-11-11)

- **Closing the desktop window over unsaved changes asks first** ([#44](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/44), reported by @aramshaw). The window's X and Alt+F4 closed the desktop app at once, and every change since the last save was gone without a word. Now, with unsaved changes, any close the system sends to the window (the X, Alt+F4, the taskbar's Close, the red button on a Mac) asks "Discard the unsaved changes to this model and close Sawhorse?". Cancel keeps the window and the model as they were. OK closes it and, as File > New does, deletes the work kept for Restore unsaved work?. With nothing unsaved the window closes at once, as before. The question can never keep the window from closing: if it gets no answer (the app stopped responding, or the dialog never appeared), closing the window again two seconds later closes it without waiting, and the unsaved work is offered back at the next start. On a Mac, Quit Sawhorse in the app menu (Cmd+Q) still quits without asking.
- **File > Close and File > Exit** ([#44](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/44)). File > Close closes the model and leaves Sawhorse running on an empty untitled one, asking first over unsaved changes ("Discard the unsaved changes to this model?"); it is File > New under another name. In the desktop app, File > Exit (Quit Sawhorse on a Mac, Quit on Linux) closes Sawhorse the way the window's X does, with the same question. The browser build has File > Close only, since a page cannot close its own tab, and still does not ask before its tab closes: it keeps your work as you go and offers it back the next time.

### Fixed (lane 34, a pull request from @sdm7306, 2026-11-10)

- **An STL or OBJ that Sawhorse exported comes back in at its true size, whatever File > Import units says** ([#48](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/48), from @sdm7306; [#47](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/47)). Sawhorse writes STL and OBJ in millimetres and says so inside the file (the STL's header, the OBJ's first line), but the importer read every STL and OBJ in File > Import units, which is remembered from one session to the next. With it on Inches, as it naturally is for someone working in fractional inches, a part sent out and back came in 25.4 times too big: a 2 x 4 x 48 in board came back ~ 1219 3/16 in long beside the original, and after a second round trip a bench frame's 48 in rail measured ~ 30967 11/16 in with the Tape (25.4 x 25.4) and the badge read 1460566899164.6 in³. The Tape was measuring the model right; the model was the wrong size. Now a binary STL whose 80 byte header is exactly Sawhorse's ("Sawhorse binary STL; units mm; Z up", the rest NULs) and an OBJ whose first line is exactly Sawhorse's ("# Sawhorse OBJ; units mm; Z up", also after a text editor added a byte order mark or CRLF line ends) are read as millimetres, and the import report says so ("Sawhorse STL: read as millimetres, as its header says (File > Import units is for other STL files)", and the same for OBJ). Every release has written exactly these bytes, so an STL or OBJ exported by an earlier version comes back at its size too. Every other STL and OBJ, every ASCII STL (Sawhorse writes none) and a DXF without units still follow File > Import units, as before; a file from another program that copies Sawhorse's header byte for byte is read as millimetres, as it says. Nothing in the model, the file format or its hash changes. A part that already came in too big stays that size: delete it and import the original export again, or scale it with the Scale tool by 0.0393701 (1 / 25.4) once for each round trip it went through.
