Tests: `crates/import/src/stl.rs` and `crates/import/src/obj.rs` (a 48 in rail exported and imported twice under each of the five import units stays 48 in long with its volume, through the STL writer, both OBJ writers and the kernel; another program's STL and OBJ, a header that only starts like ours, an OBJ whose matching line is not its first and one that says "units in" still follow the menu; our OBJ with a byte order mark or CRLF line ends is ours, and a first line that is not valid UTF-8 or only part of ours is not; short files and odd counts after our header are not read as ours and do not panic; an ASCII STL follows the menu); `e2e/files.spec.ts` (with File > Import units on Inches, another program's 48 x 3 1/2 x 1 1/2 in rail comes in in inches, Sawhorse's STL of it comes back 48 x 3 1/2 x 1 1/2 in, not 1219.2 in long, then an OBJ of both rails comes back at their size too, the report naming each). Each fails without the change. `crates/wasm-api`'s `imports_files_through_the_doc` asserted the bug: it read Sawhorse's own STL in inches and expected it 25.4 times bigger. It now gives that check another program's STL (the same triangles with a blank header), so it still shows the units reach the reader, and checks that Sawhorse's own STL in inches comes back at its size; no assertion was removed and no tolerance changed.

### Fixed (lane 33, a pull request from @aramshaw, 2026-11-10)

- **With the units on mm, cm or m, the view's badge gives the volume in cubic metres** ([#46](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/46), from @aramshaw; [#26](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/26)). The badge at the top left of the view printed the model's volume in cubic inches whatever the units were: a bookcase with the units on mm read "26967.5 in³", a number nobody working in metric can check against anything. Now it follows the document's units: the three inch systems read cubic inches to one decimal, exactly as before, and mm, cm and m read cubic metres to six decimals (a cubic centimetre) with trailing zeros dropped, so a 300 mm cube is "0.027 m³", a 19 x 90 board 2400 long is "0.004104 m³" and that bookcase is "0.441918 m³", the unit rough-sawn timber is priced in. A solid smaller than half a cubic centimetre, a 7 mm cube say, reads "< 0.000001 m³", never "0 m³". Changing the units changes the badge at once; nothing in the model, the file or its hash changes. How: `formatVolume` in `src/lib/units.ts`, which the badge's line in `src/App.tsx` calls with the document's units. This is the status bar's part of #26's "labels and totals follow the units"; the nesting labels, the totals and the stock library are not touched, and the Model report plugin still gives metric volumes in cm³. Tests: `src/lib/units.test.ts` (the inch systems print exactly what the badge printed before for a spread of volumes, zero, negative, NaN and infinity among them; the metric systems in cubic metres; the six-decimal step and the half-step edge; a real solid under the step; a large model; both texts read back to the same volume; every unit system answered); `e2e/units.spec.ts` (a 300 mm cube drawn in the app reads "0.027 m³" in mm, cm and m and never "in³", then "1647.6 in³" in each inch system and never "m³"; a 7 mm cube reads "< 0.000001 m³" and a 20 mm cube "0.000008 m³" in each metric system). Each fails without the change.

### Fixed (lane 32, a pull request from @akvalentine977, 2026-11-10)

- **On Linux, a name typed in Save As without `.shz` is saved as `.shz`** ([#29](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/29), from @akvalentine977; [#31](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/31)). The desktop app's Save dialog on Linux (GTK's file chooser) hands the name back exactly as typed, so Save As `cabinet` wrote a file named `cabinet`: a valid document that the Open dialog's "Supported files" filter then hid, so it opened only by typing its full path. Windows and macOS add the filter's extension in the dialog itself. Now a name without one of the type's extensions gets the type's first, for exports too: `cabinet` is saved as `cabinet.shz`, `cabinet.v2` as `cabinet.v2.shz`, `part` as `part.stl`, and `Cabinet.SHZ` stays as it is. The dialog asked about replacing only the name that was typed, so when a file of the final name is already there the app asks first, "cabinet.shz already exists. Replace it?". No writes nothing and leaves the document as it was; Yes replaces it, and the old copy is kept as `cabinet.shz.bak` as on every save. It asks every time, also when that file was saved the same way earlier in the session or was opened or dropped on the window; a later Ctrl+S to the file just saved does not ask again. How: the page sends the dialog's own path along with the save (`x-chosen`; `withExtension` and `saveFile` in `src/io/files.ts`), and `save_file_atomic` asks `decide` in `sawhorse-safe-save`, a pure function, which writes only when that path is one a dialog returned, is not a folder, and the final path is it plus one extension of 1 to 10 letters or digits (the rule a plugin's `file.save` keeps). A save never adds a path to the ones the app may write. A save whose path already ends in one of the type's extensions (on Windows and macOS every save where the dialog added it, the usual case; a name typed with its extension on Linux; and Ctrl+S after an open, a drop or such a save) reaches the shell exactly as before; a name a Windows dialog hands back with another extension, `cabinet.txt`, is now saved as `cabinet.txt.shz`. Found on Linux (the 0.1.0-alpha.4 `.deb`) by scripting the app under Xvfb. Tests: `src-tauri/safe-save` (the decision against a scope and a disk kept as plain sets: a path the dialog returned as it is; an added extension on a new file and on an existing one, with and without consent; a second Save As of the same name, and a file opened or dropped, asked about again; no chain through an earlier save or an opened file; a folder, a path that is not the chosen one plus one extension, and the rule's edges refused (empty, a trailing dot, 11 characters, a non-ASCII letter, a slash, a backslash or a second dot inside, a hidden name); without a chosen path the answer is the scope's alone, as before, whatever the disk or the consent say; the same on a real folder with the shell's own questions); `src/io/save.test.ts` (the name with and without its extension, in either case, for every type the app saves; on Windows and macOS the request is the old one; the chosen path travels and stays on the handle; Ctrl+S on an opened, dropped or plainly saved file writes its path exactly, and on a handle with a chosen path carries consent and asks nothing; the question, No and Yes; any other refusal, also one after Yes, is an error; a save given up during the question writes nothing; a plugin's save is asked about like any Save As). Each fails with its part of the change taken out. The desktop shell is compiled by CI's Linux job; it was not run as an app for this change.

### Fixed (lane 31, pull requests from @brewmium, 2026-11-10)

- **A rectangle drawn square on the ground stays a square** ([#23](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/23), from @brewmium; [#9](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/9)). In a 3D view, with the first corner on the ground or the grid, a rectangle dragged out to 12 x 12 in collapsed to a vertical line reading "0 x 12" the moment it became square (11 7/8 x 12 was fine). From an iso-style eye a square's diagonal lies over the blue axis through the first corner on screen, and while the rectangle's plane is still open its far corner snaps in 3D, so the blue axis won over the grid and stood the rectangle up with no width. Now, while the plane is still open, the far corner no longer offers the blue line through the first corner, or "from point" alignments along it: the inference query takes `start_skip`, a list of directions, and the Rectangle sends the blue axis until its plane is set and nothing after, since on a set plane the line that would leave it is already dropped. The red and green lines stay, so the far corner still says when the rectangle is square to an axis; so do parallel and perpendicular lines off a diagonal edge and alignments with points elsewhere. Arrow-key locks keep every line, and the Rotated Rectangle is unchanged. An upright rectangle still comes from starting on an upright face or an arrow key before the first click. Tests: `crates/inference/tests/infer.rs` (with the iso-style eye the blue axis takes the square's corner, and with blue skipped the grid gives exactly 12 x 12 in; a "from point" along the skipped blue line goes with it; a diagonal reference stays; a lock keeps a skipped line on a ray that meets nothing else); `src/tools/draw.test.ts` (the far corner skips blue while the plane is open and nothing on a set plane; the Rotated Rectangle skips nothing).
- **On a Mac, Control+drag pans the view, in every tool** ([#41](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/41), from @brewmium; [#40](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/40)). Control+click is the Mac's right click in every app, and with a one-button mouse, a trackpad, or a left hand on a Magic Mouse it is the right click that comes easily. Sawhorse took it as a left click with Ctrl held: in Select it toggled, in Paint it painted the whole solid, and the view never panned; only the right button itself did. Now, on macOS only, a left press with Control and not Cmd is the right button (`isMacControlClick` in a new `src/lib/platform.ts`, with `isMac()`, the app's first OS check): `leftButtonNav` answers "pan" for it in every tool, so lane 30's left-button path pans and keeps the press and its double-click from the tool, and the drag stays a pan through Control let go and through Shift. Cmd stays the tools' Ctrl, as it always counted: Cmd+click toggles a selection or paints a whole solid, and the Eraser softens with Cmd. A tap of Control (Move and Rotate copy, Scale about the centre) is its release with no key or click between, on macOS only, as Alt's has been since lane 30, so a Control+drag pan in Move does not switch copying. On Windows and Linux nothing changes: Ctrl+click and Ctrl+drag reach the tool as before and a Ctrl tap still counts as the key goes down. Tests: `src/lib/platform.test.ts` (only a Mac is a Mac, and only its left press with Control and without Cmd, from a mouse or a pen, is the right click); `src/tools/navigate.test.ts` (a Mac Control+click pans in every tool, the Orbit and Pan tools, Paint, Section Plane and a plugin's tool, whatever Shift and Alt do); `src/viewport/controls.test.ts` (Control+left pans on macOS through Control let go and Shift pressed and let go, and ends on its release; not on Windows, nor with Cmd); `src/App.test.tsx` (on a Mac the Control tap is its release, and not across a press or Control+Z; on Windows and Linux the tap still counts as the key goes down); `e2e/orbit-trackpad.spec.ts` (the platform set to macOS: Control+drag pans in Select and under Orbit through Control let go, Select sees nothing, Cmd+click toggles, Move's copying is untouched and a tap still switches it). Paint's, Select's and the Eraser's end-to-end Ctrl+clicks press `ControlOrMeta`, which is Control on Windows and Linux, so there they still show that a Ctrl+click reaches the tool (a toggle, a whole solid painted, an edge softened), and they pass on a Mac too.
- **A right-drag pans a drawing sheet, as it pans the 3D view; on a Mac, a Control+drag does too** ([#42](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/42), from @brewmium; [#40](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/40)). A sheet panned only with the middle button or Space+drag, and a right press did nothing on the paper, so the right-drag that pans the model did nothing there, and a Mac's Control+click (its right click) reached the sheet tools as a plain click. Now the sheet canvas starts its pan on the right button and on a macOS Control+click (`isMacControlClick`) as it does on the middle button; the browser's menu was already kept off the paper. The right-drag is on every platform; Control+drag is macOS only, and on Windows and Linux a Ctrl+drag is still the tool's own drag. Tests: `e2e/sheets.spec.ts` (with the View tool in hand, a right-drag on the platform as it is, and a right-drag and a Control+drag with the platform set to macOS, each move the paper by the drag and place no viewport; off a Mac a Ctrl+drag still places a viewport and leaves the paper where it was). Both fail without the change.

## [0.1.0-alpha.5] - 2026-11-10

A pre-release, [v0.1.0-alpha.5](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.5); every change since alpha.4: [v0.1.0-alpha.4...v0.1.0-alpha.5](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/compare/v0.1.0-alpha.4...v0.1.0-alpha.5). The release notes: [docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.5.md](docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.5.md).

### Release (the alpha.5 hygiene pass, 2026-11-10)

- Version 0.1.0-alpha.5 in npm, Tauri (MSI version 0.1.0.5), both Cargo workspaces and their locks (only the members' own version lines), the `.shz` generator examples, the site's download links and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s list of the release's files; the release notes in `docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.5.md` (Save after a deletion, #21, #34 and #35, first; the macOS ad-hoc signing, #27, with what it does not change; orbit without a middle button, #36; Back, Left and Bottom views; the discard question, #32; SketchUp curves and older files' scenes; the unit picker, #13; `sawhorse-cli`, #14; the smaller fixes; not yet, with the trackpad's two-finger orbit, #36, metric stock, #26, and Control+click on a Mac, #40; the known limits, with the window's close button, the Linux Save As name, #31, and the SketchUp 5 file, #15, with its re-save; an antivirus's hold on an unsigned download beside SmartScreen; thanks by GitHub login). The README, the site and `docs/SIGNING.md` point at them.
- Docs and site for alpha.5: `docs/LIMITS.md` says it is current for alpha.5 (with lanes 25 and 28); the site's unsigned-builds note says the release notes have a one-line fix if macOS says the app is damaged; `docs/SIGNING.md` says alpha.5 is the first release signed ad hoc and is not yet confirmed on a real Mac; the README says `cargo run -p sawhorse-cli` runs in the checkout's `crates/` folder (from its root, cargo finds no workspace); `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s release checklist names the lock regeneration and says to quote messages from the code at the release's head.

### Fixed (lane 30's small corrections, 2026-11-09)

- **A cut list with no lumber totals 0 board feet, not -0.** `sawhorse-cli cutlist` printed `"board_feet":-0.0` for a model with nothing to cut or only sheet goods, and `sawhorse-cli nest` printed `-0.0` areas for a nesting with nothing placed: an f64 sum over nothing is -0.0 in Rust. The kernel adds those totals up from 0.0 now (`cutlist.rs`, `nesting.rs`), so the app's panes get the same 0 the command line prints. Nothing else changes: every other total adds up in the same order to the same value, and no hash reads them.
- **Kerf and Trim keep what was set while a typed length waits** (from the review of [#33](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/pull/33)). A length typed in the Nesting section's Kerf or Trim box is committed by Enter, by leaving the box, or by a pause of 700 ms. The pause's timer kept the settings of the moment it was set, so typing `3/16 in` and pressing Enter committed it, then committed it again 700 ms later (a second undo step for the same change); and if the kerf and the trim were changed from outside while a typed kerf waited (a plugin, or Redo), the timer set the trim back to what it had been. A commit now reads the settings as they are when it runs, and Enter or leaving the box stops the timer.
- **The alpha.4 release notes date the ad-hoc signing right.** They said the release workflow signs the macOS app ad hoc "since alpha.4"; it does from the build after alpha.4, which is why alpha.4 itself says "damaged" on Apple Silicon.
- Tests: `crates/cli/tests/cutlist.rs` (an empty model's cut list and nesting print no `-0.0` in plain or pretty JSON, and a board's total is its row's board feet); `src/components/NestingSection.test.tsx` (Enter, then the pause, commits once; a kerf and trim set from outside while a typed kerf waits are kept, and the next typing commits against them). Both fail without the change.

### Added (how to contribute, 2026-11-09)

- **[CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md)** ([#37](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/37)). A contributor asked how help is wanted; the answer is written down at the root of the repository: reports, small fixes straight away, larger items and new ideas through an issue first, AI-assisted pull requests welcome with the trailer kept and what was run said, what makes a change fit (a test that fails without it, a changelog entry in this form, the guide with the behaviour, the full gate with its exact commands), how a pull request lands, what the maintainer keeps (the `.shz` format and the model hash, releases, signing, the site), and the tests on real hardware that would help most. The README and `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` point to it.

### Added (orbit without a middle button, 2026-11-09)

- **Orbit with a trackpad: the Orbit tool (O) and Option+drag** ([#36](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/36)). On a MacBook with only its trackpad you could pan (a two-finger click and drag) and zoom (a pinch) but not orbit: orbit was the middle button's alone, a trackpad has none, and no key or modifier stood in for it, so the standard views were the only way to turn the model. Now the **Orbit tool** (O, on the rail and View > Orbit) orbits with a plain left-drag exactly as a middle-drag does (the same pivot under the cursor, the same crosshair, the same stop short of looking straight down), Shift+drag pans, and Esc goes back to the tool you had; the **Pan tool** (H, View > Pan) pans with the left button. In every tool but Paint and Section Plane, **Option+drag** (Alt+drag on Windows and Linux) orbits without leaving the tool, halfway through a line or a move too, and Option+Shift+drag pans. Paint keeps Option+click for picking a material up and Section Plane keeps Option for reversing the cut, so in those two the O key is the way. The guide's Navigating section says all of it, with a paragraph for trackpads.
- **An Alt tap is a tap.** Move's autofold switch and Section Plane's reverse came on Alt going down, so holding Option for an Option+drag would have switched autofold every time. They now come when Alt is let go with no key and no click between. Section Plane's Alt+click now places the plane reversed, as the guide always said: the tap on the way down used to reverse it first, and the click turned it back.
- **Shift switches a middle-drag back as well.** Pressing Shift during a middle-drag switched the orbit to a pan, but letting Shift go did not switch it back, and letting the button go was only noticed at the next move: the switch took the button from the move event, which carries none. The drag now keeps the button it was pressed with, for the left button's drags too.
- How: the camera controller asks the app what a left press does (`leftButtonNav` in `src/tools/navigate.ts`: the Orbit or Pan tool, or Alt in a tool that gives Alt no meaning) and runs the middle button's own drag for it (`src/viewport/controls.ts`), so the orbit itself is the same code; that press and its double-click never reach the tool. A plugin's tool counts as a tool without an Alt meaning ([PLUGINS.md](docs/PLUGINS.md#tools)). The rail has a sixth group for the two tools, after Section (ADR-0003's lane-30 note).
- Tests: vitest (`src/viewport/controls.test.ts`: an Alt+left press orbits about the same pivot as a middle press and leaves the camera exactly where the middle drag did, Alt+Shift pans and letting Shift go orbits, Shift switching a middle or an Alt drag both ways four times with button-less moves and the release ending it at once (fails on main), a plain left press in a tool is not taken, the Orbit and Pan tools; `src/tools/navigate.test.ts`: the rule for every kind of tool, Paint, Section Plane and a plugin's; `src/App.test.tsx`: O, H, Esc back to the tool before, View > Orbit and Pan, the Alt tap); `e2e/orbit-trackpad.spec.ts` with the real mouse: Alt+drag orbits about a box corner pinned under the cursor, leaves the standard view with no view button lit and selects nothing, Alt+Shift pans, Move's autofold untouched by the orbit and switched by a tap; the O tool orbits, H pans, Esc returns to Line and then to Select; in Paint, Alt+click still samples and an Alt drag does not orbit; in Section Plane, Alt+click and an Alt tap both place the plane reversed.

### Added (a headless cut list, 2026-11-09)

- **`sawhorse-cli`: the cut list or the nesting of a saved `.shz` as JSON, with no app** ([#14](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/14): "reading a cut list from a .shz as JSON without opening the app"). `sawhorse-cli cutlist Bookshelf.shz` prints what the Cut list pane reads (rows with finished sizes, quantities, grain, stock, board feet or area, the totals, the hash), `sawhorse-cli nest Bookshelf.shz` what the Nesting section reads; `--visible-only`, `--rounding sixteenth|thirty-second|half-mm` (cutlist), `--algorithm guillotine|maxrects` (nest) and `--pretty`. What the reader had to do to open the file (a texture missing from it, an object rebuilt from `geometry.glb`) goes to stderr as `note:` lines, so a script can tell a clean read from a repaired one while stdout stays JSON. It is the kernel's own serialization through `sawhorse_shz::read`, so a file gives the same JSON here and in the app. A new workspace crate, `crates/cli`, with no dependency beyond the kernel, the reader and serde_json; it reads and prints, never writes. A CLI that builds or changes a model, and an MCP server, are not in this; the kernel's op log makes both possible later.
- Tests: `crates/cli/tests/cutlist.rs` (a saved board's cut list and nesting equal the kernel's for the same model, byte for byte; rounding, visible-only and the algorithm reach the kernel; a saved file rewritten with a `plugin-data.json` that is not text comes back with the reader's note and the same cut list, a clean file with none; a file that is not a document says so; the command line end to end: every option, every refusal, a missing file).

### Fixed (the desktop app waits for the discard question, 2026-11-09)

- **The desktop app asks before throwing away unsaved changes** ([#32](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/32)). In the Windows, macOS and Linux app, File > Open and File > New replaced a model that had unsaved changes at once, and "Discard the unsaved changes to this model?" was never seen. The dialog plugin gives the desktop's `window.confirm` back as a Promise (a yes whatever the answer, to code that does not wait), and the app did not wait; the shell had also never granted the plugin's message permission, so the dialog could not have shown. The question now goes through the dialog plugin on the desktop (`askYesNo` in `src/io/files.ts`, with the app's name on the dialog) and is awaited wherever it is asked: Open, New, and an empty SketchUp file opening as a new model. The browser build asks as before. `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` grants `dialog:allow-message`. Found on Linux (the 0.1.0-alpha.4 .deb and AppImage, and `main`) by scripting the app under Xvfb.
- Tests: vitest (`src/io/files.test.ts`: the browser asks with window.confirm; the desktop asks through the plugin and waits for the answer); `e2e/files.spec.ts` stands in the desktop's asynchronous confirm and checks that Open and New over unsaved changes wait for No and keep the model, and go ahead on Yes.

### Added (Back, Left and Bottom views, 2026-11-09)

- **Back, Left and Bottom standard views**, on the View menu ("Back view", "Left view", "Bottom view"). Each is a square, parallel view framed like the others: Back looks along -Y, Left along +X, and Bottom looks up from below with the front edge at the top and red running right, as a sheet's Bottom view draws it. The top bar keeps its five buttons; a menu view lights none of them. `ViewId`, `VIEWS` and `VIEW_DIRS` in `src/viewport/camera.ts` carry the three, and `BAR_VIEWS` is the bar's five. The README's "not yet" list loses its Back and Bottom line.
- Tests: `src/viewport/camera.test.ts` (the eight views, the bar's five, every direction, opposite pairs, up square to direction); `e2e/navigation.spec.ts` (each menu view sets the view and a parallel projection with no bar button pressed).

### Fixed (a lock ignores a point level with its start, 2026-11-09)

- **Push/Pull no longer drops to 0 partway up a drag.** In the iso view, pushing a 12 x 12 in rectangle up from near its top right, the distance went 1, 2, then 0 with the preview gone for a stretch, then 4, 5 ...; from lower on the rectangle it happened at 8 or 9. The cursor going up the screen also runs diagonally over the ground, past the rectangle's far corner, its edges' midpoints and the alignment lines through the corners just drawn, which run along its far edges and on past them. Those points are level with the face, so projected onto the locked push line they meant "no distance at all".
- How: a point that a lock would project back onto the gesture's start is no longer a snap; the cursor keeps following the line. A point at any other level still projects (push until level with a neighbour's corner). It is in the inference engine, so every locked line gets it.
- Tests: an inference test sweeps the cursor up the iso view from six grabs on the rectangle, with the drawn corners remembered, and holds that the distance never falls to 0, and that a neighbour's top corner still projects.

### Fixed (lane 28, 2026-11-09)

- **Save works after you delete, update, explode or group things** ([#21](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/21), [#34](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/34), [#35](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/35)). In alpha.4, Ctrl+S could answer "Could not save: the file did not read back as written: the model differs" and keep answering it for every edit after: once the cabinet generator had updated a cabinet in place (Generate pressed again with the cabinet selected), once a cabinet or a component had been exploded and its parts grouped, once a copy of a component had been deleted, or after any other deletion. Those models now save, open as the same model, and save again. Nothing that was saved before needs anything done to it.
- What was wrong: Save reads its own file back and refuses one that would not open as the model in the window (lane 16), and the reader numbered every list 1, 2, 3 ... again. A model in which something had been deleted has gaps in its ids, or ids in a slot used before (the cabinet's eight nodes after an update were 4294967297 to 4294967304), so the file opened with other ids, the model hash (which includes them, because ops and scenes name things by id) differed, and Save refused. The fix stores and restores the ids instead of hiding them: a `.shz` reader now keeps every id exactly as written, holes and reused slots included (`ids::with_ids`), for solids, meshes, groups and instances, section planes, and (new optional `id` fields, [format.md](docs/format.md#ids-lane-28)) sketches, component definitions, guides, dimensions and sheets. Files without the new fields open numbered as before. A tampered file's ids are refused with a plain message in under a millisecond: zero, a slot past twice its list's limit, a slot listed twice, or more than 2^24 steps to rebuild ([LIMITS.md](docs/LIMITS.md)). The same pass found that a saved section plane's normal and a guide's direction moved by their last bit on every save (the reader normalised a unit vector again); a unit one is now read as written. Neither change makes the hash ignore anything, and the read-back check is as strict as before.
- When Save does refuse, it says what to do: "Could not save: the saved copy would not have opened as the same model, so nothing was written; File > Export keeps the shapes. Please report this." (a file that does not read back at all says why inside the same sentence). The model, its undo and the unsaved mark stay as they were, and the next save writes them.
- Tests: `crates/shz` round trips, one per report (the cabinet generator's import op, updated in place three times with the stored id still naming it, then a board drawn; a cabinet exploded, its parts grouped, another deleted, two parts grouped inside an open cabinet; components arrayed, scaled, turned, made unique, deleted and exploded, then edited inside after opening), a deletion from every list, refused ids, and files without the new ids. On `bf2c9f0` the four round trips and the refusal test fail (the old-file test passes on both); all pass now. The op fuzzer and every repro replay now save, open and compare each sequence (`testing::check_saved`: same hash, ids and snapshot; about 5% more fuzzer time, 26.7 s against 25.3 s for 2,500 sequences); the golden round-trip test no longer excuses documents whose ids are not 1, 2, 3; `src/io/save-roundtrip.wasm.test.ts` imports all 203 corpus `.skp` files and saves and opens each one through the WASM kernel, after an explode and two deletions too (about 2 s; 110 of them failed on `bf2c9f0`); `crates/wasm-api` checks the refusal's wording and that the work survives it; `e2e/plugins-samples.spec.ts` saves and reopens an updated cabinet and an exploded, grouped one in the app; `e2e/files.spec.ts` drives a refused save (`?kerneltest=1`) and checks nothing is downloaded and nothing is lost. No golden hash and no corpus byte changed.
- **Save no longer refuses after an edit that empties a component is undone** (lane 28 follow-up, found by CI's long fuzz at seed 20261011 the day lane 28 landed). With three copies of a component, a group made inside one copy is in all three; delete what the group held from inside it in one copy, draw a line there, and open another copy, and the empty groups go, which takes the group out of the component and so out of every copy, and the copy that held the line is then empty. Tidying swept empty groups and copies once, before it carried that removal through the copies, so the emptied copy stayed (invisible, holding nothing) until the next edit of any kind removed it. Undoing that edit brought it back, and Save then refused, because the file of that model opened without it: Save's read-back check (lane 16) holds a file to the model in the window, and the model in the window held a copy no edit would have kept. The fix is in the model, not the file: tidying now sweeps again after every round of carrying changes through copies, until a sweep finds nothing, so the copy goes in the edit that empties it and Undo never brings back a model the reader would tidy differently. The writer, the reader and `check_saved` are unchanged. Tests: the fuzzer's minimal sequence as `crates/kernel/tests/repro/2026-11-09-save-after-a-component-copy-emptied-by-tidy.json`, and `crates/shz`'s `a_component_copy_emptied_by_tidying_is_not_saved_as_a_ghost`, which saves and opens the model at each step and holds that only the open copy is left, before and after the undo (both fail without the fix). The long fuzz passes at 10,000 sequences for seeds 20261011, 20261109 and 7. Still open, and separate: the line drawn in that group is gone with it when you leave (a group's counterparts are swept although one of them holds a line), in the window as well as the file.
- **macOS: the app is signed ad hoc until it has a certificate, and the release notes say how to open a "damaged" alpha.4** ([#27](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/27)). On Apple Silicon, alpha.3's and alpha.4's disk images said "Sawhorse.app is damaged and can't be opened" with no Open Anyway button, because the app carried no signature at all. The release workflow now sets an ad-hoc identity when no Apple identity is configured, so Tauri signs the `.app` before it makes the `.dmg`, and a step after the build verifies the app and the app inside the `.dmg` (`codesign --verify --deep --strict`), signing, rebuilding and re-uploading them if they do not verify (`scripts/macos-adhoc-check.sh`). Untested until the next release build runs on a Mac runner. For alpha.4, [the release notes](docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.4.md#unsigned-installers) and [SIGNING.md](docs/SIGNING.md) give `xattr -cr /Applications/Sawhorse.app` and, if that is not enough, `codesign --force --deep --sign - /Applications/Sawhorse.app`.

### Fixed (lane 27, 2026-11-01)

- **A SketchUp import's curve note says what the model holds** ([#17](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/17)). openskp's `gondola_v20.skp` said "16 curves came in as curves (8 arcs, 8 polylines)" while the model held 24 polylines and no arc: the note counted a component's curves once, for its definition, in the frame its items are built in, and its arcs sit in components placed with an uneven scale, which turns each copy's arcs into polylines only where it lands. The note now counts every placed copy as it lands, as the curves view does (the gondola: "24 curves came in as curves" and "16 circles or arcs in groups or components scaled unevenly came in as polylines"), and an uneven scale on a component's placement is said with a group's: "N circles or arcs in groups or components scaled unevenly came in as polylines". Five of the 35 real files tried said a different number than their curves view (Corynth's cupboard said 30 curves, 6 circles and 24 arcs, for a model holding 12 circles and 48 arcs); none does now.
- How: after an import places everything, the kernel reads the rims of every solid it placed (each copy of each component, nested ones too) the way the curves view reads them, and counts those, so the note cannot disagree with the view. A circle or an arc stretched by a group's uneven scale still comes in as a polyline, flagged for the kernel (`ImportCurve.stretched`) so it is counted per copy with the rest; what stayed plain edges (on a mesh, drawn on a face, not on one circle) is counted per copy too. The notes written before the kernel sees a curve (a record that did not match its edges, a broken chain, an ellipse, an arc cut on a chord) still count each definition once. Nothing about the geometry changed: the 35 files bring the same solids, meshes and curves, kind by kind, and no model hash, golden or corpus byte changed (ADR-0013's lane-27 addendum).
- Tests: kernel tests (`crates/kernel/tests/import_curves.rs`: a definition's arc placed evenly and unevenly, "2 curves came in as curves (1 arc, 1 polyline)" with one stretched; nested copies of a definition with a rim, a curve on a mesh and a flagged polyline, six copies, two stretched by their placement; the flag in JSON; the component test that asserted one count per definition now asserts one per copy); vitest (`src/io/skp.test.ts`: the issue's case written with openskp's writer and imported through the WASM kernel, the group test with the kernel's note, the cupboard's note against its view); `src/io/skp-sweep.test.ts` now fails any private file whose note does not count what its view lists.
- **The status line tells regions of two sketches apart** ([#10](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/10)). Moving a region of one sketch with a region of another that has the same number said "Moved region 1 and region 1". Sketches now have names like solids ("sketch 2": its place among the document's sketches, in the order they were made), and a Move, Rotate or Scale message naming regions of more than one sketch names each one's sketch: "Moved region 1 of sketch 1 and region 1 of sketch 2 24 in along +red." Regions of one sketch keep the short form ("region 1 and region 2"). Tests: `src/lib/names.test.ts`, `src/tools/move.test.ts`.
- **A typed angle that turns nothing says so** ([#10](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/10)). In Rotate, a typed `0` showed nothing and left the tool waiting, and `360` said "Nothing to move there." Both, and any other whole number of turns (`-360`, `720`), now say "An angle of 0 turns nothing; type another angle or press Esc.", change nothing, and leave Rotate waiting for another angle, during a turn or just after one, for solids and sketch shapes alike. Tests: `src/tools/rotate.test.ts` (0, 360 and -360 mid-turn, before the start is clicked, after a turn, on a sketch region, then a real angle).
- **Quarter turns are exact** ([#10](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/10)). A sketch stood up with Rotate, Right and `90` faced (0, -1, 6e-17) instead of (0, -1, 0). When the angle is typed, or snapped to a protractor tick, and is a whole number of quarter turns, Rotate builds the turn from exactly 0 and plus or minus 1, tested on the degrees, never on the radians (`turnCosSin` in `src/tools/xform.ts`); a copy divided with `/2` or `/4` keeps it. A drag that lands between ticks turns as before. Tests: `src/tools/rotate.test.ts` (the sines and cosines; a ground normal turned 90, -90, 180 and 270; typed, snapped and free turns as they reach the op; and through the WASM kernel, a ground sketch stood up and a solid turned each way, every normal exactly an axis). No golden or corpus byte changed.
- **Lane 26's loose ends.** `format.md` says a scene's `"grid": null` reads as absent (it always did), and a kernel snapshot test and a `.shz` test prove it. Clicking the scene tab that is already active brings its scene back like any other tab (camera, style, grid, hidden tags, sections; one undo step; the lane-26 note), as it already did on `main`: a new `e2e/grid.spec.ts` test holds it (the grid hidden by hand comes back on the click and goes again with Ctrl+Z), and the guide says so. The end-to-end suite runs six workers locally (`playwright.config.ts`): at Playwright's default, twelve on the development machine, three full runs on an idle machine passed, but beside 16 threads of other work three runs failed 2, 1 and 3 different interactive specs, and at six workers three runs under the same load passed; CI keeps its default, and `PW_WORKERS` overrides it ([DEVELOPMENT.md](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md)). After a reload, `e2e/session.spec.ts` and `e2e/containment.spec.ts` wait for the kernel to load as every spec does on its first load (15 s) instead of the default 5 s, which a loaded machine overran. `DEVELOPMENT.md` says to find a scene tab by its accessible name or `data-scene`, since CSS shows it in capitals.

### Fixed (lane 26, 2026-10-29)

- **A scene brings back the grid it was saved with** ([#6](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/6)). Hide the grid (View > Grid) and save Scene 1, show it and save Scene 2, go to Scene 1 and then Scene 2: the grid used to stay hidden, because Scene 2 was saved with the default style, which is stored as no style, and a scene without a style left the style (the grid with it) as it was. Now going to Scene 2 shows the grid, going to Scene 1 hides it, and the status line says so ("Scene 'Scene 2': grid shown."; nothing is said when the grid does not change). Updating a scene saves the grid that shows then. Going to a scene stays one undo step. A scene from a SketchUp file (SketchUp has no ground grid) or one saved before this fix leaves the grid as it is. The alpha.3 and alpha.4 notes listed this as a known limit.
- Format: a scene carries an optional `grid` (`true` or `false`), the ground grid shown when it is activated, written on every scene Sawhorse saves or updates ([format.md](docs/format.md)); the kernel's `activate_scene` sets the style's grid to it after bringing back the scene's style, and stores a style that becomes the default as none. The grid still lives only in the style; the scene remembers what to set it to (ADR-0008's lane-26 addendum). Older files open as before, and no model hash and no golden changed.
- Tests: kernel tests (`crates/kernel/tests/scene_grid.rs`: two scenes with opposite grids flip it both ways, from no style too, one undo step each; the switch over a disagreeing style; a scene without the switch, and the sheet golden's lane-12 scenes, leave the grid alone; the snapshot accepts the switch absent and present and refuses anything but true or false; `as_scene` leaves the document alone); `.shz` round trips (`crates/shz`: both scenes reopen with the same hash and switches; an older file's scenes leave the grid alone); a drawing of a scene is the same with the grid hidden or shown (`crates/export/tests/drawings.rs`); vitest for what a scene saves, the status line, and, through the WASM kernel, View > Grid and the Styles pane's Ground grid following a scene (`src/components/sceneGrid.test.tsx`); `e2e/grid.spec.ts` runs the issue's steps, saves the `.shz`, opens it and runs them again, and checks that a scene saved with X-ray faces and the grid hidden brings both back. The lane-21 spec that checked the known limit as written now checks the fix. The fuzzer's scenes carry the switch on, off and absent.

### Changed (lane 25, SketchUp curves come in as curves: openskp 1.4.0, 2026-10-28)

- **SketchUp circles and arcs come in as curves.** The `.skp` reader is openskp 1.4.0 (it was 1.3.0), which says which edges make up each curve and gives every arc's and circle's exact centre, radius and angles, from SketchUp 2013 to 2020 files and 2021 and later ones alike. A cylinder, a dowel or a rounded part that is a solid arrives with its circles and arcs as **rims**: click one and the whole circle selects, its centre snaps, and it rides along when you pull the cap; every copy of a component has them. A circle lying loose at the top of the model, or around a flat face there, arrives in a **sketch with its face** (not as an amber mesh), ready to pull into a cylinder. Welded curves come in as one curve of straight segments. The import report counts them: "30 curves came in as curves (6 circles, 24 arcs)" for one of the CC BY test cupboards, which brought none before.
- **What stays plain edges, and says so.** Curves on faces that are not a solid (the amber meshes most hand-made furniture arrives as), a circle drawn on a solid's face, and loose curves inside groups come in as plain edges, counted in the report ("481 curves on faces that are not a solid (or on no face) came in as plain edges: a mesh keeps no curves"). An arc SketchUp cut short on a chord or scaled unevenly is one curve but not a round one. Sawhorse still never guesses a curve from the edges' shape, and checks every circle the file records against its edges: one that does not match comes in as plain edges, named ("Leg: 1 curve's record did not match its edges; it came in as edges"). None of the 35 real files tried had one.
- **Scenes from SketchUp 2013 to 2020 files.** openskp 1.4.0 reads the scenes of files saved before 2021, with their cameras and hidden tags; they come in as a 2021+ file's always did. A **parallel** (orthographic) scene, from either kind of file, now shows the height SketchUp shows: it used to come in zoomed by its eye's distance.
- **Fixes from openskp 1.4.0 you could meet**: a component placed with its scale kept in the placement's scale term (some exporters write them) is placed and sized correctly; it came in 100 times too big and 100 times too far out. A SketchUp 2018 save that would not open ("base probe: anchor resolved to null") opens, and so do openskp's SketchUp 3, 6 and 7 test files. Guide points and guide lines in 2021+ files are counted in the report as not imported, as older files' always were.
- **Still refused**: a real SketchUp 5 save ("legacy .skp parse failed: back-ref to unwalked slot 20617"; it was "texture object is not a dib"): open it in SketchUp 2017 or later and save it again ([#15](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/15)). Nothing that opened before opens worse: the 35 files tried bring the same solids and meshes, file for file ([skp-compat.md](docs/skp-compat.md#openskp-130-and-140-on-real-files)).
- Not changed: groups and components are still told apart by name and how often they are placed; openskp 1.4.0's own group flag disagrees on 59 definitions of the files tried, and using it is [#16](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/16).
- Kernel: an import item may carry curves (`ImportItem.curves`), which become rims of the solid they run along when every segment is an edge of that solid between two different faces (ADR-0013's lane-25 addendum); the import's one curve note counts strokes and rims together, by kind. No model hash and no golden changed.
- Tests: kernel tests for imported rims (`crates/kernel/tests/import_curves.rs`); unit tests for the curve mapping (a circle, an arc both ways round, an open run of a circle, a polyline, an arc cut on a chord, an ellipse, a record that disagrees, a broken chain, a chain where only some edges carry the curve; `src/io/skpCurves.test.ts`) and for the import (the corpus's three curve files, a pulled disc, a cylinder's rims beside a disagreeing record, an unevenly scaled group, the cupboard's 30 curves, the scale term, parallel scenes, the group flag changing nothing; `src/io/skp.test.ts`); `e2e/curves.spec.ts` imports the corpus's circle, arc and welded curve as a circle, an arc and a polyline and selects the circle whole. The `.skp` corpus is regenerated by openskp 1.4.0's writer (166 files changed bytes: it flags a group's definition as a group); every file imports to the same op as before. `src/io/skp-sweep.test.ts` sweeps a private directory of `.skp` files through the import in Node (skipped without one).

### Changed (lane 24, units where the eye lands, 2026-10-22)

- **The units are findable in the first minute** ([#13](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/13): a first look at the browser demo "only gives me inches"). The unit chip beside the Value Control Box now opens a **unit picker** (Millimetres, Centimetres, Metres, Decimal inches, Feet and inches, Fractional inches, each shown with a sample length, the current one checked) instead of cycling through the six on each click; **Edit > Units** lists the same six, with a Choose… that opens the picker; and on an empty document the status line ends with "Units: fractional inches. Click to change.", which is the click. The browser demo (never the desktop app) shows a one-sentence hint above the chip on the first visit, dismissed for good by Got it or by opening the picker. The guide's first page says where units are and spells out the menu path. No kernel change; no model hash changes.
- Tests: vitest for the chip, the picker, the status line and the hint (`src/components/ValueBar.test.tsx`), the App's chip and Edit > Units, the picker's choices; `e2e/units.spec.ts` changes units from the chip on a cold open and sees a typed 300 become 300 mm (and the choice survive a reload); the other specs pick units through one helper.

## [0.1.0-alpha.4] - 2026-10-20

A pre-release, [v0.1.0-alpha.4](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.4); every change since alpha.3: [v0.1.0-alpha.3...v0.1.0-alpha.4](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/compare/v0.1.0-alpha.3...v0.1.0-alpha.4). The release notes: [docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.4.md](docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.4.md).

### Release (the alpha.4 hygiene pass, 2026-10-20)

- Version 0.1.0-alpha.4 in npm, Tauri (MSI version 0.1.0.4), both Cargo workspaces, the `.shz` generator examples and the site's download links; the release notes in `docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.4.md` (curves, #3; sketch geometry that moves, #2; the user-visible fixes; the known limits #6, #7, #8, #9, #11 and #12); the README, the site and `docs/SIGNING.md` point at them.
- `docs/LIMITS.md` says it is current for alpha.4; the user guide no longer says a circle is a chain of separate edges under Moving sketch shapes (lane 22's note, which lane 23 made untrue), and its Offset section points at a curve's radius offset, and it calls Sawhorse an alpha (it said pre-alpha). The README's feature list names curves and sketch shapes that move, and its Not yet list Weld Edges.
- `docs/LIMITS.md` has a Moving sketch geometry section (the selection after a move that splits a shape, [#7](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/7); a Move drag's preview in a sketch of more than about 1,500 edges, [#8](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/8)), and the release notes name #8 with the known limits.
- The release notes, as the hygiene pass's verifier read them: the thanks line credits the SketchUcation report with #2 only, since #3 came up while reproducing it (the report's other request, the grid, was #4 in alpha.3); the Eraser drag across a circle or a polygon is part of curves, not a fix since alpha.3 (it fixed a regression made while curves were being built), so it moved from Fixed into the curve's Erase bullet; #7 reads "the selection is not updated to the pieces it leaves" (in the notes and `docs/LIMITS.md`), as the verifier saw it; and the known limits name #9, a second rectangle that now and then lands on a vertical plane.

### Fixed (lane 23b's second fix pass, found by lane 23b's verifier, 2026-10-20)

- **A section's two halves add up to the solid whatever its faces' bends** (the fuzzer, seed 20281103, plane snapping on; a regression of lane 23b): a 700 m ring wall, two corners of its outer wall nudged up a micrometre, was cut through its centre, and the fuzzer's check read the whole 0.47 m3 above its 265.7 m3. Lane 23b measured each half as fans over its kept face rings, and a fan over a ring round a hole spans the hole: on a face bent within tolerance that is a different surface from the face's triangles. A section's kept volume is now the cone over the solid's own triangles (the ones its volume is measured by), each clipped to the kept side, so the halves make the solid's volume to rounding (9e-14 of it here, 1.5e-14 on lane 23b's fin, which lane 23b held only to 1e-4); the check holds them to it within 1e-6, as before lane 23b, without the bend allowance lane 23b had added.
- **The fuzzer's check of a far-out solid's faces measures about the solid** (seed 20281207, plane snapping off; on lane 23 too): a 3.8 mm sliver of a 700 m circle, pulled 0.5 m down, read 3.17e-6 m3 over its faces' stored planes and 1.84e-6 by its triangles, because the planes were integrated about the world origin, 700 m away, and a narrow face's stored normal may tilt off its corners' by its bend over its width. The planes are now integrated about the solid's own centre, and allowed what a face's bend can move them: its area, plus its perimeter times its distance from that centre over three, both times its bend (a face flat to rounding is allowed nothing beyond the 1e-6 floor). The old allowance, the planarity tolerance times the area, left the second term out; a new property test found it short by a factor of almost six on a ring wall 50 m across and 0.2 mm wide (8.5e-4 m3 apart, 1.5e-4 allowed).
- Tests: both finds as named repros (`2026-10-20-section-of-a-700m-ring-wall-with-two-corners-nudged-a-micrometre`, `2026-10-20-section-of-a-sliver-of-a-700m-circle-snap-off`) with a section test each; a property test of bent ring walls (5 cm to 1 km across, up to 5 km out, corners nudged, cut anywhere, snapping on and off: the halves within 1e-9 of the volume plus rounding and the documented gap of a half the tie rule leaves open); the lane-19 section finds hold each half to its volume worked out without the cut (the flux of the distance beyond the plane through the solid's triangles) and the halves to the whole to 1e-12 (it was 1e-7). The lane-19b verifier's sliver is 8.354e-9 m3 by both measures; it was asserted at 1e-8 to 1.5e-8, the reading through its bent top face's stored plane.

### Fixed (lane 23b, found by lane 23's second verifier, 2026-10-20)

- **An Eraser drag across a circle or a polygon erases it** (a regression of lane 23): a drag whose sweep crossed the shape's face as well as its rim did nothing and said "Can't do that. no curve that one there". The face's erase takes the curve with it (its ring), and the curve's own erase in the same step then found nothing and undid the whole step. The kernel's batch now treats a curve an earlier op of the same batch erased as already erased (as it does an edge already gone), and the app no longer lists a curve that is the whole boundary of a face it erases with it. One undo step brings both back; the status line says "Erased 1 item."
- **Every refusal that names something by its id reads as a sentence**: "no curve that one there" is "that curve no longer exists"; the same for a section plane, a sheet and a sheet's items. A test reads every message the kernel and the browser bridge format with an id and checks each comes out as words.
- **A section across a hair-thin fin keeps two halves above zero** (the fuzzer, seed 20271003, plane snapping on, also on lane 22): a 0.1 mm cylinder with two points of its rim moved 2.2 m up is a fin a few hundredths of a millimetre wide, and a plane at 45 degrees across it kept halves of 1.43e-7 and -4.5e-8 m3 of a 9.7e-8 m3 solid. Each kept face was measured through its stored plane, whose normal a face that narrow does not pin down (its corners sit within tolerance of it but tilt across its width), about the world origin 1.5 m away. The kept volume is now the cone over each kept face's own corners from the solid's centre (ADR-0010 is unchanged: only how the halves are measured; the second fix pass, above, takes the cone over the solid's own triangles instead).
- **A `.shz` with a curve id beyond 2^53 - 1 is refused on open** (the verifier's hostile files): a solid rim's id of u64::MAX overflowed while its rims were settled (a panic in a debug build, 33 of 60,000 mutations; a release build wrapped and then refused). Curve ids and a sketch's curve counter above 2^53 - 1 (the largest JavaScript reads exactly) are refused, and the arithmetic is checked.
- **SketchUp imports no longer say "curves came in as plain edges" on every file**: openskp 1.3.0 gives no curve record, so it cannot tell a file with curves from one without, and the note was on every import with an edge. The fact is in [skp-compat.md](docs/skp-compat.md#curves) and the guide.
- **Scale says what a circle is now**: "Scaled a circle ×2: radius 600 mm." (an arc and a polygon alike), or that it was scaled out of round. The unit chip beside the Value Control Box shows nothing for a factor (Scale) or a count (a circle's segments, a polygon's sides), and a degree sign for an angle (Rotate); it was "mm" next to "2".
- **Move, Rotate and Scale count shapes**: a circle and its face are one shape, so 200 circles window-selected with their faces move as "200 circles", not "201 items" (the same for closed curves of straight segments, "200 curves"). The curve's kind names it ("a circle", "an arc", "a polygon").
- **Scale refuses a lone point of a curve before any grip shows**, with the words Move and Rotate use (ADR-0013 said so; Scale showed no grips and no reason).
- **Sheet viewports and SVG drawings draw a soft edge only where it is the outline**: a pulled circle seen from the front is its two sides, not its 24 seams (a sheet viewport drew 13 upright lines; the SVG export drew none of the sides). Hidden edges are left out of sheet viewports as they are of SVG drawings.
- **Offset of a circle, an arc or a polygon moves its radius**: a 24-segment circle of 200 mm offset 50 mm out is a 250 mm circle (it was 249.57 mm: the polygon offset moved its edges 50 mm, so its apothem). The same centre, kind and segment count, as SketchUp draws it; a polygon inward past its apothem but not its radius is still a polygon. Any other shape offsets as before.
- Filed, not fixed: moving or importing many curves is quadratic ([#11](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/11)); zoomed out, a click mid-segment on a large circle picks a vertex ([#12](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/12)).
- Tests: a kernel test of the batch (a circle and a polygon erased with their faces, one undo; a curve never there still refused), the fin as the named repro `2026-10-20-section-across-a-fin-pulled-up-from-a-tiny-cylinder` and a section test, the curve tampers in `tampered_snapshots_are_refused`, the radius offset (circles, polygons, arcs both ways, past the centre refused), drawing and SVG tests of a soft cylinder from the front; vitest for `eraseOps`, the refusal sentences, the Scale status and chip, shapes counted with 1, 2 and 200 circles, Rotate and Scale refusing a curve's point; an e2e test (an Eraser drag across a circle and diagonally across a polygon, two circles moved, a pulled circle's front elevation on a sheet: two upright lines). `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` has the property and fuzz runs.

### Added (lane 23, curves, 2026-10-19)

- **A circle, an arc or a polygon is one curve** ([#3](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/3)), as in SketchUp. Clicking its rim used to select one of its segments or one of its points, and Move on that bent the circle. Now:
  - **Select**: hovering any segment highlights the whole curve; a click selects it and the status line says what it is, in your units: "Selected a circle (24 segments, radius 300 mm)", "an arc (12 segments, radius 1 1/2 in, 90 degrees)", "a polygon (6 sides, radius 2 in)". Shift and Ctrl add and toggle it whole; a window takes a curve when all of it is inside, a crossing box when it touches any segment; a click exactly on one of its points still selects that point.
  - **Move, Rotate and Scale** carry it as one, alone or with other things selected: a circle moved, turned, mirrored or scaled evenly stays a circle with its new centre and radius; Ctrl copies, each copy a new curve. **Moving one point of a curve on its own is refused**, saying why and naming Edit > Explode Curve (SketchUp bends the curve; this is Sawhorse's one deliberate difference, ADR-0013).
  - **Drawing across a curve splits it**: a line across a circle leaves two arcs on that circle (one line ending on a circle leaves it whole; two split it); a polygon splits into curves of straight segments.
  - **The Eraser** takes a curve whole, on a click or across a drag, one undo step. **Edit > Explode Curve** turns the selected curves back into plain edges, one undo step.
  - **Offset** of a curve, or of a region bounded by one, is a curve of its kind: a circle offsets to a circle about the same centre.
  - **Push/Pull** a circle's, an arc's or a polygon's region and the curve becomes the **rim** of both ends of the solid: a click on a rim selects it whole, and rims follow the solid through moves, copies and components (keyed by face ids, like soft edges). The walls between a circle's or an arc's segments come out **soft and smooth**, so a pulled circle is a smooth cylinder (a polygon's stay hard). Pushed back flat, the circle is a curve in the sketch again.
  - **Centre**: hovering a circle's, an arc's or a polygon's segment with a drawing or moving tool shows its centre (a small blue dot labelled Centre); moving there snaps to it, on sketches and on rims.
  - **Files**: `.shz` keeps curves and rims with their ids (new optional `curves` and `next_curve` in a sketch, `curves` in a B-rep; `docs/format.md`; files without them read and hash as before). A DXF's `CIRCLE` and `ARC` import as curves, counted in the report ("3 curves came in as curves"). A SketchUp file's curves come in as plain edges, with one note saying why: openskp's parser does not say which edges make up a curve, in either file family, and Sawhorse does not guess (`docs/skp-compat.md`, `docs/upstream/openskp-curves.md`).
- Kernel: `crates/kernel/src/curve.rs` (the curve entity, a solid's rims, their checks); a sketch settles its curves after every edit (split segments taken in, splits at junctions, polyline pieces for what lost an edge, an edge in one curve at most); `Op::Draw { curve }`, `Op::EraseCurve`, `Op::ExplodeCurve`, `Op::TransformSketch { curves }`; outcomes `Drawn { curve }`, `SketchMoved { curves }`, `SketchCopied { curves }`, `CurveExploded`; typed refusals `NoSuchCurve`, `NotACurve`, `CurvePoint`; the validator checks every curve's chain, kind and circle; the view mesh names each edge's curve (`edge_curve`, `curves`) and picks carry it; inference names the centre of the curve under the cursor. [ADR-0013](docs/adr/0013-curves.md).
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/curves.rs` (every rule, and the three finds below); `crates/kernel/tests/curve_props.rs`, a hostile property test (circles and polygons of 3 to 96 segments at radii from 0.1 mm to 9 km, arcs of 0.001 to 359.999 degrees, lines ending on a curve's point or a segment's middle, crossing a segment, crossing two curves, along a segment, erases, explodes, offsets, moves, turns, tilts, scales, mirrors, lifts, copies and arrays of whole curves and lone points, pushes; after every op the validator, `check_model`, exact undo and redo, the snapshot through JSON; then `check_sequence`); the fuzzer's lane-23 intents; `crates/inference/tests/perf.rs` (the inference hot path on 9,600-face models); vitest (`src/lib/curves.test.ts`: select, erase, move, scale, offset, the window and crossing boxes, the status line); `e2e/curves.spec.ts` (a circle drawn, clicked, moved by a typed distance, scaled from a grip, copied, split by a line, one arc erased, the other exploded; a polygon pushed and its top rim clicked; the Centre hovered and snapped to; every step undone and redone; saved and opened; a DXF's curves and the corpus's SketchUp circle imported). The corpus gains three SketchUp curve files (203 in all).

### Fixed (lane 23 fix pass, found by the lane's verifier, 2026-10-19)

- **A thin band round a huge circle draws** (the curve property test at 100,000 cases, seed 7303): a 9 km circle offset 0.07 mm inwards leaves a 3.84 m2 band between two rings of 247 million m2. The triangulator's area check took the band's area as the outer ring's less the hole's, each worked out only to its own size, so it read 2.3e-8 m2 off (5.9e-9 of the band, over the 1e-9 allowed) and the view could not be built, although earcut's triangles were exactly right. The net area is now one compensated sum over every ring at once, so it is right to the polygon's own size. Older than curves: any offset that thin, that far out, failed the same way.
- **Holes that touch at a point triangulate** (the fuzzer, seed 20267102, plane snapping on): a 700 m circle drawn round two slivers that share a corner could not be shown. earcut drops the shared point's second copy and covers one sliver, and its fallback bridged the holes one at a time, so the outline passed through the point twice the wrong way round and came out with a triangle of -80 m2. The fallback now joins rings that touch at a point into one ring first, decides every turn with exact arithmetic, checks every bridge it cuts, and never clips a triangle that another pass through one of its corners opens into; a point that is only a second copy of a used one is no longer forced into the mesh by splitting a wrong triangle. Also older than curves. No golden changed.
- **Create Group from Slice reaches caps far along a tilted plane** (the fix pass's own fuzz run, seed 20269807, plane snapping off): a sketch holds points up to 10 km from its origin, and the slice's sketch took the section's point as its origin; along a tilted plane through the world's origin a cap can lie 10.6 km from it with every point in the model's bounds, and the slice failed with "a coordinate is beyond 10 km" although the cap is drawable. The slice now moves its sketch's origin along the plane to where the caps fit, and caps more than 20 km apart along the plane, which no sketch can hold, are refused in their own words ("more than 20 km across"). Older than curves (the same sequence fails the same way before this fix pass, with the circle drawn as plain edges too); this run's seeds were the first to reach it.
- Tests: both shapes as triangulator tests and two property tests (thin bands round polygons up to 10 km across and 5 km out; two to four holes round a shared point, with or without a hole on an outer corner); the two fuzz sequences as named repros; the property find in `curves.rs`; two section tests (a slice 10.6 km along a tilted plane drawn, one 27 km across refused); and a snapshot test that a saved curve or rim broken thirteen ways (points out of order or missing, radius, centre, start or sweep wrong, an arc marked closed, two curves on one chain, a stale counter, a missing point, id 0, a rim on edges that are not there, two rims with one id) is refused on open.

### Fixed (lane 23, found by the curve property tests and the fuzzer, 2026-10-19)

- **Long slivers far out draw again**: the triangulator's area check measured triangles with the robust predicate's values, which are only sign-exact, and summed a sliver's area as a fan of huge triangles either way; a 0.14 m2 sliver between a 9 km circle and its copy turned 1e-7 degrees read 1.2e-8 off and its correct triangles were refused, so the view could not be built. Areas are now worked out to their own size (exactly split products, a compensated sum). No golden changed.
- **A hair-thin sliver's solid is not called self-intersecting**: the edge leaving a tip 1.3e-8 rad wide, 4 km out, was tested from a rounded point that fell on the wrong side of the other side's wall. An edge from a point of a face's plane meets it nowhere else unless it lies in it, and is now tested only then.
- **An extruded sliver's faces lie in their own planes**: a face's plane was fitted to its points, and a sliver's Newell normal, 9 km out on a tilted plane, was 1e-8 rad off, so its own points read 5.6e-5 m off it. Extruding now gives every face the plane it is built in.
- **An erase no longer heals an edge onto a point** (found by the fuzzer): healing a vertex left between two near-collinear edges joined their far ends even when the new edge passed within the merge distance of another point, and the sketch was refused as invalid; the two edges now stay in that case.
- **A curve bent by a repair becomes a polyline** (found by the fuzzer): a tiny circle copied a hair over itself could leave a piece that ran out along the copy's edges and back but kept its circle, and the copy was refused. A piece that no longer fits its circle is now a polyline.
- **Tiny solids far from the origin measure their volume** (found by the fuzzer, also on lane 22's region moves): a solid's volume was summed about the world origin, so a 0.1 mm cylinder 6.6 km out, turned, measured 244 times its volume; volumes are now summed about the solid's own corner. The same fix for the section check's measure of how closed each half is (a 2.5e-5 m2 cap 9 km out read as open).

### Added (lane 22, moving sketch geometry, 2026-10-19)

- **Move, Rotate and Scale move sketch geometry** ([#2](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/2)): a circle, rectangle, polygon, arc or line not yet pushed into a solid moves like a solid, with the same inference, arrow-key locks, typed distances and angles, Esc and one undo step. "Can't do that. Sketch edges don't move yet." is gone. Selected (a region, edges, points) or clicked with nothing selected; mixed with solids, groups, components or section planes, everything moves together as one undo step.
  - **A point or an edge** moves within its shape's plane and the edges beside it follow: one corner of a rectangle dragged sideways stretches its two edges and nothing else. A move pointing out of the plane is projected into it; one pointing straight out of it is refused, typed ("select the whole shape to lift it off"). An edge two regions share moves both.
  - **A whole shape** moves anywhere: off its plane it goes into the sketch on the plane it lands on (or a new one), ready to push/pull, and a neighbour it shared an edge with keeps that edge.
  - **What it lands on** joins it the way drawing there does (the same insertion code): coincident edges weld into one, crossings and touches split, an edge shrunk to nothing goes and its ends merge, and a move that would twist a region into a bow-tie splits it into two regions. Regions keep their ids when they only change shape.
  - **Rotate** puts the protractor on the shape's own plane (an arrow key or Shift chooses another; a whole shape then turns onto a new plane). A turn by 0 or 360 degrees moves nothing and makes no undo step.
  - **Ctrl copies**, and `x3` / `/4` make arrays, as for solids; a copy is independent geometry and welds onto what it touches. Scale scales sketch geometry too.
  - What moved stays selected where it went; Push/Pull's next target follows a region lifted onto another plane.
- Kernel: `Op::TransformSketch { sketch, points, edges, regions, xform, copy, count }` and `crates/kernel/src/sketch_move.rs`, on `Sketch::with_moved_vertices` (redraws every edge touching a moving vertex with the insertion rules, then `repair` and region detection), `with_segments` and `without_edges`; outcomes `SketchMoved { sketch, regions }` and `SketchCopied { regions }`; typed refusals `NoSuchSketchPoint`, `NoSuchSketchEdge`, `LeavesSketchPlane` (with `NothingToMove`, `NothingSelected`, `BadCount`, `OutOfBounds`, `OutsideContext`). [ADR-0012](docs/adr/0012-moving-sketch-geometry.md). The plugin API's batches can hold the op (`docs/PLUGINS.md`).
- Tests: `crates/kernel/tests/sketch_move.rs` (27 tests: one per rule, translate, typed circle, corner, projection, shared edge, weld, split, merge, bow-tie, turns by 0 / 45 / 180 / 360 / 1e-4 degrees, lifts and returns, a lifted neighbour, an island, copies and arrays in and off the plane, push volumes, the snapshot and log JSON, refusals, groups, a mixed selection, scales and mirrors, and the property tests' finds replayed and pushed region by region); `crates/kernel/tests/sketch_move_props.rs`, a hostile property test (landings on vertices and on or within the merge distance of edges, turns by 0, 180, 360, -360, 1e-7 and 179.99999 degrees about the normal, axes in the plane and a skew axis, moves under and over the planarity tolerance off the plane, scales including mirrors and a thousandth, copies and arrays, on the ground and on tilted planes; after each op the model validates, undo and redo land exactly, every region pushes into a solid of its area times the distance, the snapshot opens to the same hash, rigid maps of a whole sketch keep it congruent, a whole sketch lifted leaves nothing behind; then the whole sequence through `check_sequence`); the op-sequence fuzzer's `SketchMove` intents; a golden (`sketch_move.json`, WASM equal to native); `.shz` round trip (`moved_sketches_round_trip`); vitest for the tools' sketch subjects, ops, previews, selection and push target; `e2e/sketch-move.spec.ts` (a circle moved by a typed distance and by inference, turned 45 degrees, copied with Ctrl, every step undone; a rectangle's corner moved, the rectangle flat with its two edges following, pushed to exactly 336 cubic inches; a rectangle lifted 6 in and pushed there). On the final code the property test ran 300,000 cases (three fresh seeds at 100,000) and the fuzzer 160,000 sequences (100,000 with plane snapping, 50,000 without, CI's seed), all clean; the earlier rounds' finds are below. The runs are in `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`.

### Fixed (lane 22, found by the sketch-move property tests, 2026-10-19)

- **A sketch region a rounding error from the merge distance pushes into a valid solid.** The sketch merged points at 0.01 mm in its own frame and the solid measured them again in world coordinates against the same 0.01 mm: an edge 1.6e-17 m over it in the sketch was zero-length to the solid, and a corner a rounding error clear of another edge was a vertex on a face; the push was refused as a kernel bug (a debug build stopped). The sketch now merges at 0.01 mm plus the 1 nm noise floor (`SKETCH_MERGE`), so what it keeps clears the solid's tests; the section slice welds to match. Seeds 2026102201..2026102203, kept in `crates/kernel/tests/sketch_move_finds/`.
- **Two nearly parallel crossing edges no longer put a vertex at infinity.** Their crossing was worked out by dividing by a cross product that rounded to zero; it now comes from the robust orientations of one edge's ends against the other, always finite and on the edge, and the sketch check refuses a vertex that is not finite or is beyond 10 km (before, only opening the saved file noticed). Seed 2026102213.

- **Regions far from the origin, and long slivers, triangulate.** Moved and copied sketch shapes reach places drawing seldom did: a region 6 km out, slivers 10 km long between a 10 km face offset and its copy turned 1e-7 degrees. The triangulator's check summed products of absolute coordinates and lost more to rounding than it allowed (1e-9 of the area), refusing correct triangles, so the view could not be built; a section cap 7 km out measured its area 3e-7 short. The check now sums robust triangle areas, and ring areas are summed about the ring's first point, so rounding follows the shape's size, not its distance from the origin. A polygon is "near zero area" against its own extent now, not against its largest coordinate: a 17 micrometre region 5 km out (3e-10 m2) had been refused as having none. Fuzzer seeds 20268102 to 20268107, 20268201, 20268207, 20268208, 20268405 and CI's 20261011; six kept in `crates/kernel/tests/repro/2026-10-19-*`. No golden changed.

### Known (lane 22)

- A circle, arc or polygon is still a chain of separate edges: a click on its rim selects one segment or point, and moving that bends the curve within its plane. Select the shape (click inside it) to move it whole. One curve per circle is [#3](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/3) (done in lane 23, above).

## [0.1.0-alpha.3] - 2026-10-19

A pre-release, [v0.1.0-alpha.3](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.3); every change since alpha.2: [v0.1.0-alpha.2...v0.1.0-alpha.3](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/compare/v0.1.0-alpha.2...v0.1.0-alpha.3). The release notes: [docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.3.md](docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.3.md).

### Release (the alpha.3 hygiene pass, 2026-10-19)

- Version 0.1.0-alpha.3 in npm, Tauri (MSI version 0.1.0.3), both Cargo workspaces and the site's download links; the release notes in `docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.3.md`.
- **`SHA256SUMS.txt` on every release**: `release.yml` gains a job that, after all three platform builds, downloads every file of the draft release, writes the SHA-256 of each (`scripts/release-checksums.sh`: one `<sha256>  <name>` line per file, checked with `sha256sum --strict -c`, failing on a missing installer kind or a file not listed exactly once), uploads it to the same draft, and checks the uploaded copy names every other file.
- **`docs/SIGNING.md`**, the code signing policy: Windows installers to be signed by SignPath Foundation once approved, on artifacts GitHub Actions built from a tagged commit; the roles; what the app sends (nothing, unless you set up a render service or grant a plugin the network); uninstalling. Linked from the README and the site.
- The Pages workflow passes `--allow-missing-release` again, for the window between the version bump reaching `main` and the release being published.
- `docs/LIMITS.md` says in one line that the alpha.2 section-plane caveat is fixed, pointing at the lane-19c property tests, and gains the cut's measured time; the user guide says a face lying exactly on a plane counts as kept; `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` has a release checklist.

### Fixed (the alpha.3 fix pass, 2026-10-19)

- **A value typed right after a tool key goes to that tool.** "P" pressed while a rectangle's edit was still with the kernel waits for it (Push/Pull must see the rectangle), so the switch runs later, outside the key's event, and React renders it at default priority. A key's own render ("6", at input priority) could run first, from the old state, and `activeRef` was reassigned from that render's tool id: the 6 and its Enter went to Rect ("Rect takes two sizes separated by a comma"). The keys and the Value Control Box now follow the id `selectTool` sets synchronously (`toolIdRef`), never a render's state. It turned the alpha.3 CI run red (`move.spec` and `containment.spec`, flaky on a loaded runner); `e2e/tool-switch.spec.ts` forces the order (the kernel's reply held, then the switch, "6" and Enter in one task) and failed the same way before the fix.
- **Clicking a submenu after pointing at it no longer closes it.** A submenu entry opened on hover and toggled on click, so point-then-click shut it; whether a fast click did depended on whether React had rendered the hover yet, and `plugins-ui.spec` lost Plugins > Everything > Ask Me twice on the same CI run. A click now opens it (Esc, a click outside or another entry closes it); `src/components/TopBar.test.tsx` and the spec point first, then click.
- **The alpha.3 notes said a default-style scene brings the grid back if you update it while the grid shows.** It does not (the update saves the default style again, which a scene does not apply). The notes now say so; `e2e/grid.spec.ts` checks every scene-and-grid sentence of the notes and of lane 21 below. The notes link to `docs/` with absolute URLs, since a GitHub release body resolves relative ones to dead pages (the alpha.2 release has two); `src/releaseNotes.test.ts` refuses a relative link in the current version's notes. `SHA256SUMS.txt` is described as covering every other file on the release, the macOS `.app.tar.gz` included.

### Fixed (lane 21, orbit about the point under the cursor, 2026-10-16)

- **Orbit turns about the point under the cursor, and that point stays put** ([#5](https://github.com/sawhorse3d/sawhorse/issues/5)). A middle-drag turned the view about the look-at point, which was wherever the last zoom or zoom extents had left it, so once zoomed in the model swung away while orbiting: the point under the cursor climbed or dropped ("moving on both axes"), and near the poles the polar clamp stopped the drag ("locks onto a face"). Now, as in SketchUp, pressing the middle button picks the **pivot**: a corner within 6 px or a point of an edge within 4 px of the cursor, else the face under it, else the ground under it, else the look-at point. The model turns rigidly about it (across about world +Z, up and down about the level right vector), so the pivot keeps its pixel for the whole drag: in the end-to-end test a corner zoomed in on moves 0.000007 px over 200 px across and 200 px down. A small crosshair marks the pivot while the button is held. The tilt stops 0.5 degrees short of straight down or up and the turn across keeps working there, with no lock; a drag back responds at once. Right-drag and Shift+middle-drag pan with the point under the cursor at the press, at its own depth (Shift pressed or let go mid-drag switches between orbit and pan); the wheel zooms toward the cursor with the same steps as before. After every move the look-at point is put back on the line of sight at the depth of the point the gesture held, so zoom extents, the standard views, the projection switch, the grid density, scenes and the Sheets' "use 3D view" read it as before.
- **The view's own camera controller** (`src/viewport/controls.ts`) replaces three.js OrbitControls, which could not hold a picked pivot on screen; its maths is in `src/viewport/navmath.ts` with unit tests (the pivot's projection identical before and after 2,000 random orbits of any angle, to 6e-15 in normalised device coordinates against a 1e-9 bound, in both projections; azimuth about +Z; no flip over the pole; pan and zoom holding the point under the cursor; the projections matching three.js's cameras).
